## rev0478 — frontier posture: the archive now needs stage gates more than another grand program note
This revision is a **stage-gate / proof-budget** deepening.
It does **not** promote a new frontier and it does **not** change the broad ladder.

Current sharpener:
- the repo now has an explicit layer for deciding **when a worthy program is allowed to widen**;
- future “practicality” passes should usually say **current stage**, **next gate**, **earned proof budget**, and **premature promises to refuse** rather than restating rank or charter shape;
- the most common next failure mode is no longer “we forgot a worthy seam” but **we widened a worthy seam faster than its proof**.

Interpretation:
- **Evidence Spine / Build-State Evidence** remains the clearest first build because it has the strongest kernel-to-local-decision progression;
- **Feedback / Debug Acceptance Commons** remains the clearest second build because its natural problem is cross-tuple widening with explicit partial states;
- **Navigation / Defaults / Claims Commons** remains important but should widen the slowest because recommendation claims are easy to say and hard to renew honestly;
- **Package Intake + Release Boundary Review** remains the clearest urgent bridge and should stay incident-real before seeking broader authority;
- **Safety-Critical + Institutional Readiness Commons** remains the clearest stewarded long-horizon seam and should keep the strictest gate discipline; and
- no new frontier should be promoted unless it survives this stricter stage-gate reading first.

## rev0471 — decision-rights / consensus-drag addendum
This revision does **not** change the broad frontier ladder.
It adds a new comparative layer for questions like:
- which worthy Rust contributions can create **first real value under one team's own control**,
- which ones need **narrow upstream permission** before they become much better,
- which ones depend on **service or operator opt-in**,
- which ones are blocked on **cross-tool tuple agreement**,
- and which ones are inherently **consortium / standards / governance-grade** seams.

Read first:
- `design/epic-contribution-decision-rights-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/epic-contribution-support-bundle-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/epic-contribution-boundary-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/epic-contribution-proof-burden-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/epic-contribution-renewal-burden-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/epic-contribution-distortion-risk-map-2026Q1.md`
- `design/portfolio-execution-sequencing-2026Q1.md`

Interpretation:
- the broad ladder is unchanged;
- the missing new layer is **decision rights / consensus drag**, not another priority rewrite;
- **Build-State Evidence** remains the strongest broad first build and also the clearest **local-first** portfolio answer;
- **Semantic Context / Tooling Contract / Shared Spine** remain the strongest **local-first hidden multipliers**;
- **Package Intake Gateway** remains the clearest **operator / route-owner** bridge;
- **Feedback Loop / Debuggability Acceptance** remains the clearest **cross-tool tuple-consensus** seam;
- **Safety-Critical Readiness Commons** remains the clearest **consortium-grade** seam; and
- future frontier refreshes should now ask explicitly whether a seam is blocked by **capability**, **proof**, **support**, or **who has to agree** before reordering it.

## Latest addition (rev0450)
This revision is a **deepening** pass, not a new frontier promotion.

The repo's broad execution answer was already stronger:
- **Build-State Evidence** remained the strongest single-project bet;
- **Feedback Loop / Debuggability Acceptance** remained the clearest under-ranked missing middle;
- and several repeated Tier-A seams had already gained execution blueprints.

What the archive still lacked was a direct answer to:
**what should the repeatedly promoted Canonical Learning seam actually ship before it turns into another docs portal, one smart-docs score, or an assistant-owned knowledge blob?**

The new answer is now explicit in:
- `design/canonical-learning-execution-blueprint-2026Q1.md`

Why this deepening was merited:
- official survey data still says docs remain the canonical reference while editor/LLM mediation rises;
- the Rust vision work now explicitly argues that crates need better diagnostics and guidance;
- docs.rs already exposes machine-declared docs posture and hosts rustdoc JSON;
- rustdoc and mdBook already validate executable teaching surfaces;
- and compiler/Cargo diagnostic outputs plus stable diagnostic attributes keep making compile-time guidance more reusable.

Strategy:
- keep the broad ladder intact;
- keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest one-project answer;
- keep **Feedback Loop / Debuggability Acceptance** as the clearest under-ranked day-to-day missing middle;
- and make **Canonical Learning execution blueprint** the canonical answer for “what should Rust actually build around canonical docs/guides/examples/compile-guidance, consumer-import discipline, and bounded derived overlays?” rather than leaving that work split across rustdoc, docs.rs, mdBook, diagnostics, editors, assistants, and archive summaries.

## Current frontier note (rev0415)
### Promote the native-edge contract as the next cross-language / native-adoption / external-build-handoff move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **boundary truth, provider/link truth, host-vs-target/toolchain context, external-build handoff, and later consumer conclusions are not the same seam**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **FFI Boundary Kit** as the explicit **Rust↔native boundary** seam;
- keep **Native Dependency Kit** as the explicit **provider / lock / link-plan** seam;
- keep **Build Interop**, **Cross Toolchain**, and **Toolchain Productization** as adjacent context/import seams;
- keep **Polyglot Productization Stack** as the broader shipped mixed-language product seam above native-edge facts;
- but promote **Native Edge Stack**, read now as a **Native Edge Contract**, as the clearest next **cross-language / native-adoption / external-build-handoff** move for reviewed interop subjects, imported boundary/provider artifacts, host-vs-target context, foreign-build handoff, and bounded support/release/audit/guidance consumers.

Why this is the right move now:
- the accepted 2025H1 interop goal explicitly targets Rust adoption in projects that must use large, rich C++ APIs;
- the 2025H2 interop problem map says there are billions of lines of C++ and that rewrites are often infeasible;
- the July 2025 goals update says Rust should evolve toward a first-class C++ interop story while also stressing that needs differ across groups;
- the January 2026 program-management update makes **Cross-language interop** an explicit Rust application area that should help focus industry funding;
- the January 2026 safety-critical writeup says teams will carry Rust↔C/C++ boundaries for years and need those interfaces to be correct, auditable, and in sync;
- Rust 2024 `unsafe extern` blocks and newer ABI hardening reinforce that FFI signatures and ABI details remain explicit review surfaces;
- the ecosystem’s tool families are serious but non-equivalent: CXX is a safe-common regime with generated paired code, `autocxx` targets large existing C++ codebases, `bindgen` still carries `libclang` coupling, and Corrosion still leaves major binding-generation choices and integrations to users.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest broad/buildable epic contribution overall;
2. keep **Adoption Navigation Contract** as the strongest explicit **anti-tacit-knowledge / project-scoped recommendation** frontier overall;
3. keep **Reviewable Lane Defaults + renewal receipts** as the immediate execution seam beneath that frontier;
4. keep **Debuggability** high as the rising cross-cutting frontier;
5. keep **Rust inner-loop contract / Feedback Loop Stack** as the clearest next **bundle-shaping** move beneath the build/debug band;
6. keep **Workspace Environment Stack / Workspace Environment Contract** as the clearest next **environment / handoff-shaping** move;
7. keep **Package Intake Gateway** as the clearest newly sharpened **supply-chain/intake-shaping** seam;
8. keep **Toolchain Productization Stack / Toolchain Productization Contract** as the clearest next **toolchain-variant / sysroot / runtime-analysis-shaping** move;
9. keep **Migration Truth Stack / Migration Truth Contract** as the clearest next **change-program / upgrade-shaping** move;
10. keep **Public API Kit / Public API Contract** as the clearest next **release-boundary / semver-evidence-shaping** move;
11. promote **Native Edge Stack / Native Edge Contract** as the clearest next **cross-language / native-adoption / external-build-handoff** move;
12. keep **Publisher & Source Identity Contract** as the clearest next **publication / authority / route-shaping** move;
13. keep **Distribution Contract** as the clearest next **delivery / acquisition / ownership-shaping** move;
14. keep **Maintenance Reality Contract** as the clearest next **stewardship / continuity / support-routing** move;
15. keep **Defect Escalation Stack / Defect Escalation Contract** as the clearest next **upstream-routing / repro-to-regression-handoff** move;
16. keep **Benchmark Evidence Kit / Benchmark Evidence Contract** as the clearest next **performance-claim / compare-shaping** move;
17. keep **Compatibility Claims** as the clearest next **support/claim-shaping** move;
18. keep **Proc-Macro Exit Stack** as the clearest next **compile-time/transition-shaping** move;
19. keep **Cargo artifact contract** as the clearest next **final-output / handoff-shaping** move;
20. keep **Cargo build interop contract** as the clearest next **workspace-graph / plan / event / adapter-shaping** move;
21. keep **Edit Workflow Kit / Reviewable Edit Contract** as the clearest next **mutation / review / handoff-shaping** move;
22. keep **Semantic Context Kit / Semantic Context Contract** as the clearest next **context / authority / consumer-shaping** move;
23. keep **Harness Protocol Kit / Harness Protocol Contract** as the clearest next **testing / runner / adapter-shaping** move;
24. keep **Safety-Critical Evidence Stack / Safety-Critical Assurance Contract** as the clearest next **high-assurance / evidence-shaping** move;
25. keep **Observability Productization Stack / Observability Contract** as the clearest next **runtime telemetry / support / handoff-shaping** move;
26. keep **browser + Node dual-target Wasm package overlay** as the clearest next **public-lane** candidate if the archive widens maintained defaults again.

Preferred shape:
- native edge contract = **subject truth + boundary truth + provider/link truth + host/target/toolchain context + external-build handoff truth + consumer-handoff truth**;
- keep it explicitly adjacent to, but distinct from, **Public API**, **Toolchain Productization**, **Compatibility Claims**, and **Polyglot Productization**;
- do not flatten one generated header, one `bindgen` run, one CXX bridge module, one provider lock, one CMake import, or one release/support summary into the whole story; and
- do not confuse “newly promoted native-edge frontier” with “new overall #1 missing Rust contribution.”


## Current frontier note (rev0414)
### Promote the public API contract as the next release-boundary / semver-evidence-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **public-boundary subject truth, exposure truth, structural-diff truth, witness/type-proof truth, bounded verification truth, and later consumer claims are not the same seam**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Semantic Context Contract** as the explicit **analysis authority / cross-crate context** seam;
- keep **Migration Truth Contract** as the explicit **source→destination change-program** seam;
- keep **Compatibility Claims** as the explicit **support-envelope** seam;
- keep **Publisher & Source Identity Contract** and **Distribution Contract** as the explicit **who-published / how-consumed** seams;
- keep **Public API Kit** and **public-api pilot program** as the lower-layer substrate;
- but promote **Public API Kit**, read now as a **Public API Contract**, as the clearest next **release-boundary / semver-evidence-shaping** move for public-boundary subject ids, declared-vs-inferred exposure drift, structural diffs, witness-backed type compatibility evidence, bounded MSRV/matrix verification, and consumer handoffs.

Why this is the right move now:
- the 2026 flagships keep **public/private dependencies** and **SBOM support** in the top supply-chain band;
- the accepted public/private-dependencies goal says the feature should help users catch accidental exposure of implementation details and help tooling identify what constitutes an API;
- Cargo's unstable-features docs still keep `public-dependency` and `sbom` behind explicit feature gates;
- Cargo's changelog shows the `-Zpublic-dependency` lane is still evolving in `cargo add`, `cargo tree`, and manifest diagnostics;
- the 2025H2 cargo-semver-checks goal says accidental SemVer violations are common, Cargo wants eventual publish-time integration, and the hard blockers remain cross-crate items plus type-sensitive checking;
- that same goal says target-package-only rustdoc JSON is still a major source of false positives and negatives for exposed foreign items;
- the GSoC witness-generation prototype is now an explicit roadmap input for type-related SemVer breakage checks;
- docs.rs now hosts rustdoc JSON directly, but its own docs say consumers must check `format_version` and coverage can lag older releases;
- Cargo's SemVer guide still frames many cases as guideline classes rather than one mechanically final answer; and
- `cargo-public-api` / `public_api` prove the lane is real but still rely on rustdoc JSON and nightly generation, so the ecosystem still lacks a more portable release-boundary handoff.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest broad/buildable epic contribution overall;
2. keep **Adoption Navigation Contract** as the strongest explicit **anti-tacit-knowledge / project-scoped recommendation** frontier overall;
3. keep **Reviewable Lane Defaults + renewal receipts** as the immediate execution seam beneath that frontier;
4. keep **Debuggability** high as the rising cross-cutting frontier;
5. keep **Rust inner-loop contract / Feedback Loop Stack** as the clearest next **bundle-shaping** move beneath the build/debug band;
6. keep **Workspace Environment Stack / Workspace Environment Contract** as the clearest next **environment / handoff-shaping** move;
7. keep **Package Intake Gateway** as the clearest newly sharpened **supply-chain/intake-shaping** seam;
8. keep **Toolchain Productization Stack / Toolchain Productization Contract** as the clearest next **toolchain-variant / sysroot / runtime-analysis-shaping** move;
9. keep **Migration Truth Stack / Migration Truth Contract** as the clearest next **change-program / upgrade-shaping** move;
10. promote **Public API Kit / Public API Contract** as the clearest next **release-boundary / semver-evidence-shaping** move;
11. keep **Publisher & Source Identity Contract** as the clearest next **publication / authority / route-shaping** move;
12. keep **Distribution Contract** as the clearest next **delivery / acquisition / ownership-shaping** move;
13. keep **Maintenance Reality Contract** as the clearest next **stewardship / continuity / support-routing** move;
14. keep **Defect Escalation Stack / Defect Escalation Contract** as the clearest next **upstream-routing / repro-to-regression-handoff** move;
15. keep **Benchmark Evidence Kit / Benchmark Evidence Contract** as the clearest next **performance-claim / compare-shaping** move;
16. keep **Compatibility Claims** as the clearest next **support/claim-shaping** move;
17. keep **Proc-Macro Exit Stack** as the clearest next **compile-time/transition-shaping** move;
18. keep **Cargo artifact contract** as the clearest next **final-output / handoff-shaping** move;
19. keep **Cargo build interop contract** as the clearest next **workspace-graph / plan / event / adapter-shaping** move;
20. keep **Edit Workflow Kit / Reviewable Edit Contract** as the clearest next **mutation / review / handoff-shaping** move;
21. keep **Semantic Context Kit / Semantic Context Contract** as the clearest next **context / authority / consumer-shaping** move;
22. keep **Harness Protocol Kit / Harness Protocol Contract** as the clearest next **testing / runner / adapter-shaping** move;
23. keep **Safety-Critical Evidence Stack / Safety-Critical Assurance Contract** as the clearest next **high-assurance / evidence-shaping** move;
24. keep **Observability Productization Stack / Observability Contract** as the clearest next **runtime telemetry / support / handoff-shaping** move;
25. keep **browser + Node dual-target Wasm package overlay** as the clearest next **public-lane** candidate if the archive widens maintained defaults again.

Preferred shape:
- public API contract = **public-boundary subject truth + exposure truth + structural-diff truth + witness/type-proof truth + bounded verification truth + consumer-handoff truth**;
- keep it explicitly adjacent to, but distinct from, **Semantic Context**, **Migration Truth**, **Compatibility Claims**, **Publisher & Source Identity**, and **Distribution**;
- do not flatten one rustdoc JSON export, one `cargo-public-api` diff, one semver lint run, one witness compile, one MSRV matrix run, or one release note into the whole API story; and
- do not confuse “newly promoted public-API frontier” with “new overall #1 missing Rust contribution.”


## Current frontier note (rev0413)
### Promote the migration truth contract as the next change-program / upgrade-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **source-state truth, destination-intent truth, mechanical-edit truth, compatibility/support/docs/downstream import truth, run/outcome truth, and later consumer claims are not the same seam**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Reviewable Edit Contract** as the explicit **candidate / selection / application / verification** seam;
- keep **Compatibility Claims**, **Semantic Context**, and **Support Envelope** as the explicit **consequence / authority / support** imports;
- keep **Distribution Contract** and **Update Continuity** as the explicit **delivered-artifact / installed-lifecycle** seams;
- keep **Maintenance Reality** and **Defect Escalation** as neighboring stewardship and routing seams;
- but promote **Migration Truth Stack**, read now as a **Migration Truth Contract**, as the clearest next **change-program / upgrade-shaping** move for source state, destination intent, edit provenance, compatibility imports, waivers, outcome truth, and downstream handoffs.

Why this is the right move now:
- the Edition Guide still frames migration as staged and configuration-sensitive rather than one command;
- Cargo's `cargo fix` docs still require multiple runs across features and targets for many edition-sensitive projects;
- Rust 1.85 says `cargo fix` results are conservative and not recommendations;
- Cargo's 1.90 work says the current fix architecture is slow, hard to make selective, and awkward to make interactive;
- Cargo's `rust-version` docs say mixed-policy workspaces complicate verification because dependencies are unified;
- the 2026 flagships still include public/private dependencies and SBOM support, which keeps upgrade consequences on the publish path;
- cargo-semver-checks still needs cross-crate and precise type-sensitive compatibility information before deeper Cargo integration;
- docs.rs now hosts rustdoc JSON directly; and
- the 2025 survey still says docs remain canonical while editor/LLM-mediated learning rises, which increases the value of durable migration artifacts.

### Promote the toolchain productization contract as the next toolchain-variant / sysroot / runtime-analysis-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **provisioned toolchain identity, stdlib/sysroot profile identity, activation/reuse truth, instrumented-runtime truth, and later release/safety/support claims are not the same seam**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Workspace Environment Contract** as the explicit **setup / realization / observation / handoff** seam;

- keep **Compatibility Claims** and **Safety-Critical Assurance** as downstream claim/evidence consumers;

- but promote **Toolchain Productization Stack**, read now as a **Toolchain Productization Contract**, as the clearest next **toolchain-variant / sysroot / runtime-analysis-shaping** move for provisioning facts, stdlib/sysroot profile families, activation/reuse posture, runtime-analysis lanes, and consumer handoffs.

Why this is the right move now:
- the 2026 flagships keep **Building blocks** centered on rebuild-std work and larger-build-system integration;
- the accepted `build-std` goal is about a stabilizable MVP rather than indefinite experimentation;
- Cargo's current unstable `-Z build-std` flow still requires `rust-src`, nightly Cargo, nightly rustc, and passing the flag to all invocations;
- rustup still splits toolchains, targets, profiles, components, and directory-sensitive overrides into separate surfaces;
- rustup cross-compilation still says target stdlib install is not enough because linkers and SDKs are often required;
- custom-target docs still require compiler pinning because target JSON remains unstable;
- sanitizer docs and sanitizer-stabilization work both reinforce the need for instrumented standard-library families; and
- Rust for Linux plus the CPython conversations prove the pressure is no longer niche.

The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.

- the 2025H2 goals explicitly say `cargo script` should make reproducible bug reports easier;

- the rustc-dev-guide says bug fixes should come with succinct regression tests, which means escalation should anticipate testability rather than stopping at prose;
- the libtest-JSON goal is explicitly about moving reporting responsibility upward toward Cargo/runners, which supports richer escalation imports;
- Cargo 1.94 keeps making local build evidence more machine-usable through `cargo report rebuild` and `cargo report sessions`; and
- the March 2026 challenges post plus the 2025 survey both reinforce that debugging, resource usage, and ongoing friction remain live enough that a better repro-to-regression lane would help both users and maintainers.

## Current frontier note (rev0409)
### Promote the adoption navigation contract as the clearest anti-tacit-knowledge / project-scoped recommendation move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **recommendation truth is not one thing**: the Rust ecosystem needs a reviewable boundary for **what question is being asked, which lanes are serious candidates, which maintainer-authored references count, what imported evidence shaped the answer, what local-fit work was actually done, and what the final brief may honestly claim**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Ecosystem Atlas** as the explicit **domain / lane / slot / curator / freshness** seam;
- keep **Reviewable Lane Defaults** and **renewal receipts** as the immediate reusable-default execution layer;
- keep **Adoption Decision Stack** as the explicit **recommendation-composition** seam;
- keep **Canonical Learning**, **Trust Decision**, **Maintenance Reality**, and **Semantic Context** as imported evidence and grounding lanes; but
- promote **Adoption Navigation Bundle**, read now as an **Adoption Navigation Contract**, as the clearest next **anti-tacit-knowledge / project-scoped recommendation** move for question records, candidate lanes, canonical references, imported evidence, local-fit checks, and bounded human/platform/CI/assistant handoffs.

Why this is the right move now:
- Rust's March 20, 2026 challenges post names ecosystem-navigation choice paralysis and tacit knowledge directly;
- the 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain canonical while LLM-mediated learning and agentic editors rise;
- Rust's December 2025 vision work says users need help getting oriented in crates.io and lack a clear place to get advice on a good starter set of crates;
- docs.rs default targets already changed to reflect current platform reality, which means recommendation canon must stay freshness-visible;
- crates.io now exposes materially better machine-usable recommendation inputs; and
- the Rust Foundation now treats adoption growth, maintenance, and infrastructure as one strategic system.

Interpretation rule:
- treat this promotion as the clearest next **anti-tacit-knowledge / project-scoped recommendation** move;
- do **not** let it silently replace the broad ranking led by **Build-State Evidence**;
- do **not** let it erase the lower-layer seams owned by **Atlas**, **Reviewable Lane Defaults**, **Adoption Decision**, **Canonical Learning**, **Trust Decision**, **Maintenance Reality**, or **Semantic Context**; and
- do **not** let one curated site, one starter template, or one assistant summary impersonate the contract.

## Current frontier note (rev0401)
### Promote the reviewable edit contract as the next mutation / review / handoff-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **code-mutation truth is not the same seam as compiler facts, semantic context, editor sessions, or assistant proposals**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Workspace Environment Stack / Workspace Environment Contract** as the explicit **environment / realization / observation / handoff** seam;
- keep **Cargo build interop contract** as the explicit **workspace-graph / plan / event / adapter** seam;

- promote **Edit Workflow Kit**, read now as a **Reviewable Edit Contract**, as the clearest next **mutation / review / handoff-shaping** move for compiler suggestions, edition migrations, IDE refactors, bots, and assistants.

Why this is the right move now:
- the Cargo Book says `cargo fix` automatically applies `rustc` suggestions, but only over the configuration actually checked, which makes subject/config identity part of the edit story;
- the Edition Guide says migration can require repeated passes, `--broken-code`, and manual handling for macros, generated code, or doctests, which proves candidate discovery, selection, application, and verification are not one step;
- Cargo 1.90 explicitly says the current `cargo fix` architecture is slow, applies only a subset of lints, and is hard to make selective or interactive because of its `rustc`-proxy design and locking;
- the GSoC 2025 `cargo-fixit` prototype shows a top-level controlled and more interactive design is feasible;
- Cargo 1.93 says schema work could unblock a faster, more flexible fix architecture;
- the StableMIR publication goal shows Rust wants semver-governed public compiler-facing crates for analyzers, linters, and development environments;
- and the 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain canonical while editors with agentic support are rising, which strengthens the case for a governed edit boundary instead of direct assistant mutation.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest broad/buildable epic contribution overall;
2. keep **Adoption Navigation + reviewable defaults + receipts** as the strongest anti-tacit-knowledge answer overall;
3. keep **Debuggability** high as the rising cross-cutting frontier;

## Current frontier note (rev0400)
### Promote the workspace environment contract as the next environment / handoff-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.
What it needed was a sharper statement that **workspace setup truth is not the same seam as Cargo build interop or final-artifact handoff**.

The clearest next move is now:
- keep **Cargo artifact contract** as the explicit **final-output / sidecar / downstream-handoff** seam;
- keep **Cargo build interop contract** as the explicit **workspace-graph / plan / event / adapter** seam for Cargo in larger build systems; but
- promote **Workspace Environment Stack / Workspace Environment Contract** as the clearest next **environment / realization / observation / handoff-shaping** move for humans, editors, CI, and agents.

Why this is the right move now:
- the 2025 State of Rust survey says resource usage remains a real pain, online docs remain canonical, and editors with agentic support are rising;
- Cargo config is explicitly hierarchical and merges parent-directory configuration, so environment truth is already partly a discovery and precedence problem;
- Cargo workspace discovery also walks parent directories and changes package-selection defaults based on current working directory and workspace-root posture;
- rustup override selection also walks the directory tree and `rust-toolchain.toml` can declare channel/components/targets/profile, which proves toolchain pinning is real but incomplete environment truth;
- Cargo 1.94 explicitly says broken parent manifests or `.cargo/config.toml` files can fail unrelated builds and says broader workspace/config discovery behavior still needs improvement;
- rust-analyzer already exposes override commands, invocation strategies, extra args/env, linked-workspace behavior, and dedicated target-dir tradeoffs, which proves editor reality and Cargo reality still need an honest shared handoff;
- Dev Containers and Nix are real reproducible realization substrates, but they are generic substrates rather than a Rust-specific review layer; and
- the Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy explicitly couples stable infrastructure, sustainable maintenance, and adoption growth, which is exactly where environment handoff truth lives.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest broad/buildable epic contribution overall;
2. keep **Adoption Navigation + reviewable defaults + receipts** as the strongest anti-tacit-knowledge answer overall;
3. keep **Debuggability** high as the rising cross-cutting frontier;
4. keep **Rust inner-loop contract / Feedback Loop Stack** as the clearest next **bundle-shaping** move beneath the build/debug band;
5. promote **Workspace Environment Stack / Workspace Environment Contract** as the clearest next **environment / handoff-shaping** move beneath Tooling Contract + Toolchain Productization + Native Dependency + Runtime Settings + Credentials;
6. keep **Package Intake Gateway** as the clearest newly sharpened **supply-chain/intake-shaping** seam;
7. keep **Compatibility Claims** as the clearest next **support/claim-shaping** move;
8. keep **Proc-Macro Exit Stack** as the clearest next **compile-time/transition-shaping** move;
9. keep **Cargo artifact contract** as the clearest next **final-output / handoff-shaping** move beneath Cargo output surfaces;
10. keep **Cargo build interop contract** as the clearest next **workspace-graph / plan / event / adapter-shaping** move beneath Cargo plumbing, rust-analyzer discovery, and larger-build-system integration pressure;
11. keep **browser + Node dual-target Wasm package overlay** as the clearest next **public-lane** candidate if the archive widens maintained defaults again.

Preferred shape:
- workspace environment contract = **subject/discovery truth + declared-intent truth + realization truth + secret/credential posture truth + observation/drift truth + consumer handoff truth**;
- keep it explicitly adjacent to, but distinct from, both **Cargo build interop contract** and **Cargo artifact contract**;
- do not flatten Cargo config precedence, workspace discovery, rustup pinning, rust-analyzer overrides, devcontainer/Nix realization, secret posture, and agent-safe handoffs into one fake “repo setup” story;

## Current frontier note (rev0399)
### Promote the Cargo build interop contract as the next workspace-graph / adapter-shaping move
The broad ladder is unchanged, and the archive did **not** need another top-band reorder.

- Cargo removed the old unstable `build-plan` feature and explicitly pointed toward plumbing commands, `--unit-graph`, and structured logging instead;
- Cargo's unstable `--unit-graph` docs now say that `cargo metadata` fundamentally cannot represent some newer feature relationships and intra-package build-unit realities;
- Cargo 1.94 work shows structured logging, `cargo report`, and workspace/config discovery are all getting more real at once;
- rust-analyzer already documents provisional discovery/event contracts and `{label}`-aware overrides, which proves real demand for a stable Rust graph/plan interface;
- Fuchsia is a live proof that generated Rust project graphs are strategically important but still too format-fragile; and
- BSP shows the cross-language demand for build-tool / IDE interop, while still leaving room for Rust-specific unit/package/build-script semantics.

## Current frontier note (rev0395)
### Deepen the supply-chain/intake band without rewriting the broad ladder: promote package intake gateway
The rev0394 broad ranking still holds.
The archive did **not** need another ladder refresh immediately afterward.
What it needed was a sharper note on one under-modeled seam the new evidence made much more concrete: **package intake gateway**.

Why this is the right move now:
- Cargo’s March 21, 2026 security advisory says a vulnerability in the `tar` crate used by Cargo to extract packages during a build can let a malicious crate change permissions on arbitrary directories, and it explicitly calls out alternate-registry users to verify impact with their vendor;
- Cargo source replacement and alternate-registry docs already make route semantics explicit, but the ecosystem still lacks one portable artifact boundary for route + payload + local staging truth;
- `cargo package` already models normalized manifests, default lockfile inclusion, flattened symlinks, pristine extraction/build verification, and best-effort-only VCS metadata, which means package ingress is already a real contract surface rather than an implementation footnote;
- `cargo install` still has materially different lockfile, target-dir, and config-discovery behavior, proving that “package intake” and “consumer install” are adjacent but not identical layers;
- crates.io’s January 2026 update added Trusted-Publishing-only mode and `pubtime`, while the February 2026 malicious-crate policy update pushes routine malware communication toward RustSec advisories, increasing the value of durable local intake artifacts rather than ambient announcement watching;
- and sandboxed build scripts matter, but they govern compile-time execution **after intake**, not the route/extraction/staging boundary itself.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep **Build-State Evidence** as the strongest broad/buildable epic contribution overall;
2. keep **Adoption Navigation + reviewable defaults + receipts** as the strongest anti-tacit-knowledge answer overall;
3. keep **Debuggability** high as the rising cross-cutting frontier;
4. keep **Rust inner-loop contract / Feedback Loop Stack** as the clearest next **bundle-shaping** move beneath the build/debug band;
5. promote **Package Intake Gateway** as the clearest next **supply-chain/intake-shaping** seam beneath **Package Admission + Dependency Review + Consumer Install**.

Preferred shape:
- package intake gateway = **subject truth + route truth + payload truth + staging/extraction truth + lock/resolution truth + handoff truth**;
- do not flatten publication admission, local intake, dependency review, compile-time execution authority, and consumer install into one fake “supply-chain safety” blob;

## Current frontier note (rev0388)
### Add and defend the missing mobile-product card: conservative mobile app product
The strongest next correction after the three-way public-web split was no longer another web note. It was the missing **mobile-first app product** card. The archive already had a desktop-product card, a polyglot component card, and an abstract client-productization stack, but it still lacked a maintained answer to a central practical question: what should a serious Android+iOS product normalize around right now if Rust should own meaningful core logic without forcing the entire UI/runtime story into Rust?

Why this is the right correction now:
- Rust’s latest challenges framing still says ecosystem navigation is burdened by **choice paralysis** and **tacit knowledge**;
- the 2025 State of Rust survey still says docs are canonical while editor/LLM mediation rises;
- Tauri 2 is now real on mobile, but its own docs keep mobile dev-server setup, capabilities/permissions, and Kotlin/Swift plugin escape hatches explicit rather than magical;
- Dioxus mobile is much stronger than it used to be, but current setup/bundle/deploy docs still keep tooling and signing realities visible enough that it is not yet the broadest boring default;
- Slint is now a serious mobile lane too, including current iOS/Android safe-area and virtual-keyboard work, but that still reads as a strong native/declarative alternative rather than the general default;
- UniFFI gives production-quality Swift and Kotlin bindings, but still reflects a native-shell integration lane rather than one unified cross-platform product shell; and
- flutter_rust_bridge plus Flutter currently gives the clearest combination of ergonomic Rust bindings and a boring app-store distribution path.

Immediate priority order:
1. keep the new **mobile app product** card and receipt current;
2. keep **desktop-first app product**, **mobile-first app product**, and **polyglot workspace component** distinct instead of drifting back into one generic “client app” lane;
3. renew the oldest central card whose lane-level answer materially changed;
4. only then consider another new public card such as **native-shell mobile + UniFFI**, **edge-runtime web product**, or **public SDK / generated-client**.

Preferred shape:
- mobile-product lane = **mobile shell/runtime truth + Rust-core/bridge truth + native escape truth + permission/device-capability truth + store/package/signing truth + support/docs truth**;
- do not flatten this lane into a generic GUI-framework verdict;
- do not silently widen it into desktop-first client guidance, narrow shared-component guidance, or “pure Rust mobile UI” ideology.

## Current frontier note (rev0375)
Treat **Workspace Environment Bundle** (`design/workspace-environment-bundle.md`) as the immediate Tier A sharpening target under **Epic Contribution Ladder 2026**. The archive should now treat **Tooling Contract + Toolchain Productization + Native Dependency + Runtime Settings + Credentials** as one explicit workenv program with a concrete MVP: **workspace subject → declared intent → one host observation lane → one substrate realization lane → one bounded handoff**.

Why this now matters:
- the 2025 State of Rust survey says docs remain canonical, editors with agentic support are rising, and resource usage/debugging remain notable pains;
- Cargo config and Cargo workspace discovery both walk parent directories, so environment truth already depends on discovery boundaries rather than only local files;
- rustup toolchain files pin channel/date/components/targets/profile but still cover only one slice of environment reality;
- Cargo 1.94 says broken parent manifests or `.cargo/config.toml` files can poison unrelated builds;
- rust-analyzer already runs per-workspace commands by default and supports override commands, extra args/env, and linked-workspace behavior;
- Dev Containers and Nix are real repeatable substrates, but Rust still lacks the thin review layer above them; and
- the archive already has the lower-level leaves, so the stronger move is now **bundle shaping** rather than another adjacent seam.

Preferred design shape:
- workspace subject → declared intent → explicit realization reports (`host`, `devcontainer`, `nix`, `ci`, `remote`) → observed state → redacted secret posture → bounded human/editor/CI/agent handoffs.
- Prefer a thin `cargo workenv` / `workenv-pack/v0` layer over a universal environment manager, secret vault, forced substrate migration, or setup badge.

## Current frontier note (rev0374)
Treat **Consumer Lifecycle Continuity Bundle** (`design/consumer-lifecycle-continuity-bundle.md`) as the immediate Tier A sharpening target under **Epic Contribution Ladder 2026**. The archive should now treat **Consumer Install + Update Continuity + Distribution Contract + Distribution-Route Mobility** as one explicit receipt-chain program with a concrete MVP: **managed subject → current owned state → typed lifecycle event → route/home continuity → bounded support/incident handoff**.

Why this now matters:
- Cargo install already defines install-root precedence, source classes, reinstall triggers, packaged-lock behavior, and a special config-discovery boundary for non-`--path` installs;
- Cargo uninstall uses the same root-precedence model but only removes packages installed with `cargo install`, which makes ownership scope central;
- Cargo package says `--exclude-lockfile` is not for general use because some consumers expect a lockfile, including `cargo install --locked`;
- Cargo’s source-replacement docs and cargo vendor docs keep exact-copy mirrors, vendored read-only trees, and divergent local carries intentionally distinct;
- Cargo’s 1.86 development update explicitly says installed-binary update support is still outside Cargo proper, which means a portable continuity receipt chain is still missing; and
- the archive already has the leaf notes, so the stronger move is now **bundle shaping** rather than another adjacent seam.

Preferred design shape:
- managed subject → current managed state → typed lifecycle events (`INSTALL`, `UPDATE`, `ROLLBACK`, `UNINSTALL`, `ROUTE_CHANGE`) → explicit ownership scope → bounded downstream handoffs.
- Prefer a thin `cargo lifecycle` / `consumer-lifecycle-pack/v0` layer over a universal updater, package-manager abstraction layer, or app-store wrapper.

## Current frontier note (rev0373)
Treat **Epic Contribution Ladder 2026** (`design/epic-contribution-ladder-2026.md`) as the immediate anti-sprawl frontier above many adjacent seam notes. The archive should now distinguish **research priority** from **build priority** and default to sharpening the most buildable near-term companion-tool band: **Build-State Evidence**, **Workspace Environment**, **Package Admission**, **Consumer Lifecycle Continuity** (Consumer Install + Update Continuity + Distribution Contract + Distribution-Route Mobility), and **Toolchain Productization**.

Why this now matters:
- Rust’s March 20, 2026 challenges post says ecosystem navigation still depends too much on tacit knowledge and choice paralysis;
- the 2025 State of Rust survey says docs remain canonical while editor/LLM-mediated workflows are rising;
- Rust’s December 2025 vision work argues for better crate-level supportive interfaces and broader build-workflow extensibility;
- the 2026 flagship goals keep cargo-script, public/private dependencies, SBOM support, and build-std / larger-build-system integration in the active top band;
- Cargo 1.94 says workspace/config discovery remains brittle, the March 2026 build-dir-layout testing call shows tools still depend on internals, and Cargo 1.86 explicitly says installed-binary update support is still outside Cargo proper;
- the Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy couples stable infrastructure, sustainable maintenance, and adoption.

Preferred design shape:
- one ranked ladder for **what is most worth building now**;
- explicit MVP shape, artifact boundary, and non-goals for each top candidate;
- a bias toward improving the buildability of Tier A candidates before minting another top-band seam note.
- Prefer a thin **epic-candidate ladder** over another isolated frontier note when the archive already understands the seam but still has not chosen the buildable implementation path.

## Current frontier note (rev0372)
Treat **Distribution-Route Mobility Stack** (`design/distribution-route-mobility-stack.md`) as the next cross-cutting source/distribution seam worth sharpening beside **Publisher & Source Identity**, **Distribution Contract**, **Airgap**, **Fork Continuity**, and **Sunset Transition**, above **registry-switch / mirror-config / vendored-copy / local-registry / `[patch]` carry / multiple-location bridge** routes.

Why this now matters:
- Cargo’s registries docs make alternate registries a first-class route, let `package.publish` restrict allowed publish homes, and recent release notes say `cargo publish` will use an alternate registry by default when it is the only allowed target;
- Cargo’s source-replacement docs say replacement sources must be **exact copies**, may not add new crates, and are not the same thing as patching or a private registry;
- Cargo’s dependency docs say crates.io packages cannot depend on code published outside crates.io, while **multiple locations** let local `git`/`path` work bridge to a registry version later;
- `cargo vendor` says vendored sources are read-only and directs actual modifications toward `[patch]` or local `path`, so local copy and divergent carry already have different official semantics;
- crates.io’s January 2026 update expanded Trusted Publishing and Trusted-Publishing-only mode, making publish-home and publish-authority shifts more explicit;
- the Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy explicitly names **signed crates** and **official mirrors**, making route plurality part of Rust’s infrastructure strategy; and
- Rust’s March 2026 challenges post says ecosystem navigation still depends too much on tacit knowledge and choice paralysis, which gets worse when route changes hide in config, CI, or release prose.

Preferred design shape:
- route subject → current route class → exact-copy-or-divergent posture → publish/consumer constraints → route-change intent → bounded install/continuity/incident/sunset handoff.
- Prefer a thin `cargo route` / `route-pack/v0` layer over another registry implementation, mirror manager, vendoring helper, or migration dashboard.

## Current frontier note (rev0371)
Treat **Sunset Transition Stack** (`design/sunset-transition-stack.md`) as the next cross-cutting lifecycle/governance seam worth sharpening beside **Lifecycle Ledger**, **Succession Continuity**, **Fork Continuity**, **Migration Kit**, and **Trust Decision**, above **deprecated-badge / selective-yank / repo-archived / last-good-line / successor-README / unmaintained-advisory** routes.

Why this now matters:
- Cargo’s manifest docs still define maintenance states like `passively-maintained`, `as-is`, `looking-for-maintainer`, and `deprecated`, but they also say crates.io no longer displays badges directly, so Rust has lifecycle vocabulary without a strong shared review surface on the main registry;
- Cargo’s publishing docs say yanks do **not** delete code and existing lockfiles continue to work, so “we do not support this anymore” is different from “this disappeared”;
- RFC 3660 keeps deletions tightly constrained, which means many real Rust artifacts will remain in the registry archive long after maintainers want users to stop selecting them by default;
- RFC 3646 says unreachable-owner transfer mediation should stop and many continuity cases must pick a different name, so sunset often borders but should not be confused with succession or fork-continuation routes;
- the Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy plus the maintenance writeup say maintenance is ongoing, invisible, and burnout-sensitive, which makes support narrowing or retirement a legitimate stewardship act rather than automatic neglect; and
- Rust’s March 2026 challenges post says ecosystem navigation still depends too much on tacit knowledge and choice paralysis, which gets worse when sunset state lives only in READMEs, yanks, or rumor.

Preferred design shape:
- sunset subject / narrowing-or-retirement intent → last-supported-line and support-window truth → replacement-or-no-successor posture → registry/archive action posture → consumer off-ramp → closure witness / re-entry-or-terminal-state.
- Prefer a thin `cargo sunset` / `sunset-pack/v0` layer over another badge scheme, graveyard site, or stealth deprecation score.

## Current frontier note (rev0370)
Treat **Fork Continuity Stack** (`design/fork-continuity-stack.md`) as the next cross-cutting governance/distribution seam worth sharpening beside **Lifecycle Ledger**, **Succession Continuity**, **Publisher & Source Identity**, **Migration Kit**, and **Trust Decision**, above **successor-map / org-fork / temporary-fork / renamed-crate / `[patch]` carry / alternate-registry** routes.

Why this now matters:
- RFC 3646 says the crates.io team wants to stop mediating crate transfers, and unreachable-owner cases should expect to pick a different name instead of relying on registry arbitration;
- crates.io policy still requires explicit current-owner approval for ownership transfer, so “continuity” and “transfer” are not the same thing;
- Cargo’s overriding-dependencies docs make `[patch]` a first-class local carry lane, while source replacement explicitly says exact-copy mirrors are **not** patching or private-registry divergence;
- Cargo’s registries and dependency docs make alternate registries and multiple-location bridging real routes, but crates.io packages still cannot depend on code from outside crates.io;
- the Rust Project’s crate-ownership policy explicitly names an **Expatriated** category, proving continuation outside the original official owner is already a real concept; and
- the Rust Innovation Lab plus Rust’s 2026 challenges post together say that neutral-home relocations and tacit-knowledge-heavy successor choices are now ecosystem-scale problems rather than edge cases.

Preferred design shape:
- origin subject / continuation intent → authority-and-identity relationship → distribution route → compatibility-and-migration posture → continuity witness / reconvergence-or-exit.
- Prefer a thin `cargo continuation` / `continuation-pack/v0` layer over a forced-transfer service, fork marketplace, or universal successor badge.

- crates.io’s Trusted Publishing enhancements mean publish authority now often lives in CI/OIDC posture as much as in human intention; and
- the Rust Innovation Lab proves there is a real continuity lane above simple co-maintainership: a stable neutral home, governance support, legal/admin backing, and fiscal sponsorship for keystone infrastructure.

Preferred design shape:

## Current frontier note (rev0367)
Treat **Deviation Governance Stack** (`design/deviation-governance-stack.md`) as the next cross-cutting control-plane seam worth sharpening beside **Institutional Overlay**, **Preview Adoption**, **Canary Validation**, **Baseline Ratchet**, and **Ecosystem Incident Response**, above **Release Truth / Support Envelope / Trust Decision / local policy consumer** routes.

Why this now matters:
- the rustc lint docs already distinguish temporary/self-checking suppressions (`expect`) from other lint posture, which proves some exceptions are meant to clear rather than linger silently;
- the Edition Guide’s advanced migrations docs treat partial carries, repeated runs, and configuration-specific migration passes as ordinary Rust migration reality rather than an embarrassing edge case;
- nightly/unstable docs make preview activation explicit and per-project, which means preview use is already a governed deviation path rather than a hidden implementation detail;
- Cargo’s override docs make `[patch]` a first-class way to carry local fixes or unpublished upstream work, while Cargo config docs warn that config-local patches are easy to hide because they usually are not checked into source control and inherit through parent directories;
- source replacement and patching carry different semantic promises, so not every “override” means the same thing;
- Cargo’s publishing docs say yanks do not fix existing lockfiles, which means exceptional carries can outlive upstream corrective action;
- Cargo’s CI guide shows that allow-fail or scheduled watch lanes are already real design choices with notification/ownership consequences; and
- Cargo’s `rust-version` docs say policy drift causes users to infer promises you did not intend, which makes explicit deviation records more important.

Preferred design shape:
- normative basis → deviation subject → mechanism → scope → owner/justification → expiry/watch → exit handoff.
- Prefer a thin `cargo deviate` / `deviation-pack/v0` layer over another policy wiki, giant waiver spreadsheet, or hidden compliance engine.

## Current frontier note (rev0365)
Treat **Canary Validation Stack** (`design/canary-validation-stack.md`) as the next change-management seam worth sharpening beside **Preview Adoption**, **Baseline Ratchet**, **Migration Truth**, and **Cargo Report**, above **Compatibility Claims / Release Truth / Defect Escalation** consumer routes.

Why this now matters:
- The Rust book’s release-train appendix says Rust ships on a six-week train model and explicitly frames beta as the last stop before stable where regressions should be caught early.
- Cargo’s CI guide is unusually explicit that watch lanes have different semantics: nightly may be allow-fail, latest-deps verification is its own lane, `rust-version` verification is its own lane, and higher-risk projects may need more combinations.
- Cargo’s `rust-version` docs say support policy should be chosen and documented, call `rust-version` changes a minor incompatibility, and explicitly discuss dev-branch versus release-branch policy drift.
- Cargo’s resolver docs plus the CI guide’s latest-deps example show that “latest graph watch” and “declared floor watch” are different lanes with different dependency-selection semantics.
- Cargo’s future-incompat docs, `cargo report future-incompat`, and `--future-incompat-report` flags on build/check/test make incoming hard-error warnings replayable and attachable instead of transient terminal folklore.
- Cargo config exposes `future-incompat-report.frequency`, showing that notification policy is already an explicit part of the watch surface.
- The Cargo 1.94 cycle keeps making Cargo-native report/session/rebuild evidence more reusable, which creates a stronger substrate for machine-usable canary outcomes.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says most users stay on stable while nightly is mostly used out of necessity, which raises the importance of advance warning without laundering preview lanes into whole-project support claims.

Preferred design shape:
- current baseline/support policy → selected canary lanes / cadence / coverage slices → gating and notification rules → observed canary outcomes → escalation to preview / ratchet / migration / dependency / defect lanes → bounded consumer handoff.
- Prefer a thin `cargo canary` / `canary-pack/v0` layer over another CI template repo, dashboard, dependency bot, or generic “test stable/beta/nightly” essay.

## Current frontier note (rev0364)
Treat **Preview Adoption Stack** (`design/preview-adoption-stack.md`) as the next change-management seam worth sharpening beside **Baseline Ratchet**, **Migration Truth**, and **Workspace Environment**, above **Compatibility Claims / Support Envelope / Canonical Learning** consumer routes.

Why this now matters:
- The Rust book’s release-train appendix says unstable features land behind feature flags on nightly, stable/beta deliberately reject them, and beta is a real pre-stable testing lane.
- Cargo’s unstable-features docs say nightly-only features require explicit activation (`cargo-features`, `-Z unstable-options`, `-Z ...`) and should be followed through tracking issues.
- The Unstable Book says its docs are best-effort and may be out of date, which means projects need an explicit watch/landing posture rather than assuming unstable docs are the durable contract.
- The Edition Guide says unstable next-edition work can be tested on nightly with explicit `cargo-features`, may lack automatic migrations, and is not yet fully finished.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says most people use stable while nightly is mostly used out of necessity; the 2024 survey says a substantial minority still use nightly for unstable features and beta is underused.
- Rust-for-Linux’s stable-support goals say unstable features have no reliability guarantee and can force pinned compiler versions, which makes preview usage a real support-policy seam.
- Active goals like scalable vectors and build-std are explicitly framed as nightly experiments or unstable MVPs intended to reach stabilization.

Preferred design shape:
- preview subject → activation/pin → adoption scope → guardrails / support boundary → watch/landing posture → stable-landing, quarantine-renewal, or removal handoff.
- Prefer a thin `cargo previewadopt` / `preview-pack/v0` layer over another feature-tracker portal, nightly distro, or generic “use stable unless you must” essay.

## Current frontier note (rev0363)
Treat **Baseline Ratchet Stack** (`design/baseline-ratchet-stack.md`) as the next change-management seam worth sharpening beside **Migration Truth**, **Compatibility Claims**, and **Lifecycle Ledger**, above **Release Truth / Update Continuity / support-policy** consumer routes.

Why this now matters:
- Rust 1.85 stabilized **Rust 2024**, and the Edition Guide still presents edition migration as a staged, configuration-sensitive workflow rather than one command.
- Cargo’s `rust-version` docs say changing `rust-version` is a minor incompatibility, recommend documenting a policy, warn against drift, and explicitly allow multiple policies in one workspace.
- Rust 1.84 stabilized MSRV-aware dependency selection, says resolver `3` can require a higher MSRV, and says the 2024 edition gets the new resolver behavior by default.
- The resolver docs say mixed-policy workspaces are only handled heuristically, so one member can force lower dependency versions for another or version unification can still force a higher shared version.
- Cargo’s CI guide now documents **verifying latest dependencies** and **verifying `rust-version`** as distinct checks.
- Cargo 1.94 says TOML 1.1 support can inadvertently raise the Cargo parser floor and explicitly points to verifying `rust-version` in CI.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says developers mostly use stable and keep up with releases, which means baseline ratchets are part of ordinary Rust maintenance rather than an edge case.

Preferred design shape:
- current baseline vector → proposed ratchet delta → workspace/member variance → verification matrix → release-line policy → bounded migration/release/support handoff.
- Prefer a thin `cargo ratchet` / `baseline-pack/v0` layer over another upgrader, CI snippet, or “always latest Rust” policy page.

- Cargo’s build-analysis work and `cargo report` direction make machine-usable evidence about rebuilds, flags, profiles, and sessions more available.
- The build-dir-layout work plus March 2026 testing call say editor/CLI coexistence and cache-layout assumptions are still live, tool-breaking seams.
- rust-analyzer now documents `cargo.targetDir` as an explicit lock-avoidance tradeoff that duplicates artifacts.
- The 2026 debugging survey says debugger quality still varies materially by debugger family / OS and that async debugging is not yet first-class.

Preferred design shape:

## Current frontier correction (rev0353)
### Rank the cross-cutting control-plane epics before adding more narrow kits
Treat the archive as missing one specific thing: it already has many good frontier notes, but it still needed a **current priority ladder** telling future revisions which of those worthy contributions actually deserve to be built first.

Why this now matters:
- the 2025 State of Rust survey still says resource usage is a major productivity problem, debugging is still high, and online docs remain canonical while learning flows drift toward editor/LLM mediation;
- the 2025 compiler-performance survey says rebuild latency, `cargo check`/`cargo build` duplication, editor latency, and lack of build explanations remain real blockers;
- Cargo’s current work keeps build analysis, build-dir layout, target-dir locking, workspace/config discovery, and cargo-script active rather than hypothetical;
- crates.io now exposes Security-tab data, Trusted Publishing controls, SLOC, and `pubtime`, which means package-review and recommendation inputs are materially richer than before;
- Rust’s 2025 vision work explicitly asks for better crate guidance and better supportive diagnostics/guidance from crates; and
- the Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy keeps stable infrastructure, sustainable maintenance, and responsible adoption at the center of ecosystem investment.

What to prefer next:
- prefer `design/ecosystem-priority-ladder-2026.md` and `meta/ACTIVE_FRONTIER.md` as the archive’s default ranking and working-set governor;
- rank **Build-State Evidence + Adoption Decision + Canonical Learning + Package Admission + Workspace Environment** ahead of most narrower domain kits unless official motion or multi-stack leverage justifies an exception;
- treat **Cargo Report**, **Atlas**, **Maintenance Reality**, and **Compatibility Claims** as strong adjacent/control-plane seams that often feed or follow that first band rather than competing with it randomly;
- treat narrower domain kits — vector, tensor, dataflow, filesystem, native-edge, geospatial, safety-critical, and similar — as crucial substrate when they unlock multiple later stacks, but not as automatic top-priority just because they are technically interesting; and
- force future revisions to say whether they are improving a **control-plane seam**, a **supporting evidence substrate**, or a **domain lane map** before reordering the frontier.

The worthy contribution here is increasingly not another one-off crate family. It is the thin, reviewable layer that preserves evidence, freshness, and bounded handoffs across build/debug, ecosystem choice, canonical learning, package review, and workspace/operator reality.

Current frontier note (rev0352): the archive should now treat **Vector Surface Kit** as a freshly sharpened lane-mapped frontier across performance-sensitive language/library work and as a lower-layer substrate for scientific, media, crypto, and systems consumers. Prefer **portable-fixed-width + target-specific/compile-time + runtime-multiversioned + fallback-backed + scalable-vector/experimental + adapter/consumer-import + lane-shape/mask + target-feature + dispatch/fallback + alignment/layout + semantic evidence** over another intrinsic wrapper, one fake “SIMD support” verdict, or a universal vector facade. The concrete rule for what must stay separate is now [`design/vector-surface-lane-map.md`](./design/vector-surface-lane-map.md), while the ranked execution path now lives in [`design/vector-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/vector-surface-pilot-program.md).

### Sharpen Vector Surface with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “SIMD support” or “vector portability” verdict
Treat **Vector Surface Kit** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right substrate for lane shape, target features, dispatch/fallback, alignment/layout, operation semantics, and evidence, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different vector lanes** Rust is actually using.

Why this now matters:
- Rust’s 2026 flagships keep **Sized Hierarchy and Scalable Vectors** active, including refined `Sized`, `const Sized`, scalable-vector RFC progress, SVE types/intrinsics in `stdarch`, and initial SME design work;
- the 2025H1 SVE/SME goal says SVE is still unsupported today and explicitly warns against overfitting the design to one architecture;
- `std::simd` is portable across targets but still nightly-only, and it still documents real semantic caveats like subnormal-`f32` flush-to-zero on some older architectures;
- the Rust Reference keeps `#[target_feature]` hazards explicit, while also documenting a materially different Wasm load-time lane;
- `safe_arch` is explicitly compile-time-only via `#[cfg()]`, not a runtime-fallback surface;
- `multiversion` is explicitly a runtime detection / function-multiversioning lane; and
- `wide` is explicitly a fallback-backed lane that may use explicit SIMD, let LLVM vectorize, or become scalar while keeping the math correct.

Archive decision for this revision: add `design/vector-surface-lane-map.md` and `design/vector-surface-pilot-program.md`; refresh `design/vector-surface-kit.md`, `gaps/vector-surfaces-simd-target-features-and-dispatch-contracts.md`, and `proposals/epic-vector-surface-kit.md`; prepend matching frontier notes in `PRIORITIES.md` and `STRATEGIC_FRONTIER.md`; update `INDEX.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `RESEARCH_LOG.md`; and strengthen `meta/AMNESIA_RESISTORS.md` so future vector/scientific/media/crypto/offload revisions keep **portable SIMD lanes, target-specific compile-time lanes, runtime multiversion lanes, fallback-backed lanes, scalable-vector experiments, adapter/import posture, and target/layout/semantic evidence** distinct instead of narrating one fake vector lane.

Current frontier note (rev0351): the archive should now treat **Tensor Surface Kit** as a freshly sharpened lane-mapped frontier inside the wider Scientific Productization Stack. Prefer **dense-array + matrix/linalg + runtime-tensor/device + persisted-array + interchange/attachment + adapter/consumer-import + shape/dtype + layout/view + ownership/device/storage evidence** over another universal array trait, one fake “tensor support” verdict, or a new numerical mega-runtime. The concrete rule for what must stay separate is now [`design/tensor-surface-lane-map.md`](./design/tensor-surface-lane-map.md), while the ranked execution path still lives in [`design/tensor-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/tensor-surface-pilot-program.md).

### Sharpen Tensor Surface with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “tensor support” or “array portability” verdict
Treat **Tensor Surface Kit** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right substrate for shape/dtype, layout/view, ownership, device/autodiff, and interchange/storage truth, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different numerical lanes** Rust is actually using.

Why this now matters:
- `ndarray` explicitly centers `ArrayBase`, owned arrays, views, and raw-storage vocabulary for general n-dimensional array work;
- `nalgebra` and `faer` remain materially different matrix/linalg lanes rather than just synonyms for generic tensors;
- Candle and Burn keep runtime-managed tensor, backend, device, and autodiff posture concrete;
- `zarrs` keeps persisted multidimensional-array metadata and validation concrete enough that stored-array truth should not dissolve into runtime tensor truth;

### Sharpen Trait Surface with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “trait support” or “dyn support” verdict
Treat **Trait Surface Kit** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right substrate for semantic obligations, dyn posture, return-shape truth, and migration metadata, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different trait lanes** Rust is actually using.

Why this now matters:
- Rust’s 2026 flagships explicitly keep **return type notation**, **async fn in dyn trait**, **next-solver stabilization**, and **evolvable trait hierarchies** on the active roadmap;
- the 2025H1 async goal explicitly tracks **implementable trait aliases**, **async fn in dyn Trait**, and **Dynosaur 1.0**, which means public async trait design is still an active ecosystem seam rather than a settled crate choice;
- the evolving-traits goal is directly about making `Receiver` a supertrait of `Deref` and splitting `tower::Service` into a non-`Sync` supertrait plus a `Sync` subtrait, which proves family evolution belongs in the review surface;
- RFC 3654 exists because trait methods returning opaques need explicit reviewable bounds like `Send`, while RFC 3185 explicitly left dyn safety for `async fn` traits to future work;
- docs.rs trait pages now visibly expose **Dyn Compatibility** on many traits, while `async-trait`, `dynosaur`, and `trait-variant` openly provide different present-tense adapter and split-family lanes rather than one converged answer;
- the 2025H2 in-place-initialization goal explicitly says dyn Trait for `async fn` and `-> impl Trait` relies on callee-initialized unsized returns, which ties trait-surface truth directly to initialization-surface truth; and
- the next-solver goal makes clear that some trait-family claims are still acceptance- and solver-sensitive rather than purely semantic.

## Current frontier correction (rev0348)
### Sharpen Initialization Surface with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “constructor support” or “in-place init” verdict
Treat **Initialization Surface Kit** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right substrate for placement profiles, staged assembly, and teardown posture, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different initialization lanes** Rust is actually using.

Why this now matters:
- Rust’s 2026 flagship themes explicitly keep **immobile types and guaranteed destructors** plus **design alignment on in-place initialization** on the active roadmap;
- the 2025H2 in-place-initialization goal says the project is actively comparing multiple proposals and real ecosystem experiments including the Linux kernel and Crubit;
- `MaybeUninit` explicitly documents out-pointers as a caller-controlled allocation lane rather than a hidden optimization trick;
- `Pin` and `Box::pin` prove “construct then pin” is a real mainstream lane, not the same as pinned-in-place construction;
- `Arc::new_cyclic` and `UniqueArc` prove cyclic bootstrap and unique-to-shared publication are distinct present-tense lanes with different exposure and fallibility properties;
- `pin-init` / `pinned-init` prove pinned-in-place, fallible construction is already real crate-level infrastructure;
- `self_cell` and `yoke` prove self-referential and borrow-carrying setup is another lane rather than just a footnote to cyclic init; and
- `moveit` proves foreign emplacement and destructor-coupled construction remain strategically important rather than historical curiosities.

What to prefer next:
- keep **Initialization Surface Kit** responsible for `init-surface/v0`, `placement-init-profile/v0`, `init-sequence-profile/v0`, `pin-destruction-profile/v0`, `init-adapter-profile/v0`, `init-vector-set/v0`, `init-check-report/v0`, and `init-pack/v0`;
- keep **Pointer Surface Kit** responsible for post-construction identity/aliasing/projection/receiver truth rather than letting init docs re-own pointer semantics;
- keep **Borrowing Frontier Stack** responsible for the cross-kit migration story above the init lane map rather than flattening trait/pointer/lending/init into one mega-boundary; and
- prefer the ranked rollout in [`design/initialization-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/initialization-surface-pilot-program.md): std out-pointer + construct-then-pin baseline first, cyclic bootstrap second, unique-to-shared publication third, pinned in-place/fallible lanes fourth, self-referential borrow-carrying fifth, foreign-emplace/destructor-coupled sixth.

## Current frontier correction (rev0346)
### Sharpen Async Commons with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “runtime agnostic” verdict
Treat **Async Commons Kit** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right substrate for capability profiles, common surfaces, adapters, and readiness reports, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different async lanes** Rust is actually using.
