## Current frontier correction (rev0344): Compatibility Claims Stack needs an explicit lane map

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust’s target-tier policy already distinguishes target support from stronger host-tool guarantees, so dev-host and source-build posture are not the same claim.
- rustc’s platform-support pages already record real runtime-floor facts for some targets, which means platform support is not reducible to a target triple.
- docs.rs metadata and builds docs make docs-target posture and `#[cfg(docsrs)]` behavior explicit, while the October 2025 docs.rs default-target change proved docs surfaces are a public compatibility lane that can drift.
- The Rust 2024 edition guide plus Cargo’s resolver/MSRV docs make source-build compatibility more dynamic than the archive previously modeled: resolver `3` is Rust-version aware, and one workspace member’s `rust-version` can influence another’s dependency choices.
- The Rust Reference, compiler debuginfo guide, and 2026 debugging survey together make debugger-family/version/OS/visualizer reality explicit rather than folklore.
- The 2026 / 2025H2 goals keep next-solver, Polonius, and evolving traits active, so acceptance-profile drift remains a live compatibility boundary.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey and the March 2026 challenges writeup both strengthen the case for portable compatibility truth: online docs remain canonical while machine consumers rise, and expert pain shifts toward domain-specific ecosystem maturity problems.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `design/compatibility-claims-lane-map.md`, `design/compatibility-claims-stack.md`, `design/compatibility-claims-pilot-program.md`, `gaps/compatibility-claims-platform-debugger-and-acceptance-contracts.md`, and `proposals/epic-compatibility-claims-stack.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Support Envelope Kit** responsible for dev-host, source-build, release-artifact, docs-surface, and runtime-floor truth; keep **Debuggability Stack** responsible for debugger family/version/OS/visualizer evidence; keep **Acceptance Surface Kit** responsible for compiler-lane/pattern/workaround truth; and use the lane map to stop those truths collapsing into one fake compatibility verdict.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo compat` / `compat-pack/v0` layer whose lane catalog comes first, released-binary support second, debugger tuples third, acceptance profiles fourth, mixed-workspace bundles fifth, and bounded consumer views sixth.
- The bar is not “a better matrix page”, “a compatibility badge”, or “a smarter UI-test runner”. The bar is “reviewable compatibility claims that humans, CI, release processes, docs, policy, and safety-oriented adopters can all consume honestly,” while preserving host/build/release/docs/runtime/debugger/acceptance truths as distinct lanes.

## Current frontier correction (rev0343)
### Sharpen Trust Signals and Trust Decision with an explicit lane map instead of one fake “crate trust” verdict
Treat **Trust Signals Kit / Trust Decision Stack** as newly under-modeled in one specific way: the archive already had the right trust, policy, and package-admission substrate, but it still needed a clearer map of the **different trust/admission lanes** Rust is actually using.

Why this now matters:
- crates.io’s January 2026 update added a Security tab, GitLab Trusted Publishing support, Trusted-Publishing-only mode, blocked risky triggers, and `pubtime`, which means registry discovery now emits materially richer trust-relevant facts than it used to;
- the February 2026 malicious-crate policy update makes RustSec advisories the durable public record for most routine malicious-crate removals, which means ambient incident/blog awareness is no longer the canonical lane;
- RustSec itself already feeds several consumers (`cargo-audit`, `cargo-deny`, OSV, GitHub Advisory Database), so “advisory truth” and “rendered consumer view” are clearly different lanes;
- Cargo Vet already distinguishes imported audits, criteria, trusted publisher windows, and first-party/third-party subject boundaries, which means audit attestations are not just another advisory feed;
- cargo-deny already distinguishes advisories, bans, licenses, and sources, supports JSON diagnostics, and exposes per-check exit classes, which means local graph-policy lint is not the same thing as advisory facts or imported human audits; and
- `cargo audit bin` plus `cargo-auditable` make artifact-linked vulnerability review a real post-build lane rather than a footnote to lockfile review.

What to prefer next:
- keep **Trust Signals Kit** responsible for trust-signal / trust-report / trust-import-profile / trust-diff-report / trust-pack truth;
- keep **Policy Kit** responsible for rules, waivers, and local decision reports;
- keep **Trust Decision Stack** responsible for the evidence-to-policy-to-thin-view continuity boundary;
- keep **Package Admission Stack**, **Dependency Review Stack**, **Publisher & Source Identity Stack**, and **Adoption Decision Stack** importing bounded trust artifacts instead of reinventing private trust scores;
- add and use [`design/trust-decision-lane-map.md`](./design/trust-decision-lane-map.md) so **registry discovery, advisory feeds, audit attestations, graph-policy lint, artifact recovery, local decisions/waivers, and thin consumer views** stop getting blurred together; and
- prefer the ranked rollout in [`design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md`](./design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md): lockfile evidence-to-policy first, scope-sensitive audit review second, publisher/freshness plus graph-policy third, package/release plus artifact-recovery fourth, and thin consumer views fifth.

## Current frontier correction (rev0341): SBOM Evidence Kit needs an explicit lane map

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust’s 2026 flagship supply-chain theme explicitly names **SBOM generation** and **stabilize SBOM support**, so this is no longer merely plugin territory.
- Cargo’s unstable `sbom` docs still call the output a **pre-cursor**, emit it alongside each compiled artifact, preserve duplicate differently-compiled crates, and expose `CARGO_SBOM_PATH`. That is strong evidence that Cargo is defining a build-lane source of truth, not a one-size-fits-all standards document.
- `cargo-cyclonedx` still represents a separate project-export lane that calls into Cargo and carries exporter/project-subject semantics of its own.
- The CycloneDX resolver-v2 issue still says metadata-driven export can misclassify some build-only dependencies as runtime dependencies, which is direct evidence that export lanes and Cargo-native precursor lanes must stay distinct.
- `cargo-auditable` now explicitly documents a nightly mode that uses Cargo’s native precursor for more accurate recording, and its README lists multiple downstream consumers and artifact-recovery tools. That makes embedded/recovered binary inventory a first-class lane.
- Trivy’s current Rust docs still keep source scanning (`Cargo.lock` + `Cargo.toml`) separate from binary scanning of cargo-auditable artifacts.
- cargo-dist’s changelog still keeps standalone CycloneDX release attachments separate from embedded binary metadata, proving there are at least two materially different producer-side release lanes.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `design/sbom-evidence-lane-map.md`, `design/sbom-evidence-kit.md`, `gaps/sbom-evidence-and-artifact-linked-dependency-inventory.md`, `design/inventory-evidence-stack.md`, `design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md`, `proposals/epic-sbom-evidence-kit.md`, and `proposals/epic-inventory-evidence-stack.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **SBOM Evidence Kit** responsible for `inventory-subject/v0`, `inventory-capture-report/v0`, `inventory-graph/v0`, `artifact-inventory-report/v0`, `inventory-projection-report/v0`, `inventory-diff-report/v0`, `inventory-pack/v0`, and the lane map in `design/sbom-evidence-lane-map.md`.
- Keep **Inventory Evidence Stack** responsible for package→release→install continuity above that lane map rather than letting one capture/export/recovery lane quietly become the whole lifecycle story.
- The next credible move is still the ranked rollout in `design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md`: Cargo precursor capture first, standards projection second, binary-recovery cross-check third, package-to-release attachment fourth, and install-receipt continuity fifth.
- The bar is not another exporter wrapper, scanner badge, or one-file compliance story. The bar is a thin `cargo inventory` / `inventory-pack/v0` layer whose lane reports, artifact-link reports, projection-lossiness records, recovery imports, and bounded downstream handoffs make Rust inventory claims reviewable.

## Current frontier correction (rev0339): Manifest Truth Stack needs an explicit lane map

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Cargo 1.94 makes it unusually clear that manifest meaning is not just “parse `Cargo.toml`”: parent-manifest and parent-config discovery hazards are still active design problems, and a future `package.workspace = false`-style escape hatch is being discussed.
- `cargo package` already creates a materially different packaged manifest lane by rewriting and normalizing `Cargo.toml`, removing `[patch]`, `[replace]`, and `[workspace]`, shipping `Cargo.lock` by default, and emitting `.cargo_vcs_info.json`.
- `cargo metadata` remains the stable machine-readable import lane but still asks consumers to pin `--format-version`, while `cargo info` is a distinct human-facing projection lane whose local-vs-registry default behavior is still evolving.
- workspace inheritance now spans package metadata, dependencies, and lints, which means package-local authored text and effective package meaning are no longer safely flattenable.
- single-file scripts/frontmatter are now strong enough that manifest-bearing subjects can live outside ordinary package roots while still needing reviewable defaulting/disallowed-field semantics.
- Rust 1.93’s extraction of `cargo-util-schemas` is a useful reminder that schema availability and final manifest semantics are related but not identical truths.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/manifest-truth-authored-published-and-consumed.md`, `design/manifest-surface-lane-map.md`, `design/manifest-surface-kit.md`, `design/manifest-truth-stack.md`, `design/manifest-truth-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-manifest-truth-stack.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Manifest Surface Kit** responsible for `manifest-subject/v0`, `manifest-authored-report/v0`, `manifest-publish-diff/v0`, `feature-catalog/v0`, `manifest-consumer-report/v0`, `manifest-pack/v0`, and the lane map in `design/manifest-surface-lane-map.md`.
- Keep **Repo Composition Stack** responsible for discovery/config/workspace-root truth; keep **ScriptKit** responsible for execution/cache/run posture; keep **Dependency Control Stack** responsible for chosen-graph and activated-feature truth; keep **Publisher & Source Identity Stack** responsible for issuer-backed authority and origin claims.

## Current frontier correction (rev0335): Sanitizer Battery Kit needs an explicit lane map

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust’s 2026 flagship roadmap explicitly includes **Stabilize MemorySanitizer and ThreadSanitizer Support**, including the infrastructure changes needed to provide **precompiled and instrumented standard libraries**.
- The 2025H2 sanitizer-support goal still frames the same substrate problem: practical MSan/TSan should not depend on rebuilding std by hand forever.
- The unstable-book sanitizer docs still keep partial-versus-full instrumentation, instrumented-stdlib posture, mixed-language runtime linkage, and `--target` handling materially sharp.
- The rustc exploit-mitigations chapter makes an adjacent distinction explicit: CFI / KCFI / safestack / shadow-call-stack are real Rust lanes, but they are **mitigation lanes**, not merely another bug-finding sanitizer run.
- Miri’s docs still make default host isolation and deterministic fake host APIs part of the lane, while nextest’s Miri docs still say runner-imported Miri loses some shared-resource race visibility.
- `cargo-careful` remains a real middle lane with rebuilt debug-assertion std, extra UB checks, optional sanitizer add-ons, and arbitrary system/C FFI support.
- The retag/codegen goal plus BorrowSanitizer’s February 2026 update show the horizon is widening toward aliasing-focused native instrumentation rather than collapsing onto today’s engines.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another checker, wrapper, CI matrix helper, or fake universal battery score. It is the portable lane-aware boundary above engines and below testing/toolchain/safety consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/sanitizers-and-dynamic-analysis.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-lane-map.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-kit.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-sanitizer-battery-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Test Execution Evidence Stack** responsible for harness/run/runner-native recording truth; let Sanitizer Battery import that truth explicitly via execution-import artifacts rather than re-owning it.
- Keep **Sysroot Pack Kit** responsible for rebuilt or precompiled instrumented-stdlib identity and reuse compatibility.
- Keep **Toolchain Productization Stack** responsible for the wider provisioning/activation story around toolchains, linkers, SDKs, sysroots, and runtime families.
- Keep **Safety-Critical Evidence Stack** responsible for broader assurance composition above imported dynamic-analysis packs.
- Let **Sanitizer Battery Kit** own lane identity, runtime provenance, execution imports, capability/blind-spot truth, findings, mitigation posture, waivers, and comparability drift.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md`: Miri isolated baseline first, Miri + runner import second, `cargo-careful` native checking lane third, LLVM sanitizer + instrumented stdlib finding lane fourth, mitigation/hardening lane fifth, BorrowSanitizer import sixth, and safety-critical imports last.
- The bar is not another “run all sanitizers” command. The bar is a thin `cargo sanitize` / `sanitize-pack/v0` layer with explicit lane-map truth and `sanitize-execution-import/v0` records that make Rust dynamic-analysis claims reviewable.

- The bar is not another percentage service or engine replacement. The bar is a thin `cargo cov` / `coverage-pack/v0` layer whose lane profiles, criterion profiles, execution imports, merge reports, and bounded consumer handoffs make Rust coverage claims reviewable.

- Ratatui’s panic-hook docs keep restore behavior explicit, which means cleanup is part of the public terminal contract;
- Termwiz capability docs keep heuristics, probe posture, remote/multiplexer caveats, and escape-semantic scope explicit;
- `anstream`, `unicode-display-width`, `reedline`, `TestBackend` / `vt100`, and `ratatui-image` together prove styled-output, grapheme-layout, line-editor, parser/test, and graphics lanes are all real.

The archive should therefore prefer **lane-family truth, adapter lossiness, named vectors, bounded consumer handoffs, and a ranked pilot program** over more crate enumeration, backend bake-offs, or one fake terminal-support score.

- keep **Polyglot Productization Stack** responsible for the stack-level join above Host Package + FFI Boundary + Wasm Component + Release Truth + Support Envelope;
- prefer the ranked ladder in `design/python-host-lane-map.md`: full-API extension first, `abi3` limited-API second, free-threaded extension third, `asyncio` bridge fourth, packaging/link/test handoff fifth, and embedding/CPython-upstream adjacency sixth;
- avoid another “Python support” matrix that smears together ordinary extension builds, `abi3`, free-threaded ABI, async-runtime bridges, wheel portability, and upstream CPython adoption.

## Frontier adjustment (rev0321)
- Sharpen **Native Edge Stack** with an explicit **C++ lane map**. The archive already knew that FFI Boundary + Native Dependency + external-build handoff belonged together, but it still needed a cleaner answer to which lane is under review: C ABI export/import, CXX-style safe-common bridges, autocxx-style large-codebase automation, provider/link-plan resolution, or foreign-build/package handoff.
- The new design center is therefore not “find the best interop crate” and not “wait until Rust has first-class C++ interop.” It is a thin native-edge layer that can preserve lane identity, boundary provenance, provider truth, handoff expectations, and `watch` posture for upstream-facing questions.
- This sharpens the archive’s native band: **FFI Boundary** should own explicit interface/provenance truth, **Native Dependency** should own provider/link truth, and **Native Edge Stack** should own the joined subject plus lane profile and downstream handoff.
- It also sharpens the adoption band: **Polyglot Productization**, **Release Truth**, **Support Envelope**, and **Safety Evidence** should be able to import native-edge facts without narrating one fake “interop readiness” story.

## Frontier adjustment (rev0320)
- Promote **Async Commons Kit** into explicit frontier treatment. Official Rust signals now make it hard to ignore that async pain is not only about language ergonomics or runtime reliability; it is also about missing lower-level shared async vocabulary and honest portability claims.
- The new design center is therefore not “pick the best runtime” and not “wait for std to solve everything.” It is a thin async-commons layer that can preserve capability requirements, neutral seams, adapter lossiness, and watch/defer readiness for seams still blocked on language/compiler progress.
- This sharpens the archive’s async band: **Async Lifecycle** should own ownership/cancellation/shutdown truth, **Async Reliability** should own lifecycle/replay/exploration composition, and **Async Commons** should own the lower-level portability substrate they import.
- It also sharpens the navigation band: **Ecosystem Atlas / Adoption Decision** should be able to point to explicit shared async substrate instead of free-associating about “Tokio-compatible” or “runtime agnostic” lanes.

## Frontier adjustment (rev0319)
- Radical Governance’s maker-checker / independent-challenge / fallback-review pack, plus DelayBasin’s dual-control instinct, point to one remaining missing cut in Cargo Report Kit: **manual-review carry should not silently count as independent review when requester, reviewer, and executor overlap**.
- Cargo Report Kit is now strict about authority path, projection, quarantine, currentness, compatibility, waivers, contradiction packets, lineage, request/decision split truth, and head-scoped rereview, but it still needed one compact answer to whether stronger carry cleared an independent checker, a weaker same-team second look, or only a compensating fallback control.
- Keep **Cargo Report Kit** responsible for review-separation receipts, maker-checker classes, compensating-control expiry, and self-review hold posture; keep downstream build/perf/debug consumers above that seam rather than letting them smuggle independent approval out of one generic `reviewer` field.

## Frontier adjustment (rev0318)
- pyCausalWeave’s expected-head guard, Goldenrule’s lineage-head register, and the review/frozen-surface discipline in VHK and EvidenceVault all point to one remaining missing cut in Cargo Report Kit: **manual review should be head-scoped, and stale review must fail closed into rereview/reissue rather than silently carrying onto a newer head**.
- Cargo Report Kit is now strict about authority path, projection, quarantine, currentness, compatibility, waivers, contradiction packets, lineage, fixtures, and manual-review split truth, but it still needed one compact answer to *which exact head was reviewed* and *what happens when the lineage tip moves before or after that review*.
- Keep **Cargo Report Kit** responsible for expected-head refs, head-lineage refs, reviewed-head refs, stale-head warnings, and rereview gates; keep downstream build/perf/debug consumers above that seam rather than letting them smuggle “latest head” approval out of old review artifacts.

## Frontier adjustment (rev0317)
- pyCausalWeave’s review-request split truth, VHK’s release/review packs, EvidenceVault’s queue-to-frozen-public discipline, and Radical Governance’s control-surface style all point to one remaining missing cut in Cargo Report Kit: **manual review must stay split into request truth and decision-witness truth**.
- Cargo Report Kit is now strict about authority path, projection, quarantine, currentness, compatibility, waivers, contradiction packets, lineage, and fixture hygiene, but it still needed one compact answer to what stronger carry is actually being requested, what basis is being consulted, what the safe sentence would be without approval, and what decision witness cleared or refused that carry.
- Keep **Cargo Report Kit** responsible for `cargo-report-review-request/v0` and `cargo-report-decision-witness/v0`; keep downstream build/perf/debug consumers above that seam rather than letting them smuggle review approval into artifact status labels.

## Current frontier correction (rev0317)
### Cargo Report Kit also needs contradiction/arbitration discipline
The archive has already made Cargo Report Kit strict about basis, scope, projection, quarantine, currentness, compatibility, waivers, fixtures, and lineage.
One still-missing cut remained after rev0314: **what happens when two same-scope report artifacts compete**.

Why promote that seam now:
- DelayBasin’s contradiction-packet / arbitration-witness work shows that once several candidates remain eligible, honest continuation means preserving the tie set, the allowed precedence rule, and the abstention/fallback path rather than narrating one winner as if the route were self-evident.
- Goldenrule’s lineage-head register sharpens the same point for retained artifacts: operational heads can branch, and a citation/frozen head may be blocked by ambiguity rather than merely absent.
- EvidenceVault and VHK add the operational discipline: unresolved disagreement belongs in an explicit hold/review state, not in folder motion, folder recency, or operator memory.
- Cargo’s new report surfaces now make this a real Rust seam because stable replayed reports, nightly session imports, copied native bytes, copied renderings, and derived summaries can all coexist around one build/debug story.

Strategic conclusion:
The worthy contribution is still a thin `cargo reportpack` / `cargo-report-pack/v0` layer, but it now also needs a **`cargo-report-contradiction-packet/v0` + arbitration/hold posture** so same-scope competing artifacts can remain explicitly unresolved and stronger current/public/frozen-head reuse can fail closed until native-basis precedence or manual review settles them.

## Emerging execution seam inside the Testing Contract Stack and beneath Firmware Productization: Device Lab Kit (board/probe/fixture truth + lab capability/quarantine truth + run-plan truth + native capture imports + normalized on-device reports + bounded handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- `probe-rs` now presents itself as a flexible embedded toolkit that can run programs, decode RTT/`defmt`, support many targets and probes, and even power “awesome HITL setups”.
- `embedded-test` now documents a libtest-compatible host/target runner for ARM, RISC-V, and Xtensa, with per-test reset, async support, and explicit `probe-rs-tools` installation.
- The January 2026 `probe-rs` release moved `probe-rs`, `cargo embed`, and `cargo flash` binaries into the separate `probe-rs-tools` crate, which makes the case for durable metadata/evidence above one install layout stronger rather than weaker.
- The `defmt` book now says `defmt-test` belongs to the deprecated `probe-run` path and points users to `embedded-test`; `defmt` itself is now 1.0.
- Espressif’s `esp-hal` beta says hardware-in-the-loop testing was crucial for stabilization, while the Embedonomicon’s SoC-support guide still lists flash algorithms, examples, templates, and debugging/testing guidance as essential target-enablement work.
- Archived quickstart/runner repos (`cortex-m-quickstart`, `probe-run`, standalone `cargo-embed`) show the workflow surface is evolving, not settled around one permanent UX.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another board template, runner wrapper, or hosted board-farm scheduler. It is the portable physical-target boundary above current tools and below testing/firmware/support/release consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/embedded-device-labs-and-hardware-in-the-loop.md`, `design/device-lab-kit.md`, `design/device-lab-lane-map.md`, `design/device-lab-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-device-lab-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Harness Protocol Kit** responsible for runner capability/discovery truth.
- Keep **Test Execution Evidence Stack** responsible for generic run-result truth and runner-native recordings.
- Keep **Toolchain Productization Stack** responsible for target/linker/sysroot/stdlib activation.
- Keep **Firmware Productization Stack** responsible for the larger support/release/resource/runtime composition above the device boundary.
- Let **Device Lab Kit** own board/probe/fixture truth, lab capability/quarantine truth, run-plan truth, native capture-import truth, normalized on-device report truth, and bounded downstream handoffs.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/device-lab-pilot-program.md`: single-board smoke/attach first, `embedded-test` per-test-reset lane second, mixed transport/tool lane third, shared-lab/quarantine lane fourth, and firmware-productization import lane fifth.
- The bar is not another embedded platform. The bar is a thin `cargo devicelab` / `lab-pack/v0` layer with an explicit `device-capture-import/v0` boundary that makes Rust hardware evidence reviewable.

## Emerging execution-evidence lane between Test Execution Evidence and Safety-Critical Evidence: Coverage Evidence Kit (criterion truth + execution imports + merge/comparability honesty + diffable gates + bounded safety handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust’s 2026 flagship roadmap explicitly includes **MC/DC coverage support** under **Safety-Critical Rust** and **better test tooling** under **Building blocks**.
- The January 2026 safety-critical adoption writeup says industry participants are organizing around upstream MC/DC support rather than treating it as a forever-external requirement.
- The rustc coverage docs already show a real LLVM instrumentation workflow, but they still expose profiler runtime requirements, doc-test-specific flags, raw profile handling, and report assembly steps that ordinary teams paper over with bespoke CI glue.
- `cargo-llvm-cov` now occupies a serious Cargo-native execution lane with `cargo test` / `cargo run` / `cargo nextest`, proc-macro support, external tests, C/C++ coverage, and optional doctest/branch coverage.
- nextest’s own coverage docs explicitly say doctest coverage must be merged separately, which means execution-import truth and merge provenance are part of the actual ecosystem problem.
- Tarpaulin remains a real contrasting engine family, and its recent changelog now refuses meaningless delta coverage when configuration changes would make comparisons dishonest.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another badge, dashboard, or hosted-service convention. It is the criterion-aware evidence boundary above engines and below testing/safety consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/coverage-evidence-and-ci-review.md`, `design/coverage-evidence-kit.md`, `design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-coverage-evidence-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Test Execution Evidence Stack** responsible for harness/run/runner-native recording truth; let Coverage Evidence import that truth explicitly via `coverage-execution-import/v0` artifacts rather than re-owning it.
- Keep **Config Set Kit** responsible for bounded matrix identity and skipped-space rationale.
- Keep **Safety Evidence Kit** responsible for broader case assembly above imported criterion-aware coverage artifacts.
- Let **Coverage Evidence Kit** own criterion identity, execution imports, merge/comparability truth, diff/gate results, and bounded downstream handoffs.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md`: LLVM default-harness baseline first, nextest + doctest merge second, external/FFI merge third, Tarpaulin contrast fourth, and criterion-aware safety import fifth.
- The bar is not another percentage service. The bar is a thin `cargo cov` / `coverage-pack/v0` layer with explicit `coverage-criterion-profile/v0` and `coverage-execution-import/v0` truth that makes Rust coverage claims reviewable.

## Emerging execution and evidence seam between Toolchain Productization and Safety-Critical Evidence: Sanitizer Battery Kit (lane identity + runtime provenance + execution imports + capability limits + findings/waivers + bounded handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust’s 2026 flagship roadmap explicitly includes **Stabilize MemorySanitizer and ThreadSanitizer Support**, including the infrastructure work needed to provide **precompiled and instrumented standard libraries**. That makes dynamic-analysis provisioning and review a live ecosystem boundary rather than a niche nightly workflow.
- The 2025H2 sanitizer-support goal sharpened the same point earlier: practical sanitizer use should not require users to rebuild std by hand forever.
- The unstable-book sanitizer docs still expose material lane differences around target support, `-Z build-std`, runtime linkage, and cross-language instrumentation.
- `cargo-careful` now occupies a serious middle lane with rebuilt debug-assertion std, extra UB checks, optional sanitizer add-ons, and arbitrary system/C FFI support.
- Miri remains uniquely valuable, but nextest’s Miri integration shows execution mode changes observability: per-test processes can be much faster while losing some shared-process race visibility.
- The retag/codegen goal plus BorrowSanitizer’s 2026 updates show the horizon is widening toward native aliasing instrumentation rather than settling on one existing engine.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another checker, wrapper, or CI matrix helper. It is the portable battery boundary above dynamic-analysis engines and below testing/toolchain/safety consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/sanitizers-and-dynamic-analysis.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-lane-map.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-kit.md`, `design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-sanitizer-battery-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Test Execution Evidence Stack** responsible for harness/run/runner-native recording truth; let Sanitizer Battery import that truth explicitly via execution-import artifacts rather than re-owning it.
- Keep **Sysroot Pack Kit** responsible for rebuilt or precompiled instrumented-stdlib identity and reuse compatibility.
- Keep **Toolchain Productization Stack** responsible for the wider provisioning/activation story around toolchains, linkers, SDKs, sysroots, and runtime families.
- Keep **Safety-Critical Evidence Stack** responsible for broader assurance composition above imported dynamic-analysis packs.
- Let **Sanitizer Battery Kit** own lane identity, runtime provenance, execution imports, capability/blind-spot truth, findings, waivers, and comparability drift.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md`: Miri isolated baseline first, Miri + runner import second, `cargo-careful` native checking lane third, LLVM sanitizer + instrumented stdlib finding lane fourth, mitigation/hardening lane fifth, BorrowSanitizer import sixth, and safety-critical imports last.
- The bar is not another “run all sanitizers” command. The bar is a thin `cargo sanitize` / `sanitize-pack/v0` layer with explicit `sanitize-execution-import/v0` truth that makes Rust dynamic-analysis claims reviewable.

## Current frontier correction (rev0332): Consumer Install Kit needs an explicit lane map and pilot program

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- `cargo install` keeps the source-build lane sharp: packaged `Cargo.lock` is ignored unless `--locked` is passed, install-root tracking metadata is created by default, and `--no-track` disables that metadata plus Cargo’s concurrent-install protection.
- Cargo packaging docs now explicitly say `--exclude-lockfile` is not for general use because some tools may expect a lockfile, including `cargo install --locked`.
- `cargo-binstall` now makes the prebuilt lane materially explicit: it searches linked repository releases, falls back to quickinstall, tries alternate targets, and finally falls back to `cargo install`; its signing docs keep signature support separate rather than ambient.
- cargo-dist’s mirror-aware hosting fallback makes candidate order and selected host first-class install facts instead of hidden deployment detail.
- `taiki-e/install-action` now exposes manifest-managed installs, `cargo-binstall` fallback, `cargo install` fallback, checksum/signature verification, and prerequisite package-manager setup, which is direct evidence that CI install is its own lane.
- `axoupdater` already consumes cargo-dist install receipts to reason about installed applications, which proves durable install receipts are real but still too tool-local to solve the ecosystem boundary.
- crates.io’s Trusted Publishing / `pubtime` work sharpens producer truth without answering what any given machine actually installed.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another installer wrapper, package-manager shim, bootstrap script, or “fast install” dashboard. It is the portable first-install boundary above current tools and below lifecycle continuity.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/consumer-install-selection-channels-mirrors-and-receipts.md`, `design/consumer-install-kit.md`, `design/consumer-install-lane-map.md`, `design/consumer-install-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-consumer-install-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Release Truth Stack** responsible for what producers published and attached.
- Keep **Signed Binaries Kit** responsible for downloadable-artifact issuer and verification evidence.
- Keep **Airgap Kit** responsible for mirror topology and restricted-network validation posture.
- Keep **Distribution Contract Stack** responsible for composition/handoff above the leaf install event instead of re-owning the leaf install itself.
- Keep **Update Continuity Kit** responsible for compare/plan/apply/rollback/uninstall continuity after the first install event.
- Let **Consumer Install Kit** own install subject identity, visible candidates, install policy/plan, install receipts, and managed-content claims.
- Let `design/consumer-install-lane-map.md` preserve the distinct lanes: source-build installs, prebuilt installs, mirror/host fallback, CI-wrapper installs, delegated/imported installs, and receipt/managed-content claims.
- Let `design/consumer-install-pilot-program.md` drive the ranked rollout: `cargo install` source-build first, prebuilt second, mirror/host-order third, CI-wrapper fourth, delegated/imported fifth, and update/support handoff sixth.
- The bar is not another installer. The bar is a thin `cargo installproof` / `install-pack/v0` layer whose lane profiles, candidate catalogs, plan/receipt reports, and bounded handoffs make Rust first-install claims reviewable.

## Emerging lower-level anchor between Distribution Contract and productization stacks: Update Continuity Kit (current installed subject + candidate/comparator truth + plan/apply/rollback truth + ownership/uninstall truth + bounded handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- `cargo install` already exposes lifecycle-bearing state rather than just one-shot acquisition: reinstall triggers, default lockfile ignoring, system/user-level config discovery, and install-root tracking metadata are all part of the contract.
- The Cargo 1.86 development-cycle report explicitly highlighted `cargo install-update` and said built-in support is tracked in `#4101`, which is unusually direct evidence that installed-tool updating is still outside Cargo proper.
- `cargo-binstall` is explicit about fallback across release artifacts, quickinstall, and `cargo install`, and its docs point to `cargo-update` for updating all tools. That means update continuity is already a composition problem across tools.
- cargo-dist and `axoupdater` make release-hosted updater flows real, while the February 2026 cargo-dist release added mirrors and explicitly reduced risk around interrupted partial installation.
- Tauri’s updater docs now expose dynamic/static update sources, downgrade control, target matching, permissions, and Windows pre-exit behavior, which means app-update semantics are already a product boundary in real Rust software.
- `update-kit` and `self-replace` show the same lifecycle decomposition emerging in library form: Detection -> Check -> Plan -> Apply, plus platform-sharp self-replace and self-uninstall behavior.
- Cargo 1.90’s path-ownership work says Rustup should only remove content it manages, which makes uninstall scope and cleanup ownership first-class lifecycle truth.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another self-update helper, package-manager wrapper, release-feed parser, or “up to date” badge. It is the portable continuity boundary above install receipts and below broader CLI/client/extension/operator/support consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/update-continuity-detection-selection-apply-rollback-and-uninstall.md`, `design/update-continuity-kit.md`, `design/update-continuity-lane-map.md`, `design/update-continuity-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-update-continuity-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Distribution Contract Stack** responsible for one acquisition/install event: visible candidates, selection, verification, fallback, and install receipt.
- Keep **Release Truth Stack** responsible for producer-side releases, updater material, signatures, and mirrors.
- Keep **Migration Truth Stack** responsible for source/API/config migration rather than installed-machine mutation.
- Keep **Support Envelope** and **Policy** responsible for their downstream claims and decisions.
- Let **Update Continuity Kit** own current installed-subject truth, candidate/comparator truth, chosen plan truth, apply/rollback/uninstall result truth, ownership scope, and bounded downstream handoff.
- The next credible move is now split across a lane rule plus a ranked rollout: `design/update-continuity-lane-map.md` should keep source-installed tool, prebuilt reinstall, receipt-driven updater, bundle/app updater, delegated-manager, ownership/uninstall, and forensic/imported lanes distinct; `design/update-continuity-pilot-program.md` should rank source-installed tool first, prebuilt reinstall second, receipt-driven updater third, bundle/app updater fourth, delegated-manager + ownership fifth, and forensic/support handoff sixth.
- The bar is not another updater engine. The bar is a thin `cargo update-continuity` / `update-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust update claims reviewable.

## Emerging execution-evidence lane inside the Testing Contract Stack: FuzzPack Kit (target profile + corpus/crash/regression truth + shrink/minimization + execution envelope + replay/diff handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- The Rust Fuzz Book still says `cargo-fuzz` is the recommended tool for fuzz testing Rust code, but it is explicit that `cargo-fuzz` is a tool to invoke a fuzzer and currently supports `libFuzzer`. That is strong evidence of adoption, but also strong evidence that the ecosystem still lacks a portable review contract above one engine lane.
- `cargo-fuzz` already has substantial side surfaces — workspace posture, `fmt`, `tmin`, `cmin`, `coverage`, feature forwarding, and `--cfg fuzzing` assumptions — which means real fuzzing truth already spills across target shape, build posture, minimization, and campaign evidence.
- `honggfuzz` remains a real alternative lane with conditional-compilation and persistent-fuzzing posture.
- `proptest` already has persisted-failure machinery and `std` versus `no_std` differences, which means Rust’s durable generative-regression truth is broader than byte corpora.
- `test-fuzz` shows another important bridge: ordinary `cargo test` executions can become corpus-generation input for later fuzzing.
- `LibAFL` shows the backend/runtime horizon is widening: structured inputs, multi-core/multi-machine scaling, binary-only ASan-in-Rust, QEMU/snapshot lanes, and other execution-envelope differences are now part of the problem.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another engine wrapper, trophy-case site, or fuzzing dashboard. It is the portable replay/review boundary above Rust fuzz engines and generative-testing lanes.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/fuzzing-orchestration.md`, `design/fuzzpack-kit.md`, and `proposals/epic-fuzzpack-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Harness Protocol Kit** responsible for capability/discovery/adapter truth.
- Keep **Test Run Evidence Kit** responsible for generic run/result/flake truth.
- Keep **Config Set Kit** responsible for bounded matrix ids.
- Keep **Coverage Evidence Kit** and **Replay Kit** as imported specialized evidence families.
- Let **FuzzPack Kit** own fuzz target profile truth, corpus/crash/regression imports, shrink/minimization truth, execution-envelope facts, and replay/diff handoff.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/fuzzpack-kit.md`: `cargo-fuzz` replay lane first, campaign-report lane second, `proptest`/`test-fuzz` regression-import lane third, richer-engine import lane fourth, and bounded testing-stack handoff fifth.
- The bar is not a new universal fuzzer. The bar is a thin `cargo fuzzpack` / `fuzz-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust fuzz evidence reviewable.

## Emerging lower-level anchor between Manifest Truth, Publisher & Source Identity, and Release Truth: Publish Set Kit (publish subject + payload + checks/waivers + authority path + upload/index receipt + handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Cargo’s unstable-feature docs now say **multi-package publishing** was stabilized in Rust 1.90.0, which means source publication is no longer only a single-package edge case.
- `cargo publish` and `cargo package` now both document workspace-aware default-members/package selection, `--workspace`, `--exclude`, registry selection, and registry-aware packaging for inter-dependent crates.
- `cargo package` now has an unstable machine-readable `--message-format json` for `--list`, which is exactly the kind of substrate that says packaged payload truth is becoming machine-visible but still lacks a full publish contract.
- Cargo manifest/workspace docs make `publish`, `include`, `exclude`, and inherited `workspace.package` fields part of the real publication surface.
- The 2025H2 cargo-semver-checks goal explicitly aims at integrating SemVer compliance into the `cargo publish` workflow.
- crates.io’s January 2026 update added GitLab Trusted Publishing, Trusted-Publishing-only mode, blocked risky triggers, and `pubtime`, which means publish authority and publish-time/index visibility are materially richer than they used to be.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another release bot, registry dashboard, or publisher badge. It is the portable review boundary above Cargo packaging/upload and below broader release/install consumers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/publish-sets-package-selection-contents-verification-and-registry-receipts.md`, `design/publish-set-kit.md`, `design/publish-set-lane-map.md`, `design/publish-set-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-publish-set-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Manifest Truth Stack** responsible for authored-manifest truth, packaged-manifest diffs, discovery/context, and consumer-import lossiness.
- Keep **Publisher & Source Identity Stack** responsible for project-family claims, authority/source posture, and trust/policy imports.
- Keep **Release Pipeline Kit** responsible for the broader release subject including binaries, installers, signatures, provenance, and SBOM attachments.
- Keep **Package Admission Stack** responsible for intake-side graph/exposure/trust/policy review.
- Let **Publish Set Kit** own selected publish subject truth, packaged payload truth, check/waiver truth, authority-path and registry-path truth, upload/index receipt truth, later registry/index observation such as `pubtime`, and bounded downstream handoff.
- The next credible move is now split across a lane rule plus a ranked rollout: `design/publish-set-lane-map.md` should keep single-package, workspace-set, alternative-registry, trusted-publishing, check/waiver, receipt/`pubtime`, and forensic lanes distinct; `design/publish-set-pilot-program.md` should rank single-package crates.io first, workspace publish-set second, alternative-registry/auth-required third, trusted-publishing fourth, check/waiver fifth, receipt/`pubtime` sixth, and release/package-admission handoff seventh.
- The bar is not another publish workflow wrapper. The bar is a thin `cargo publish-set` / `publish-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust source-package publication claims reviewable.

## Emerging lower-level anchor beneath Repo Composition, ScriptKit, and Workspace Environment: Discovery Boundary Kit (invocation subject + manifest walk + workspace attachment/opt-out + config-discovery provenance + consumer-import truth)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Cargo’s own docs already show discovery as a multi-layer story: config is probed through the current directory, parent directories, and `$CARGO_HOME`; workspace roots are found by upward search unless `package.workspace` overrides it; and command defaults still depend on cwd-sensitive manifest discovery.
- Cargo’s 1.94 design discussion makes the pain concrete: broken parent `Cargo.toml` or `.cargo/config.toml` files can break unrelated projects, explicit workspace-auto-discovery opt-outs are being discussed, and cargo-script is starting detached.
- The accepted Cargo plumbing goal begins with **Locate project** and **Read the manifests for a workspace**, while the 1.90 development-cycle report says the prototype still had to re-read manifests within commands because Cargo’s APIs were not exposing enough structure cleanly.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another workspace manager, config wrapper, or editor-specific discover hook. It is the portable review boundary above Cargo discovery facts and below later governance/build/environment layers.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/discovery-boundaries-manifest-workspace-config-and-explicit-attachment.md`, `design/discovery-boundary-kit.md`, and `proposals/epic-discovery-boundary-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Workspace Governance Kit** responsible for effective inheritance, ignored/shadowed settings, nested-workspace ambiguity, and package-selection explanation.
- Keep **Repo Composition Stack** responsible for the larger workspace/config/build handoff story.
- Keep **Build Interop Kit** responsible for machine-facing graph/plan/event exports.
- Keep **ScriptKit** responsible for one-file package/frontmatter/workspace posture in script lanes.
- Keep **Workspace Environment Stack** responsible for realized environment truth.
- Let **Discovery Boundary Kit** own invocation subject truth, manifest walk truth, workspace attachment or opt-out truth, config-discovery provenance, and consumer-import lossiness.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/discovery-boundary-kit.md`: plain Cargo workspace/member lane first, explicit cwd/manifest-path lane second, detached/opt-out lane third, script-runner lane fourth, editor/CI wrapper imports fifth, and broader repo/tooling handoff sixth.
- The bar is not another root detector or shell helper. The bar is a thin `cargo discoverbound` / `discovery-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust discovery claims reviewable.

## Emerging execution bridge between Adoption Decision and Workspace Environment: Starter Pack Kit (starter subject + import truth + render-plan/adapter truth + ownership + overlays + freshness)
Rust now has clear signals that recommendation alone is not enough: the vision work says users lack a clear place to get a good “starter set” of crates; the 2025 survey says docs remain canonical while LLM/editor workflows rise; `cargo new` remains intentionally simple even as it inherits workspace fields; `cargo-generate` proves template-driven bootstrapping is real but fragmented; and domain-specific generators like `create-tauri-app`, `dx new`, `cargo lambda new`, and `esp-generate` show the ecosystem already has many bootstrap paths but no reviewable contract above them. Starter Pack Kit should therefore be treated as an execution bridge: not another template engine, framework quickstart wrapper, or repo generator, but a thin `cargo starter` / `starter-pack/v0` layer with explicit `starter-render-plan/v0` and `starter-render-report/v0` artifacts above both direct and delegated renderers.

## Emerging lower-level anchor between Tooling Contract and Release Truth: Artifact Surface Kit (selected subject + final output identity + origin/location truth + sidecar attachment + bounded handoff)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Cargo’s public story around final outputs is getting sharper rather than softer: Cargo now distinguishes final artifacts from intermediate build state, `artifact-dir` is being treated as public API while `build-dir` stays internal, and build-dir-layout changes are actively surfacing which tools depended on accidental path details.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another target-dir scraper, JSON helper, or release-only manifest. It is the portable review boundary above Cargo final outputs.
- The archive already has nearby seams — Build Cache, Build Extension, Tooling Contract, Release Truth, Distribution Contract, Inventory Evidence, Repro Build, Host Package, Native Edge, Firmware Productization — but none of them should absorb **final Cargo-facing output truth** by accident.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/final-artifact-surface-identity-selection-outputs-and-handoffs.md`, `design/artifact-surface-kit.md`, and `proposals/epic-artifact-surface-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Build Cache Kit** responsible for intermediate-state topology, locks, reuse, and retention.
- Keep **Build Extension Kit** responsible for authored/generated-output declaration and Cargo-mediated uplift requests.
- Keep **Release Truth Stack** responsible for producer-side release bundles, signatures, rebuild continuity, and hosted-release handoff.
- Keep **Distribution Contract Stack** responsible for catalog/channel selection and install receipts.
- Let **Artifact Surface Kit** own selected-subject truth, final output identity, origin/path truth, sidecar attachment, and bounded downstream handoff above Cargo’s final-output surface.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/artifact-surface-kit.md`: Cargo-native binary/library lane first, doc+package lane second, uplift lane third, sidecar lane fourth, and downstream handoff lane fifth.
- The bar is not another build wrapper or release page. The bar is a thin `cargo artifacts` / `artifact-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust final-output claims reviewable.

## Emerging seam just below the event/data band: Dataflow Surface Kit (source/sink truth + event-time semantics + state/checkpoint posture + materialization/progress evidence)

**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust now has several serious but non-equivalent dataflow lanes rather than one canonical runtime: DataFusion is explicitly aiming at streaming applications; Arroyo makes event time, watermarks, stateful processing, remote checkpoints, and source/sink contracts first-class; Fluvio + Stateful Dataflows make programmable connectors, SmartModules/Rust+WASM transforms, watermarks, grace periods, and idleness first-class; Timely keeps distributed progress and capture/replay first-class; and RisingWave keeps streaming jobs, shared-source progress, backfill, barriers/checkpoints, materialized views, catalogs, and runtime-inspection surfaces first-class.
- That pluralism is exactly the signal the archive should read as “missing portable boundary above the lanes,” not “pick the winner.” The recurring missing questions are the same across the lanes: what sources and sinks are really supported; what event-time/watermark/window/lateness assumptions are promised; what state/checkpoint/recovery semantics are claimed; what materializations and freshness claims are exposed; what runtime/storage/catalog/object-store/parallelism settings materially shape behavior; and what downstream service/data/search/scientific/support consumers may later import.
- The archive already has nearby seams — Event Surface, Dataset Surface, Runtime Settings, Observability, Data Productization, Search Productization, Scientific Productization, and Workflow Productization — but none of them should absorb **continuous dataflow graph truth** by accident.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat `gaps/streaming-dataflows-sources-time-state-materialization-and-progress-contracts.md`, `design/dataflow-surface-kit.md`, `design/dataflow-surface-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-dataflow-surface-kit.md` as one coupled frontier band.
- Keep **Event Surface Kit** responsible for channel/message/envelope/replay truth.
- Keep **Dataset Surface Kit** responsible for bounded dataset/layout/publication truth.
- Keep **Workflow Productization Stack** responsible for declared jobs/workflows/schedules and durable business-process truth.
- Let **Dataflow Surface Kit** own continuous graph/source/time/state/materialization/progress truth above the engines.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/dataflow-surface-pilot-program.md`: streaming-SQL lane first, programmable stream-platform lane second, custom dataflow/progress lane third, streaming-database lane fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth.
- The bar is not another stream-processing engine, connector hub, SQL dialect, dashboard, or fake “real-time maturity” score. The bar is a thin `cargo dataflowcheck` / `dataflow-pack/v0` layer that makes Rust dataflow support claims reviewable.

# Strategic Frontier

## Current frontier correction (rev0319)
The comparison set still surfaced one missing cut worth importing into **Cargo Report Kit**: **manual-review carry is incomplete until the archive can say whether the stronger reuse cleared an independent checker, a same-team second look, or only a fallback compensating control**. That matters because replayed Cargo reports, nightly build-analysis sessions, projected support bundles, contradiction packets, and frozen/public heads can now retain authority over time; one bare `reviewer` field is not enough to say whether the resulting carry escaped self-approval, relied on time-bounded fallback review, or needs renewed outside challenge before stronger reuse continues.

 (rev0291)

This file is the **evidence-weighted near-term ranking** for where a new Rust contribution could matter most **right now**.

It is **not** the same thing as the broader conceptual map in [`PRIORITIES.md`](./PRIORITIES.md). That broader map still matters. But when choosing where to spend real effort over the next few revisions, we should weight:
- present user pain,
- active upstream motion,
- how well an external contribution can **compose with** that motion instead of duplicating it,
- and whether the result would leave behind durable, reusable evidence instead of another dashboard, wrapper, or one-off script.

## What would count as a worthy or epic contribution?
A serious contribution should usually do **most** of the following:
1. define a reviewable artifact boundary instead of another opaque score, hidden index, or transient CI trick;
2. compose with active Cargo/rustc/crates.io/project-goals work rather than competing with it blindly;
3. turn folklore into importable/diffable evidence;
4. make downstream policy, release, support, or debugging decisions easier;
5. preserve waivers, incompleteness, and uncertainty honestly;
6. be good enough that other tools can build on it.

## Frontier ranking

### 1) Public API Kit
**Why this is the strongest current bet**
- Public API now reads best as the keystone inside a broader **Package Admission Stack**: with **Publish Set Kit** now explicit beneath it, Public API is the layer that turns publish-side package/payload facts plus dependency-control, inventory, trust, and policy facts into a legible public-contract boundary rather than leaving publish review trapped in graph dumps and registry metadata.
- The project-goals pipeline is already pushing on public/private dependencies, better semver checking, and eventual publish-path integration. That means a contribution here can ride real upstream momentum instead of fighting for attention.
- This seam connects maintainer workflow, ecosystem hygiene, SBOM/policy work, downstream packaging, and future relink-oriented build work.
- It is unusually close to becoming a default release boundary rather than “yet another cargo subcommand.”

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/public-api-kit.md`](./design/public-api-kit.md), [`design/public-api-pilot-program.md`](./design/public-api-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-public-api-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-public-api-kit.md) as one execution band.
- Prioritize `api-pack/v0` as the attachable release artifact tying together exported surface, exposure drift, semver reason codes, witness-based compatibility evidence, and MSRV truth.
- Follow a ranked rollout: single-crate release gate first, workspace release groups second, downstream packaging intake third, policy/trust/inventory correlation fourth, and change-impact / relink consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “another API diff.” The bar is “a pack that could plausibly sit on the road to `cargo publish` admission, downstream packaging review, and change-impact analysis.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Library Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Public API is still the strongest single current bet, but the archive has now accumulated enough adjacent evidence to make the next synthesis obvious: Rust still lacks a **crate-as-product boundary** above API diffs, feature/default posture, docs.rs behavior, release provenance, and support language.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain the canonical reference, which means docs/support behavior is part of what library users actually consume, not just post-release decoration.
- Rust’s 2026 flagships explicitly frame supply-chain progress around public/private dependencies, breaking-change detection, and SBOM generation. Those are not isolated tasks; they are ingredients of a more honest library product contract.
- Cargo publishing remains effectively permanent, Cargo now includes `Cargo.lock` in published crates, features on crates.io have a hard cap unless exceptions are granted, docs.rs explicitly treats build metadata and CI replication as real concerns, and crates.io trusted publishing plus owner management make release authority/provenance part of the product boundary. Together, those signals say the missing contribution is not another point fix.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/library-productization-stack.md`](./design/library-productization-stack.md), [`design/library-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/library-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/public-api-kit.md`](./design/public-api-kit.md), [`design/manifest-truth-stack.md`](./design/manifest-truth-stack.md), [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md), and [`design/docproof-kit.md`](./design/docproof-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Public API should keep owning exposed Rust contract, semver reasoning, MSRV verification, and public-dependency exposure. Manifest Truth should keep owning feature/default/package/docs.rs/native posture. Release Truth should keep owning what source package and provenance were actually published. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs/example truth.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in [`design/library-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/library-productization-pilot-program.md): single-crate library release lane first, docs.rs + feature/default posture second, public-dependency + native/build-script posture third, support/release drift fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth.
- The bar is not another semver wrapper, manifest linter, registry dashboard, or release bot. The bar is “a thin linked pack that lets maintainers, downstream users, and tools review a crate release as a **productized library** instead of reconstructing the truth from five partial systems.”

## Emerging seam just below the service/library band: SDK Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- The archive already has strong producer-side lanes in Schema Contract, Service Surface, Protocol Productization, Identity Productization, Runtime Settings, Library Productization, Release Truth, and Support Envelope. What it still lacked was the thin product layer for **Rust SDK families** derived from those sources.
- The 2024 State of Rust survey says Rust is especially popular for server backends, web/networking services, and cloud technologies, which means Rust increasingly sits on both sides of API-product boundaries rather than only on the service side.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain canonical while LLM workflows and agentic editors are rising. That increases the value of bounded, machine-readable SDK product truth instead of leaving support to README prose and generator folklore.
- The ecosystem already has credible point tools across multiple contract families: `utoipa` + `progenitor` for OpenAPI, OpenAPI Generator’s Rust lane for broader schema-driven generation, `tonic`/`tonic-build` for protobuf/gRPC, and `smithy-rs` for large-scale generated SDKs. The missing contribution is not one more generator; it is the shared **SDK product boundary** above them.
- Oxide’s Rust SDK/CLI repo is the strongest practical signal: one OpenAPI source feeds a CLI, SDK, and mocking library; generated code is checked in; CI validates sync; and versioning explicitly accounts for both API incompatibilities and generation incompatibilities. That is exactly the kind of reviewable family truth the archive should model.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/sdk-surface-kit.md`](./design/sdk-surface-kit.md), [`design/sdk-productization-stack.md`](./design/sdk-productization-stack.md), [`design/sdk-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/sdk-productization-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-sdk-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-sdk-productization-stack.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- SDK Surface should own SDK family identity, operation maps, runtime/auth profiles, checked examples, and generated-versus-hand-written ownership boundaries. Schema Contract / Service / Protocol layers should keep owning upstream contract truth. Identity / Runtime Settings should keep owning activation truth. Library Productization / Release Truth / Support Envelope should keep owning shipped-artifact and support truth.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in [`design/sdk-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/sdk-productization-pilot-program.md): OpenAPI SDK family lane first, Smithy/large-SDK lane second, gRPC/protobuf lane third, release/support lane fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth.
- The bar is not another OpenAPI generator fork, HTTP client wrapper, auth helper, or provider-specific SDK starter. The bar is “a thin linked pack that lets maintainers, downstream users, and tools review a Rust SDK family as a **productized API surface** instead of reconstructing truth from specs, generated code, examples, and release notes.”


### 2) Build-State Evidence Stack (Build Cache + Change Impact + Build Doctor)
**Why this is near the top**
- The 2025 State of Rust survey again calls out compile times, storage usage, and debugging/build friction as major pain points.
- Cargo now has live work on build-dir relayout, fine-grained locking, relink-don't-rebuild, structured reporting, and the emerging `build.analysis` / `cargo report` direction. That is exactly when a companion evidence layer is most valuable.
- This cluster can improve local DX, CI economics, remote caching, rust-analyzer coexistence, and future build-system architecture all at once.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/build-state-evidence-stack.md`](./design/build-state-evidence-stack.md), [`design/build-state-evidence-pilot-program.md`](./design/build-state-evidence-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md), [`design/build-cache-kit.md`](./design/build-cache-kit.md), [`design/change-impact-kit.md`](./design/change-impact-kit.md), [`design/build-doctor-kit.md`](./design/build-doctor-kit.md), [`design/cargo-report-kit.md`](./design/cargo-report-kit.md), and the new [`design/cargo-report-pilot-program.md`](./design/cargo-report-pilot-program.md) as one strategic band, not isolated ideas.
- Prefer a shared artifact family around build-state topology, rebuild reasons, relink opportunities, workflow-aware diagnoses, and bounded consumer handoffs; let **Cargo Report Kit** own native report import, authoritative native basis, authority-origin/import-path truth, fallback-order posture, refusal-posture gates, stable-vs-unstable posture, exact session/report scope, observed coverage slices, lane-wide claim-class honesty, observation-epoch truth, currentness classes, recheck-gate discipline, field authorship, local-private-capture versus portable-pack versus shared-surface versus frozen-head posture, share-safe projection receipts, redaction/omission discipline, retained-basis-loss warnings, quarantine-first retained-capture posture, omitted-field non-inference, derived-artifact separation, promotion/head-warning discipline, replay/copy provenance, superseded/stale-head warnings, import-compatibility receipts, decoder/toolchain/schema-drift posture, material-change reimport/re-review gates, **fixture-backed schemas/examples/scenario corpus plus taxonomy-backed class registers, scenario coverage index, machine-readable review gates, machine-checked contract hygiene, expiring waiver ledgers, execution receipts, and derivation lineage registers**, and normalized report-pack truth beneath the broader stack. The proposal-layer candidates are now [`proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md) and [`proposals/epic-cargo-report-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-cargo-report-kit.md).
- The next credible move is now a ranked shared pilot program: editor/CLI coexistence and lock-scope truth first, private-change/relink-opportunity lanes second, workspace/CI exchange lanes third, workflow-aware diagnosis fourth, and broader consumer imports fifth.
- The bar is not “faster builds in one repo.” The bar is “portable evidence that explains *why* Cargo rebuilt, *what* could have been reused, *where* work blocked or duplicated, and *which* diagnoses are actually justified.”

## Emerging seam just below the build/debug band: Feedback Loop Stack (Build-State Evidence + Debuggability + bounded Edit Workflow / Semantic Context imports)
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- The archive already had a high-ranked Build-State Evidence Stack and a mature Debuggability Stack, but the Rust ecosystem still lacks the composition layer for the thing users actually experience: one reviewable iteration from change or intent, through build evidence, into debugger/diagnostic evidence, and out into issue/support/assistant consumers.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey still says resource usage and debugging remain major productivity problems, and the 2025 build-performance survey sharpens that by saying workflow differences matter, more than 35% of users see IDE and Cargo blocking one another as a big problem, and debug info materially affects both disk usage and build/link speed.
- Cargo’s build-analysis goal and the 1.94 development-cycle work make session-shaped build evidence much more real: `CARGO_RUN_ID`, `cargo report rebuild`, `cargo report sessions`, and better timing data now exist as first-party substrates rather than wishful abstractions, while the goal’s own privacy/performance/no-stability posture makes a thin honest import/share/promote layer more valuable than a premature universal schema. The most important new discipline is that the latest imported pack should be allowed to differ from the current frozen shared head whenever authoritative basis, authority path, scope, coverage, projection/redaction receipts, unstable-field warnings, currentness/recheck posture, import-compatibility/drift-review posture, or quarantine posture have not been resolved yet. Just as importantly, one imported session should not silently pose as workspace-wide, all-target, or current-active truth unless the pack carries both an exact coverage slice and an explicit currentness class that justify the stronger sentence; one copied HTML or summary should not silently inherit the authority of native replayable evidence unless an authority-path receipt says the stronger reuse is still safe; one unstable-derived pack should not silently keep its old authority after Cargo/toolchain/importer drift unless a compatibility receipt says the stronger reuse is still safe; and one quarantined-retained import should not silently become search/snippet/summary/public truth merely because later tooling derived a convenient explanation from it. Just as importantly, a shared pack that filtered CLI args, env-derived values, local paths, or raw payload sections should carry a projection receipt rather than pretending the shared artifact is the full native capture, omitted or decoder-unknown fields should not back-form a fake “absent” story, any later summary or normalization should live on a distinct derived artifact rather than semantic back-writing onto the quarantined source, and a later basis-lost artifact should demote rather than quietly retaining replay-grade authority.
- The build-dir-layout goal plus the March 2026 call for testing show that tools still rely on Cargo internals because some missing features force them to. That means a worthy companion layer should make those boundaries explicit rather than hiding them behind fresh folklore.
- The 2026 debugging survey says Rust still lacks strong multi-debugger, multi-OS, async-aware debugging support, which strengthens the case for linking debugger tuple truth to build/debug session identity without pretending parity already exists.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/feedback-loop-stack.md`](./design/feedback-loop-stack.md), [`design/feedback-loop-pilot-program.md`](./design/feedback-loop-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-feedback-loop-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-feedback-loop-stack.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Build-State Evidence should keep owning build-state topology, change/rebuild scope, and diagnosis truth. Debuggability should keep owning diagnostics, runtime-correlation, debugger tuple, and repro/escalation truth. Edit Workflow and Semantic Context should remain bounded imports when they are actually available.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in [`design/feedback-loop-pilot-program.md`](./design/feedback-loop-pilot-program.md): session identity first, editor/CLI coexistence second, build→debug handoff third, issue/support/docs consumers fourth, and assistant/editor consumers fifth.
- The bar is not another IDE platform, local daemon, dashboard, or “AI dev loop.” The bar is “a thin linked pack that lets maintainers, tools, and downstream consumers review one Rust iteration as a **feedback loop** instead of reconstructing it from shell history, target-dir workarounds, debugger setup pages, and issue archaeology.”

### 3) Policy / Trust / SBOM / Lifecycle Stack
**Why this stays elite**
- crates.io is actively adding security-facing surfaces (Security tab, richer trusted-publishing support, better metadata like `pubtime`, more browsing/discovery affordances), while the RustSec/crates.io malicious-crate response path keeps evolving.
- The ecosystem still lacks one explainable decision layer that can import trust, inventory, lifecycle, advisory, support, and signature evidence without flattening it into a fake score.
- The same trust and maintenance signals now also suggest a missing layer above ordinary crate review: a way to describe when a project has become keystone infrastructure and what institutional-support or continuity posture follows from that status.
- This is where a lot of real-world organizational adoption pressure lands.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Keep [`design/policy-kit.md`](./design/policy-kit.md), [`design/policy-pilot-program.md`](./design/policy-pilot-program.md), [`design/trust-signals-kit.md`](./design/trust-signals-kit.md), [`design/trust-signals-pilot-program.md`](./design/trust-signals-pilot-program.md), [`design/trust-decision-stack.md`](./design/trust-decision-stack.md), [`design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md`](./design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-trust-decision-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-trust-decision-stack.md), [`design/sbom-evidence-kit.md`](./design/sbom-evidence-kit.md), and [`design/lifecycle-ledger-kit.md`](./design/lifecycle-ledger-kit.md) tightly coupled.
- `cargo trust` should now be treated explicitly as the evidence layer: signals, import profiles, reports, diffs, and packs. `cargo policy` should remain the explainable decision layer. A thin `cargo trust-decision` / `trust-decision-pack/v0` layer should sit above them as the stack-level continuity boundary, while Typosquat Guard, Lifecycle Ledger, Signed Binaries, and SBOM Evidence remain imported companions rather than competing truth engines.
- The next credible move is now a paired-and-stacked ranked rollout: the **trust pilot program** should start with lockfile trust reports, then build/proc-macro split-scope truth, then publisher/freshness truth, then release attachment, then thin registry/review consumers; the **policy pilot program** should keep owning package-release, workspace-split, cooldown, artifact-release, and migration-review decisions; the **trust-decision pilot program** should coordinate them through lockfile evidence-to-policy continuity, scope-sensitive review, freshness/cooldown posture, package/release attachment, and bounded thin-view consumers.
- The bar is not “a prettier security dashboard,” “another cargo-vet replacement,” or “a registry score.” The bar is “diffable evidence plus rule-driven decisions with visible waivers, honest `INCONCLUSIVE` states, explicit policy handoff, and thin consumer views that do not overclaim.”

## Emerging seam just below the policy/trust band: Dependency Review Stack (Trust Decision + effect audit + Runtime Capability + artifact linkage)
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- crates.io now exposes more intake-time security and publisher posture directly (Security tab, Trusted Publishing expansion, `pubtime`), while the malicious-crate policy is shifting routine incidents toward RustSec advisories as the durable record. That makes local dependency-review artifacts more valuable than ambient announcement noise.
- Cargo Vet is mature enough to serve as a first-class import lane rather than merely inspiration: it is explicitly built to minimize friction, share audits, compute the smallest useful diff, and reduce attack surface as exemptions shrink.
- Cargo Scan’s 2026 paper changes the strategic picture: if most flagged effects can be decided locally, if median human review can fall to 0.2% of lines of code, and if dangerous effects are concentrated in a small part of the ecosystem, then Rust now has a plausible lane for **dangerous-code review** above advisories and publisher signals.
- Alpha-Omega / Rust Foundation work plus `cargo-capslock` mean runtime-authority review is no longer only theoretical for Rust. The missing contribution is now the intake boundary that keeps trust, dangerous-code review, capability review, and built-artifact linkage connected without collapsing them.
- `cargo-auditable` and Cargo-native inventory work make it increasingly practical to tie dependency review to the binaries or release artifacts actually shipped.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/dependency-review-stack.md`](./design/dependency-review-stack.md), [`design/dependency-review-pilot-program.md`](./design/dependency-review-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-dependency-review-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-dependency-review-stack.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Trust Decision should keep owning publisher/advisory/freshness/policy truth. Effect-audit imports should keep owning dangerous-code and caller-checked review truth. Runtime Capability should keep owning declared/inferred/enforced authority truth. Inventory / artifact-linked evidence should keep owning package↔artifact↔binary linkage. Dependency Review should own the **review subject, diff point, bounded handoff, and uncertainty discipline** above them.
- The next credible move is the ranked rollout in [`design/dependency-review-pilot-program.md`](./design/dependency-review-pilot-program.md): lockfile/version-bump review first, effect-attached review second, capability-attached review third, artifact/binary attachment fourth, and PR/release/policy/assistant consumers fifth.
- The bar is not another crate score, security dashboard, universal allowlist, or `cargo vet` replacement. The bar is “a thin linked pack that lets maintainers and downstream consumers review one dependency change as a **dependency review** instead of reconstructing it from advisories, audit imports, research tools, and binary archaeology.”

## Emerging seam just below the policy/trust band: Runtime Capability Kit
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
