- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo release-truth` / `release-truth-pack/v0` composition layer above those leaves, with crate-only publish continuity first, lane-aware rebuild evidence second, signed/inventory attachment discipline third, and inventory/distribution handoff fourth.
- The bar is not “a better release GitHub Action”, “another installer wrapper”, or “one provenance badge”. The bar is “reviewable producer-side release truth that maintainers, mirrors, policy tools, downstream packagers, and skeptical consumers can all consume offline and honestly”, while preserving the boundary where **package reproducibility**, **artifact equivalence**, **provenance/SBOM attachments**, and **consumer-side install truth** remain distinct.
- The next adjacent seams are now the **Publisher & Source Identity Stack**, the **Inventory Evidence Stack**, and the **Distribution Contract Stack**. The first should keep publish-authority and project-family claims distinct from release/install conclusions; the latter two should import release truth later rather than forcing this stack to own inventory continuity or installed-state receipts outright.



### 21) CLI Productization Stack (Command Surface + Terminal Surface + Runtime Settings + Distribution Contract + Support Envelope)
**Why this now deserves frontier treatment**
- Rust’s 2025H2 goals explicitly say the project wants `cargo script` to make single-file Rust utilities, examples, and reproducible bug reports easier, which is a strong signal that tool-shaped workflows are strategic rather than incidental.
- Cargo custom subcommands are already a first-class extension lane with argument forwarding and `cargo help` integration, which means many Rust tools live inside the Cargo UX instead of outside it.
- The ecosystem already has credible point ingredients: `clap` for command graphs and related generators/tests, `clap_complete` and `clap_mangen` for generated surfaces, `cargo-dist` and `cargo-binstall` for acquisition/distribution flows, and `crossterm` / Ratatui / `anstream` for terminal and output semantics.
- The missing contribution is therefore not another parser framework, TUI shell, installer wrapper, or shell-completion helper. The missing contribution is the productization layer for **declared command truth, live terminal truth, runtime-setting activation, install/lifecycle truth, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/cli-productization-stack.md`](./design/cli-productization-stack.md), [`design/cli-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/cli-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/command-surface-kit.md`](./design/command-surface-kit.md), [`design/terminal-surface-kit.md`](./design/terminal-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Command Surface should keep owning parse/help/completion/manpage and cargo-subcommand identity truth. Terminal Surface should keep owning raw-mode/color/layout/input/render truth. Runtime Settings should keep owning config/env/cache/profile activation. Consumer Install should keep owning acquisition/install receipts, while Update Continuity should keep owning post-install mutation receipts. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs truth.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: classic CLI lane first, cargo-subcommand lane second, machine-readable operator-tool lane third, terminal/TUI lane fourth, and install/update/support consumer lane fifth.
- The bar is not another parser crate, TUI framework, self-update utility, or release-script bundle. The bar is portable CLI-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, and atlas/documentation consumers can import honestly.


### 22) Cryptography Productization Stack (Cryptography Surface + Randomness Surface + Runtime Settings + Support Envelope)
**Why this now deserves frontier treatment**
- Rust’s cryptography story is now product-shaped enough that kit-level thinking is no longer sufficient. `rustls` has a first-class `CryptoProvider` seam with built-in `aws-lc-rs` and optional `ring` lanes, process-default installation, explicit custom-provider support, and documented HSM-style key-loading examples. That makes provider choice and key-source posture public product facts rather than hidden implementation details.
- The compliance story is also explicit now. `rustls` documents a concrete FIPS flow: enable the `fips` feature, use the FIPS provider, and validate FIPS status at run time. `aws-lc-rs` documents materially different build requirements for FIPS versus non-FIPS builds and tells consumers tied to older validated modules to pin crate versions deliberately. That is strong evidence that “supports FIPS” is not one checkbox.
- The trait and secret-handling side of the ecosystem is equally telling. RustCrypto’s `crypto` facade exists because independently versioned trait crates still need compatibility coordination; `secrecy`, `zeroize`, and `subtle` already expose different wrapper/wipe/constant-time caveats; `signature::Error` is intentionally opaque for side-channel reasons while still allowing source errors for remote signers; and `password-hash` gives Rust a real PHC-string interchange lane.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain the canonical reference even as machine-assisted/editor-driven workflows rise. That makes it more important that crypto-facing support claims become importable artifacts instead of security-team lore and README archaeology.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/cryptography-productization-stack.md`](./design/cryptography-productization-stack.md), [`design/cryptography-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/cryptography-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/cryptography-surface-kit.md`](./design/cryptography-surface-kit.md), [`design/randomness-surface-kit.md`](./design/randomness-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Cryptography Surface should keep owning algorithm/provider/key-material/side-channel/compliance truth. Randomness Surface should keep owning entropy/seed/repro posture. Runtime Settings should keep owning provider selection, feature activation, key-source selection, and runtime validation truth. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs/runtime-floor truth. Protocol, Identity, Service, and Policy consumers should remain importing lanes rather than becoming new crypto authorities.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: `rustls` provider/runtime-validation lane first, `aws-lc-rs` FIPS/non-FIPS build-and-pin lane second, RustCrypto generic-traits lane third, secret-handling/side-channel lane fourth, and downstream protocol/identity/service/support consumers fifth.
- The bar is not another primitive crate, provider wrapper, compliance badge, or universal crypto facade. The bar is portable cryptography-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, policy, and downstream product surfaces can import honestly.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Tooling Contract Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s 2026 roadmap explicitly puts **integrate Cargo into larger build systems** and **prototype cargo plumbing commands** inside the active “Building blocks” flagship. That is a strong signal that machine-facing Cargo contracts are now strategic infrastructure rather than niche tooling convenience.
- Cargo’s accepted plumbing goal is unusually direct about the gap: current plumbing commands are limited or deprecated, `cargo metadata` excludes feature resolution, `--build-plan` was not durable, and the build can be decomposed into phases from project discovery through final-artifact staging.
- Cargo’s stable external-tools docs still offer only three broad lanes — `cargo metadata`, `--message-format=json`, and subcommands — while warning that JSON build messages cannot control arbitrary tool output. That means the ecosystem still lacks a coherent discovery→plan→execute contract story.
- Cargo’s build-cache docs now separate final artifacts in `target-dir` from internal intermediates in `build-dir` and explicitly say the build-dir layout is internal. The March 2026 build-dir-v2 testing call makes the missing seam even clearer: many projects still rely on unspecified layout details because the right Cargo features do not yet exist.
- Cargo’s build-analysis/report direction is promising, but it is still opt-in and experimental. That makes a stack that can import report evidence honestly more timely than pretending the report logs are already the whole public interface. It also means any shared example built on top of those logs should carry explicit basis, instability, and import-compatibility warnings rather than silently turning into an implied public contract.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/tooling-contract-stack.md`](./design/tooling-contract-stack.md), [`design/tooling-contract-pilot-program.md`](./design/tooling-contract-pilot-program.md), [`design/repo-composition-stack.md`](./design/repo-composition-stack.md), [`design/build-interop-kit.md`](./design/build-interop-kit.md), [`design/build-state-evidence-stack.md`](./design/build-state-evidence-stack.md), and [`proposals/epic-tooling-contract-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-tooling-contract-stack.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Repo Composition should keep owning discovery roots, config layers, inheritance/effective settings, package selection, and bounded execution matrices. Build Interop should keep owning machine-facing workspace graphs, build plans, execution events, and adapter posture. Build-State Evidence should keep owning rebuild reasons, cache/reuse/block truth, and diagnostic imports. The proposal layer should stay thin: a `cargo tooling-contract` / `tooling-contract-pack/v0` composition boundary above those artifacts, not a replacement for them.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: discovery/package-selection lane first, graph/plan lane second, build-analysis/report-import lane third, IDE/outer-build consumer lane fourth, and release/docs/support handoff lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a Cargo daemon”, “a BSP-only bridge”, “a target-dir scraper that knows more folklore”, or “a universal monorepo platform”. The bar is “portable machine-facing contract truth that lets Cargo-heavy tools share one story about discovery, selection, planning, execution, stability posture, adapter lossiness, and rebuild evidence without freezing all of Cargo’s internals.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Manifest Truth Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Cargo 1.94 makes it clear that manifest behavior is still a strategic seam rather than a solved file-format detail: TOML 1.1 compatibility, `cargo-cargofmt`, workspace/config discovery, and now the stabilized config `include` key all sit close to the authored-manifest boundary.
- `cargo package` already rewrites and normalizes the published manifest, removes sections like `[patch]`, `[replace]`, and `[workspace]`, and includes `Cargo.lock` by default. That means repo truth and packaged truth already diverge in meaningful ways.
- RFC 3416 plus the continuing Cargo work on visibility/deprecation show that feature metadata is moving toward a more explicit consumer-facing surface.
- cargo-script/frontmatter means manifests can now live in `.rs` files with explicit defaulting and restrictions, widening the seam beyond package-root `Cargo.toml` files.
- Cargo’s external-tools guidance makes `cargo metadata` the stable machine-readable import lane, while `cargo info` is a useful human-facing projection. That still leaves authored-vs-packaged diffs, frontmatter/defaulting truth, and consumer-lossiness without a shared review substrate.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/manifest-surface-kit.md`](./design/manifest-surface-kit.md), [`design/manifest-truth-stack.md`](./design/manifest-truth-stack.md), [`design/manifest-truth-pilot-program.md`](./design/manifest-truth-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-manifest-truth-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-manifest-truth-stack.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Manifest Surface Kit should own `manifest-subject/v0`, authored/defaulted manifest reports, publish-diff reports, feature catalogs, consumer-import reports, and `manifest-pack/v0`.
- Repo Composition should keep owning discovery/config/workspace-selection truth; ScriptKit should keep owning script execution posture; Dependency Control should keep owning activated graph truth; Publisher & Source Identity should keep owning issuer-backed source/auth/publish-authority truth.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo manifest-truth` / `manifest-pack/v0` layer above those leaves, with the ranked pilot program proving ordinary package lane first, packaged publish-diff lane second, workspace/discovery lane third, script/frontmatter lane fourth, and multi-consumer import lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a better formatter”, “a smarter manifest editor”, or “a prettier registry page”. The bar is “portable manifest truth that keeps what was written, what Cargo inferred, what got packaged, what consumers imported, and what downstream tools concluded as distinct but composable facts.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Publisher & Source Identity Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- crates.io now has stronger publish-authority signals: Trusted Publishing support has expanded to GitLab CI/CD and crate owners can require Trusted Publishing only, which means publisher identity is increasingly issuer-backed rather than just token-backed.
- Cargo’s registry-authentication and registries/source-replacement docs now make a subtle but important distinction explicit: authenticated alternate registries, ordinary crates.io usage, and exact-copy source replacement are different identity/configuration stories, not interchangeable knobs.
- RFC 3243 and the active open-namespaces goal mean namespace control is now a live cross-team implementation seam rather than a permanently hypothetical debate.
- The archive already had an Org Identity & Registry UX kit, but it was still too easy to read as “tap-like UX plus maybe namespaces”. The stronger seam is a stack that keeps **claim truth + publish-authority truth + source identity + downstream trust/admission/install handoffs** distinct while the ecosystem continues debating final namespace/governance details.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/publisher-source-identity-stack.md`](./design/publisher-source-identity-stack.md), [`design/publisher-source-identity-pilot-program.md`](./design/publisher-source-identity-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-publisher-source-identity-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-publisher-source-identity-stack.md), [`design/org-identity-registry-ux-kit.md`](./design/org-identity-registry-ux-kit.md), [`design/trust-signals-kit.md`](./design/trust-signals-kit.md), [`design/package-admission-stack.md`](./design/package-admission-stack.md), and [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Org Identity & Registry UX should keep owning project-family claims, publish-authority reports, and source/auth posture. Trust Signals should keep importing those facts without turning them into verdicts. Package Admission should keep importing them for registry-facing publish review. Distribution Contract should keep importing selected fields for install receipts and support/incident consumers.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo source` / `cargo publisher` / `publisher-source-pack/v0` layer above those leaves, with source/auth posture first, publisher-authority posture second, claim/namespace posture third, package-admission handoff fourth, and install/support consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “finally pick the namespace answer”, “a prettier cargo config editor”, or “an org verification badge”. The bar is “portable publisher/source identity truth that lets Cargo, registries, CI, policy, and install consumers describe the same situation without telling five incompatible stories.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Distribution Contract Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust now has credible producer-side release tooling and a newly explicit leaf install boundary, but still lacks one portable **stack-level** consumer distribution layer. `cargo install`, `cargo-binstall`, cargo-dist mirrors/installers, CI fallback wrappers, and restricted-network lanes can now all produce or import install truth, but the ecosystem still needs the composition layer above them.
- crates.io’s current momentum is still mostly publish-side: Trusted Publishing controls, blocked risky triggers, Security-tab advisory views, and `pubtime` all improve release/package truth. Those are valuable, but they still do not replace the need to compose leaf install receipts with producer-side evidence.
- The archive now has the missing leaf acquisition ingredient — **Consumer Install Kit** — plus Release Pipeline, Signed Binaries, Airgap, and Support Envelope. The remaining missing seam is the stack-level execution layer that composes those truths without erasing where each one came from.
- This seam is strategically strong because it speaks to developers, CI, enterprise mirrors, security teams, and support consumers at once without requiring one monopoly installer or registry.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md), [`design/distribution-contract-pilot-program.md`](./design/distribution-contract-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-distribution-contract-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-distribution-contract-stack.md), [`design/consumer-install-kit.md`](./design/consumer-install-kit.md), [`design/release-pipeline-kit.md`](./design/release-pipeline-kit.md), [`design/signed-binaries-kit.md`](./design/signed-binaries-kit.md), and [`design/airgap-kit.md`](./design/airgap-kit.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Consumer Install should keep owning install subjects, visible candidates, install policy/plan, receipts, and managed-content claims. Signed Binaries should keep owning artifact signature/provenance truth. Airgap should keep owning restricted-network topology and validation posture. Release Pipeline should keep owning producer-side release truth. Distribution Contract should own stack-level diffs and downstream support/incident/policy handoffs above those imported layers.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo distribution-contract` / `distribution-contract-pack/v0` composition layer above those leaves, with leaf install-pack import first, mirror/host fallback truth second, package-manager/imported-lane truth third, restricted-network imports fourth, and support/incident/archaeology handoffs fifth.
- The bar is not “a nicer installer script”, “a host-page scraper”, or “one cargo command to rule them all”. The bar is “portable distribution composition that can explain how leaf install truth, producer evidence, mirror posture, and downstream conclusions fit together without telling four incompatible stories.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Conformance Traceability Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s specification work is now operational enough that the missing problem is no longer “should Rust have a spec?” but “how do spec text, executable vectors, compiler-lane reality, and assurance consumers stay connected without being flattened?” RFC 3355, the rust-lang-owned FLS, the upkeep goal, and the Reference-expansion goal all point in that direction.
- The 2026 roadmap makes Safety-Critical Rust concrete through MC/DC, normative `unsafe` documentation, safety-critical Clippy work, and FLS release cadence. That means conformance artifacts now have obvious downstream consumers instead of being just language-team nice-to-haves.
- The experimental-language-specification goal makes another missing seam explicit: Rust wants a way to publish stability-marked, team-reviewed language text before or alongside stabilization. That makes it much more important to keep stable text, experimental text, conformance vectors, and acceptance diffs distinct but connected.
- The archive already had the ingredients — Spec Conformance Kit, Acceptance Surface Kit, and Safety-Critical Evidence Stack — but they still read too much like adjacent ideas instead of one traceability band.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/conformance-traceability-stack.md`](./design/conformance-traceability-stack.md), [`design/conformance-traceability-pilot-program.md`](./design/conformance-traceability-pilot-program.md), [`design/spec-conformance-pilot-program.md`](./design/spec-conformance-pilot-program.md), [`design/spec-conformance-kit.md`](./design/spec-conformance-kit.md), [`design/acceptance-surface-kit.md`](./design/acceptance-surface-kit.md), [`design/safety-critical-evidence-stack.md`](./design/safety-critical-evidence-stack.md), and [`proposals/epic-conformance-traceability-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-conformance-traceability-stack.md) as one execution band.
- `cargo conform` should keep owning spec references, vectors, capability declarations, and conformance reports. Acceptance Surface should keep owning compiler-lane and workaround truth. Safety-Critical Evidence should keep owning criterion-aware coverage, runtime-checking, proof, and safety-case imports rather than trying to define language semantics itself. The new stack-level proposal should own the reviewable handoff across those lanes, not replace them.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: stable paragraph-linked core profile first, experimental-language-spec traces second, acceptance diffs third, unsafe-contract / safety-doc consumers fourth, and release / qualification / archaeology packaging fifth.
- The bar is not “a compiletest replacement”, “one universal qualification binder”, or “an official Rust compiler badge”. The bar is “portable traceability that lets teams move from text to vectors to results to assurance conclusions without losing honesty about scope, authority, or incompleteness.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Migration Truth Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s official migration story is now unusually explicit about being staged, configuration-sensitive, and partly manual at the edges. That means the ecosystem pain is no longer “someone should automate upgrades more”; it is “there is still no shared migration-program truth”.
- Rust 1.85 / Rust 2024 says `cargo fix` output is conservative and should not be treated as a recommendation, while Cargo’s 1.90 work and the 2025 GSoC results show the edit engine itself is still evolving. That makes a higher-level orchestration/evidence layer much more leveraged than a new fixer wrapper.
- Toolchain/MSRV policy, public/private dependency exposure, semver checking, docs.rs rustdoc JSON, and support-envelope/report work now give migrations real downstream consumers. That makes migration packs more plausible as ecosystem infrastructure instead of just maintainers keeping tidier notes.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/migration-truth-stack.md`](./design/migration-truth-stack.md), [`design/migration-pilot-program.md`](./design/migration-pilot-program.md), [`design/migration-kit.md`](./design/migration-kit.md), [`proposals/epic-migration-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-migration-kit.md), and the new stack-level proposal [`proposals/epic-migration-truth-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-migration-truth-stack.md) as one execution band, with Edit Workflow, Public API, Support Envelope, DocProof, and Downstream Testing as imported consumers rather than collapsed subfields.
- `cargo migrate` should own source-state truth, destination-intent truth, imported analysis reports, ordered plans, run receipts, outcome claims, and waivers. It should not try to absorb edit mechanics, semver reasoning, support claims, docs validation, or downstream runs into one mega-schema.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: edition transition truth first, workspace toolchain / `rust-version` truth second, dependency-upgrade + API/MSRV truth third, docs/support/downstream truth fourth, and release / archaeology / migration-diff truth fifth.
- The bar is not “another upgrade bot”, “a friendlier `cargo fix` wrapper”, or “one giant migration dashboard”. The bar is “portable change-program truth that lets maintainers, reviewers, release managers, policy tools, and future maintainers understand what transition was attempted, what actually happened, and what final claim is now supported.” The stack-level proposal candidate is now [`proposals/epic-migration-truth-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-migration-truth-stack.md): a thin `cargo migrate` / `migration-pack/v0` layer above Migration Kit + imported edit/API/support/docs/downstream evidence.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Harness Protocol Kit
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The strongest new testing signal is no longer just “machine-readable test output” but **a harness/runner protocol question**. The libtest JSON RFC explicitly anticipates Cargo UX built on programmatic output and a future Cargo RFC so custom harnesses can opt into that protocol, which is a stronger ecosystem signal than “please add another output mode”.
- The Testing Team exists because Rust’s testing experience is split across Cargo, libtest, rustdoc doctests, CI, IDEs, and external frameworks. That means the missing contribution is plausibly a contract between those actors rather than one more point tool.
- Cargo’s own `bench` docs already admit the stable story is hybrid: `cargo bench` can drive custom harnesses while `#[bench]` remains unstable/nightly-only. That makes benchmark interop part of the same missing substrate.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/testing-harness-protocols-and-benchmark-interop.md`](./gaps/testing-harness-protocols-and-benchmark-interop.md), [`design/harness-protocol-kit.md`](./design/harness-protocol-kit.md), [`design/harness-protocol-pilot-program.md`](./design/harness-protocol-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-harness-protocol-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-harness-protocol-kit.md) as one execution band.
- `cargo harness` should own capability declarations, subject discovery, suite/case hierarchy, benchmark/doctest posture, and runner-adapter compatibility reports. It should not try to absorb final run results, CI matrix truth, performance comparison policy, or docs-host validation.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: libtest capability/discovery lane first, nextest adapter lane second, custom-framework opt-in lane third, benchmark lane fourth, and doctest/IDE lane fifth.
- The bar is not “another test runner”, “a benchmark framework replacement”, or “a hidden libtest schema”. The bar is “a portable harness contract that runners, IDEs, CI, docs, and benchmark tools can actually build on honestly.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Test Execution Evidence Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s 2026 flagships explicitly list **better test tooling** under **Building blocks**, so the archive should now treat test-execution evidence as roadmap-visible infrastructure work rather than only a local devex annoyance.
- The 2025H2 libtest-JSON goal says libtest is Cargo’s default harness, says users rely on programmatic output, and points toward reporting shifting into Cargo to enable better summaries, parallel execution, and lower-friction custom runners/harnesses.
- Cargo 1.94 still lists finishing libtest JSON as a live area needing owners, which means the seam is still open.
- `cargo test` still mixes libtest, rustdoc doctests, and `harness = false` custom flows, while warning that doctest execution details are not guaranteed and may change. The rustc tests book still says libtest JSON is unstable, and benchmarks/custom test frameworks remain unstable or experimental. So Rust still needs an honest boundary between portable run truth and unstable/native testing detail.
- nextest already behaves like infrastructure rather than just a nicer CLI: machine-readable test and binary lists, JUnit output, experimental libtest-like JSON, debugger/tracer hooks, and persistent run recordings with full event streams, captured outputs, and workspace metadata are all real. At the same time, nextest’s own docs still list first-class JSON test-run output and detected configuration as future work.
- Cargo’s external-tools docs still say `--message-format=json` only covers Cargo and rustc output, not arbitrary tool output. That means there is still no single raw JSON stream the ecosystem can safely pretend is “the” canonical test contract.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/harness-protocol-kit.md`](./design/harness-protocol-kit.md), [`design/test-run-evidence-kit.md`](./design/test-run-evidence-kit.md), [`design/test-execution-evidence-stack.md`](./design/test-execution-evidence-stack.md), [`design/test-execution-pilot-program.md`](./design/test-execution-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-test-execution-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-test-execution-evidence-stack.md) as one execution band, with Harness Protocol as the upstream contract layer and Test Run Evidence as the execution/results/import layer.
- `cargo testrun` should own run profiles, result reports, imported runner-native recordings, flake observations, and attachment indexes. `cargo harness` should own capability/discovery/adapter truth. `cargo configset` should continue to own bounded matrix ids and rationale. Coverage/fuzz/replay/downstream/device consumers should remain distinct evidence families that attach to the shared run subject instead of redefining it.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: harness capability/discovery first, default-harness/nextest run truth second, config-bound CI third, coverage + Miri attachments fourth, replay/fuzz/mutation consumers fifth, and downstream/device-lab consumers sixth.
- The bar is not “yet another test runner”, not “one more JUnit bridge”, and not “just standardize nextest’s native store”. The bar is “portable execution truth that lets Rust testing compose across runners and specialized assurance lanes without flattening their differences.” The stack-level proposal candidate is now [`proposals/epic-test-execution-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-test-execution-evidence-stack.md): a thin `cargo test-execution` / `test-exec-pack/v0` layer above Harness Protocol + Test Run Evidence + Config Set + attachment consumers.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Repo Composition Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Cargo’s current signals are converging on one operational truth: repo behavior depends on **discovery roots**, **workspace governance**, and **package/config selection**, not just on dependency graphs. Cargo 1.94 explicitly called out workspace/config discovery as a live design problem, while Cargo’s configuration docs remain clear that parent directories and `$CARGO_HOME` participate in config probing.
- Workspace semantics are carrying more governance load than they used to: `default-members`, `package.workspace`, inherited workspace fields, workspace lints, and tool-facing `workspace.metadata` all affect what people think “the repo” means.
- `cargo metadata` now includes `workspace_default_members`, which means package-selection truth is increasingly machine-visible. That makes a companion stack more practical now than when the archive first added Workspace Governance and Config Set as separate Tier 1 ideas.
- Cargo 1.93’s revived `-Zconfig-include` work is a quiet but important signal: config layering is getting more expressive, so explanation and diffing matter more, not less.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/repo-composition-discovery-package-selection-and-scope-continuity.md`](./gaps/repo-composition-discovery-package-selection-and-scope-continuity.md), [`design/repo-composition-stack.md`](./design/repo-composition-stack.md), [`design/repo-composition-pilot-program.md`](./design/repo-composition-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md) as the synthesis/rollout/product layer above [`design/workspace-governance-kit.md`](./design/workspace-governance-kit.md), [`design/config-set-kit.md`](./design/config-set-kit.md), and [`design/build-interop-kit.md`](./design/build-interop-kit.md).
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot posture: workspace-truth lane first → discovery-boundary lane second → package-selection/`config-set` handoff third → tool/export consumers fourth → cross-tool mixed-build lane fifth.
- The proposal-layer candidate is now [`proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md): a thin `cargo repo-compose` / `repo-compose-pack/v0` layer that records discovered roots, config layers, selected scope, and consumer lossiness rather than becoming a monorepo manager or repo-manifest standard.
- The bar is not “another monorepo manager”, “a universal repo manifest”, or “more CI YAML generation”. The bar is “reviewable repo-composition truth that Cargo users, CI, editors, docs lanes, release lanes, and adjacent tools can all consume honestly.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Airgap Kit / restricted-network truth
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The Rust project already has an accepted goal for **cryptographic verification and mirroring** of crates.io and Rust releases, explicitly motivated by restrictive firewalls, unreliable internet, CI infrastructure, and supply-chain assurance. That means secure mirroring is not fringe ops folklore; it is recognized project terrain.
- Cargo’s source-replacement model is unusually explicit: replacement sources are for mirrors or exact local copies, and Cargo assumes the source code is **exactly the same** across original and replacement sources. That makes mirror topology and exact-copy posture reviewable seams rather than background configuration noise.
- Cargo’s own consumer-side behavior remains sharp-edged enough that the ecosystem still lacks one honest restricted-network contract. `cargo install` is system/user-level, starts config discovery at `$CARGO_HOME`, and by default ignores the packaged `Cargo.lock` unless `--locked` is passed. So “workspace builds offline” and “bootstrap developer tools offline” are not the same lane.
- rustup already supports mirror roots for toolchain distribution, which means toolchain mirroring is real and distinct from Cargo registry mirroring. crates.io’s new Trusted Publishing Only Mode strengthens the publish side, but it does not solve install/mirror/offline-validation truth on the consumer side.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/airgap-kit.md`](./design/airgap-kit.md), [`design/airgap-pilot-program.md`](./design/airgap-pilot-program.md), [`gaps/airgapped-bootstrapping-and-offline-profiles.md`](./gaps/airgapped-bootstrapping-and-offline-profiles.md), and [`proposals/epic-airgap-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-airgap-kit.md) as one execution band.
- `cargo airgap` should keep owning `airgap-profile/v0`, `airgap-lock/v0`, `airgap-check-report/v0`, and `airgap-pack/v0`. The new pilot layer should add plane maps, mirror-topology profiles, install profiles, and scorecards so restricted-network claims stop hiding behind shell scripts and warmed caches.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: workspace dependency/build lane first, developer-tool install lane second, rustup/toolchain mirror lane third, secure mirror verification lane fourth, and integrated restricted-network CI/bootstrap lane fifth.
- The bar is not “another mirror server,” “a better offline flag,” or “one more bootstrap script generator.” The bar is “portable restricted-network truth that keeps dependency builds, developer-tool installs, toolchain distribution, and verification posture distinct while still making them composable.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: ScriptKit / script truth
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- cargo-script is no longer just a neat side experiment; it is a 2026 flagship, and Cargo’s unstable docs now define concrete single-file package behavior including embedded frontmatter, defaulted fields, manifest-command invocation, a hashed target dir under `$CARGO_HOME`, and a lockfile in `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`.
- The Rust project is explicitly presenting scripts as useful for **minimal bug reproducers** and quick prototypes. That means one-file packages are not merely a convenience lane; they are becoming a public-facing support and debugging surface.
- Cargo 1.94 makes the discovery story sharper by saying cargo-script starts with workspace auto-discovery disabled while broader workspace/config discovery remains an active design problem. That creates a real need for explicit standalone-vs-repo-governed lane truth.
- The remaining gaps are mostly about import discipline, not basic possibility: rustdoc frontmatter handling still mattered as a blocker in late 2025, rust-analyzer shebang support is still open, and Cargo still has active script-output UX questions.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/scriptkit.md`](./design/scriptkit.md), [`design/scriptkit-pilot-program.md`](./design/scriptkit-pilot-program.md), [`gaps/single-file-scripts-first-class.md`](./gaps/single-file-scripts-first-class.md), and [`proposals/epic-scriptkit.md`](./proposals/epic-scriptkit.md) as one execution band.
- `cargo scriptkit` should own `script-subject/v0`, `script-frontmatter-report/v0`, `script-lane-profile/v0`, `script-run-report/v0`, `script-consumer-import-report/v0`, and `script-pack/v0` boundaries. Compile-time, build-state, repo-composition, and policy consumers should import those artifacts rather than re-infer script semantics from temp directories or shell folklore.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: minimal bug repro lane first, shebang utility lane second, in-repo automation lane third, editor/CI import lane fourth, and workspace opt-in/policy lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a better cargo-script wrapper,” “a Rust shell-script brand campaign,” or “publish scripts somewhere.” The bar is “portable one-file package truth that humans and tools can share without guessing.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Dependency Control Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Cargo is increasingly making dependency selection policy reviewable rather than purely implicit: public/private dependencies remain an active Rust flagship milestone, publish-time-aware resolution is landing in Cargo, and workspace feature-unification controls now exist as an unstable configuration seam.
- Resolution and feature posture are still too easy to discuss separately even though many real maintainer problems cross the boundary: “why this version?”, “why is this feature on?”, “why did workspace selection change this build?”, and “is this dependency boundary actually public?” are often the same incident.
- Public API, semver, policy, build-state, and semantic-context tooling all need dependency truth, but the archive still lacked the shared execution layer that says how chosen-graph evidence and feature evidence become one reusable substrate.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/dependency-control-stack.md`](./design/dependency-control-stack.md), [`design/dependency-control-pilot-program.md`](./design/dependency-control-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-dependency-control-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-dependency-control-stack.md), [`design/resolution-doctor-kit.md`](./design/resolution-doctor-kit.md), [`design/feature-kit.md`](./design/feature-kit.md), [`proposals/epic-resolution-doctor-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-resolution-doctor-kit.md), and [`proposals/epic-feature-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-feature-kit.md) as one execution band.
- Resolution Doctor should keep owning chosen packages / versions / sources / conflicts / overrides / reproducers. Feature Kit should keep owning activation why-chains, unification-sensitive deltas, minimization posture, and feature policy. Public/private dependency and API-boundary questions should remain an explicit handoff to Public API / Policy consumers.
- The next credible move is now a ranked stack rollout: single-crate graph + feature-why truth first, workspace unification / split-selection second, public/private boundary handoff third, MSRV / publish-time / upgrade lanes fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth. The stack now also has a proposal-layer candidate in [`proposals/epic-dependency-control-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-dependency-control-stack.md): a thin `cargo dependency-control` / `dependency-control-pack/v0` layer above Resolution Doctor + Feature Kit + boundary imports rather than a replacement resolver or one-score dependency platform.
- The next adjacent seams are now the **Resolution Strategy Stack** and the **Inventory Evidence Stack**. The former should stay above Dependency Control as the objective/tradeoff layer instead of being silently absorbed into resolver facts or policy verdicts; the latter should keep package, release, and install inventory continuity explicit instead of letting SBOM/export/recovery/install facts blur together.
- The bar is not “another dependency dashboard”, “a smarter cargo tree skin”, or “a universal minimal-feature solver”. The bar is “portable dependency-control evidence that lets Rust tools share chosen-graph truth and activated-capability truth without re-deriving them or collapsing them into one score.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Resolution Strategy Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Cargo now has enough live resolution-policy motion that “the lockfile is the explanation” is no longer credible. MSRV-aware resolution is mainstream, minimal-version checks still matter, publish-time-aware resolution is emerging, and PubGrub work is explicitly meant to unlock richer extension space rather than remain a private implementation detail.
- The archive already has a strong **Dependency Control Stack**, but that stack mostly answers **what Cargo chose**. The missing execution layer is **why this objective was being pursued at all**, which alternatives mattered, and what tradeoffs were accepted for a specific consumer.
- This seam composes unusually well with migration, release admission, semver, support, and policy work because all of them increasingly need to know whether a graph was chosen for “latest”, “MSRV-safe”, “direct-minimal validation”, “publish-time replay”, or another declared strategy.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/resolution-strategy-kit.md`](./design/resolution-strategy-kit.md), [`design/resolution-strategy-stack.md`](./design/resolution-strategy-stack.md), [`design/resolution-strategy-pilot-program.md`](./design/resolution-strategy-pilot-program.md), [`design/dependency-control-stack.md`](./design/dependency-control-stack.md), [`proposals/epic-resolution-strategy-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-resolution-strategy-kit.md), and [`proposals/epic-resolution-strategy-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-resolution-strategy-stack.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Dependency Control should keep owning chosen graph / feature / conflict truth. Resolution Strategy should own named objective profiles, accepted tradeoffs, bounded candidate comparisons, and consumer handoff posture above those facts.
- The next credible move is a ranked rollout: direct-minimal validation first, workspace multi-MSRV strategy second, publish-time / release-age exploration third, release-admission / semver handoff fourth, and migration / support / policy consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “a solver fork,” “a dependency score,” or “a smarter lockfile diff.” The bar is “portable strategy truth that explains what kind of resolution was intended and why this outcome was acceptable.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Firmware Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The 2024 State of Rust survey says embedded use is gaining traction while documentation remains a canonical strength and debugging/resource friction remain live pain. That is exactly the pattern where a productization layer starts to matter: real deployment pressure with still-fragile operational truth.
- `embedded-hal` 1.0 now states its scope clearly: it is a foundation for platform-agnostic drivers, it is not tied to one execution model, and functionality beyond that scope should use the target platform directly. That is a strong reason to stop looking for the missing contribution inside the trait layer.
- The host↔device workflow substrate is now real: `probe-rs` spans flashing/debugging/RTT/`defmt`, `embedded-test` provides a libtest-compatible on-device runner, and Embassy plus RTIC/vendor SDK lanes mean execution-model diversity is here to stay. The missing contribution is therefore the contract/evidence layer above them.
- Espressif's `esp-hal` reached a 1.0 release in October 2025 as the first vendor-backed Rust SDK for embedded devices, after treating HIL work as crucial in the beta path. That is a maturity signal that the ecosystem is ready for boring support/release evidence above point frameworks.
- The build-std goal remains active and the Embedonomicon's SoC-support guide is explicit about docs, register descriptions, flash algorithms, and enablement work. Meanwhile the 2024 embedded-systems study found inadequate support, poorly organized docs, and tool/build diversity challenges. The right answer looks like a stack note and ranked rollout, not another monolith.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/firmware-productization-stack.md`](./design/firmware-productization-stack.md), [`design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md), [`design/device-lab-kit.md`](./design/device-lab-kit.md), [`design/cross-toolchain-kit.md`](./design/cross-toolchain-kit.md), [`design/sysroot-pack-kit.md`](./design/sysroot-pack-kit.md), [`design/footprint-kit.md`](./design/footprint-kit.md), and [`design/observability-kit.md`](./design/observability-kit.md) as one execution band.
- Device Lab should keep owning board/probe/flash/reset/run truth. Toolchain Productization should keep owning target/linker/sysroot/stdlib activation truth. Footprint should keep owning RAM/FLASH/stack/layout budgets. Observability should keep owning logging/fault/runtime evidence. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs/runtime-floor truth. Release Truth should keep owning shipped-image/imported-artifact truth.
- The next credible move is now split into an explicit proposal layer plus the ranked rollout: [`proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md) should own the stack-level contract, while [`design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md) should still run device profile/run reports first, target/sysroot activation second, footprint/layout evidence third, runtime/fault evidence fourth, and support/release/incident consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “one embedded framework”, “one better flasher”, “a universal board template”, or “a lab daemon”. The bar is “portable firmware product truth that keeps device, toolchain, footprint, runtime, and support evidence separate while making them composable.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Robot Description Surface Kit
**Why this now deserves explicit frontier treatment**
- Rust robotics now has enough serious but non-equivalent lanes that the missing contribution looks like a **portable robot-description boundary**, not another framework. ROS 2 rolling now explicitly lists Rust client-library work, FOSDEM 2026 material around `rclrs` says Rust support is now part of ROS 2 rolling, and the runtime lanes are already plural: `rclrs`, `r2r`, Zenoh-backed ROS 2, and pure-Rust-over-Zenoh experiments all solve real but different problems.
- The model/inspection side is also real: `urdf-rs`, `robot-description-builder`, `k`, `urdf-viz`, Rerun URDF/frame tooling, and replay/import lanes like `rosbags-rs`/MCAP mean the ecosystem already has authored-description, kinematics, runtime, replay, and visualization ingredients.
- What is still missing is one honest answer to: “what robot is this, what frames/joints/assets/limits does it declare, and what runtime/replay evidence actually matched it later?” Today those truths are scattered across URDF files, builder code, runtime graphs, MCAP logs, and viewer assumptions.
- That is exactly the pattern where a thin evidence contract matters more than another parser, simulator, or robotics mega-stack.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/robot-description-surface-kit.md`](./design/robot-description-surface-kit.md), [`design/robot-description-pilot-program.md`](./design/robot-description-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-robot-description-surface-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-robot-description-surface-kit.md) as one execution band.
- The first credible layer is a thin `cargo robotdesc` / `robot-description-pack/v0` boundary exporting subject identity, structure, frame topology, geometry/assets, limits/calibration posture, reviewable diffs, and explicit runtime/replay attachments.
- Keep ownership honest: authored robot-description truth should stay separate from runtime activation truth, replay/import truth, and downstream support/product claims.
- The bar is not “a Rust robotics platform.” The bar is portable robot-description evidence that parsers, builders, kinematics libraries, ROS runtime lanes, replay tools, and viewers can import without pretending they are all the same layer.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Scientific Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s current scientific/offloading goal explicitly says scientific computing, HPC, and ML users expect efficient libraries, high-performance autodifferentiation, and easier GPU use, while naming a future with state-of-the-art libraries like `faer` and more Rust backends. That means the ecosystem problem is broader than any one tensor crate or accelerator wrapper.
- The October 2025 program-management update still explicitly lists **GPU support for scientific computing and machine learning** as active work. That means backend and device truth are not speculative extras; they are part of the moving frontier.
- The current ingredients are real but heterogeneous: Burn is both tensor library and deep-learning framework; Candle exposes explicit tensor/device/storage structures; `faer` covers pure-Rust linear algebra with portability/correctness/performance goals; `zarrs` covers multidimensional-array storage; `ort` covers hardware-accelerated ONNX inference/training; and `tract` covers tiny portable TensorFlow/ONNX inference. That is exactly the pattern the archive should read as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The outside lesson is also already written down elsewhere: the 2024 Array API release explicitly frames interoperability and consistent semantics as the point, while DLPack exists as a stable in-memory tensor exchange format across frameworks and hardware. That makes it easier to justify an evidence/contract layer than another runtime-winner pitch.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/scientific-productization-stack.md`](./design/scientific-productization-stack.md), [`design/scientific-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/scientific-productization-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-scientific-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-scientific-productization-stack.md), [`design/tensor-surface-kit.md`](./design/tensor-surface-kit.md), [`design/dataset-surface-kit.md`](./design/dataset-surface-kit.md), [`design/offload-surface-kit.md`](./design/offload-surface-kit.md), and [`design/model-surface-kit.md`](./design/model-surface-kit.md) as one execution band.
- Tensor Surface should keep owning in-memory array/matrix/tensor shape/layout/view/device/autodiff/interchange truth. Dataset Surface should keep owning persisted/chunked/table/array storage truth. Offload Surface should keep owning backend/device/kernel/dispatch truth. Model Surface should keep owning artifact/tokenizer/runtime truth. Runtime Settings and Support Envelope should keep owning activation and promise boundaries.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo science-product` / `scientific-product-pack/v0` layer above **Tensor Surface + Dataset Surface + Offload Surface + Model Surface + Runtime Settings + Support Envelope**, with the ranked rollout still starting from CPU array/storage truth, then interchange, then backend/offload activation, then model attachments, then support/release/atlas/service/agent consumers.
- The bar is not “a Rust NumPy clone,” “a JAX/PyTorch replacement,” “a giant universal array trait,” or “one magical GPU stack.” The bar is “portable scientific product truth that lets real Rust numerical/data/ML systems compose honestly.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Model Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s model-facing ecosystem is now clearly heterogeneous: Candle is a native Rust transformers/runtime lane with Hub-cache and safetensors integration; Burn is a multi-backend training/inference framework with new storage and ONNX-import capabilities; `ort` is a hardware-accelerated ONNX Runtime attachment; `tract` is intentionally tiny and self-contained for ONNX/TensorFlow inference. That is the exact pattern where the missing contribution is above the runtimes, not inside them.
- Tokenizer and artifact truth are now real first-class concerns. `tokenizers` models tokenization as an explicit pipeline and can load pretrained tokenizers from the Hub. safetensors emphasizes safe zero-copy tensor storage, while GGUF adds standardized metadata and quantized variants visible on the Hub. Hub model cards now carry structured task, library, lineage, license, and evaluation metadata.
- The missing contribution is therefore not another inference runtime, weight-format winner, hub wrapper, or model-serving control plane. It is the productization layer for **model/artifact/tokenizer truth, backend/device/precision posture, acquisition/cache/auth activation, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/model-productization-stack.md`](./design/model-productization-stack.md), [`design/model-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/model-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/model-surface-kit.md`](./design/model-surface-kit.md), [`design/offload-surface-kit.md`](./design/offload-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Model Surface should keep owning model/task/tokenizer/artifact/runtime truth. Offload Surface should keep owning backend/device/precision/quantization posture. Runtime Settings should keep owning source/revision/cache/auth/backend activation. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs truth.
- The next credible move is a ranked rollout: local CPU + artifact/tokenizer lane first, hub-backed acquisition/cache lane second, runtime-diversity comparison lane third, quantized/GGUF variant lane fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth.
- The bar is not another model runner, format converter, or “serve any model” platform. The bar is portable model-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, and higher-level Rust products can import honestly.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Agent Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says official online docs remain the canonical reference while some learning traffic appears to be shifting toward LLM tooling and agentic/editor workflows. That means Rust now needs more machine-usable, supportable workflow truth — not just more wrappers around model APIs.
- MCP has matured into a real product surface: its current architecture and specification distinguish lifecycle and transport concerns and treat resources, prompts, and tools as first-class server features. `rmcp` is now the official Rust SDK for that surface, which means Rust has a real protocol substrate rather than only ad hoc JSON-RPC glue.
- The Rust-side framework landscape is already heterogeneous: `rig` emphasizes ergonomic modular LLM apps and includes eval support, while `swiftide` spans indexing/query pipelines and agents that can use tools and call other agents but also warns that it remains under heavy development. That is exactly the pattern the archive should care about: strong ingredients, weak shared boundary.
- Provider APIs are also becoming more operationally explicit. OpenAI documents structured outputs, agent evals, and trace grading as first-class features; Anthropic distinguishes client tools from server tools, documents fine-grained tool streaming, and warns that multiple execution environments do not share state. That means the missing support boundary spans prompts, tools, runtime capabilities, permissions, traces, and docs — not one crate.
- The missing contribution is not another agent framework, MCP helper, or provider wrapper. The missing contribution is the portable productization layer for **supported workflows, model/retrieval attachments, runtime/identity activation, eval/trace evidence, support/docs truth, and migration evidence**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/agent-productization-stack.md`](./design/agent-productization-stack.md), [`design/agent-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/agent-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/agent-surface-kit.md`](./design/agent-surface-kit.md), and the explicit proposal-layer candidate [`proposals/epic-agent-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-agent-productization-stack.md) as one execution band.
- Agent Surface should remain the anchor for workflows, prompts, tools, resources, and session posture. Model Surface and Retrieval Surface should remain lower-layer attachment lanes. Runtime Settings and Identity Surface should remain activation/permission lanes. Observability plus eval/diagnostic consumers should remain runtime-evidence lanes. Support Envelope, DocProof, and Canonical Learning should remain support/docs/import lanes.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo agent-product` / `agent-product-pack/v0` layer above those imports, rolled out in ranked order — workflow/prompt/tool/resource truth first, model/retrieval attachments second, runtime/identity/permission activation third, eval/trace evidence fourth, and support/docs plus migration/release/support/policy consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “a Rust LangChain”, “one true MCP runtime”, or “a better chat wrapper”. The bar is “portable boring agent-productization evidence that keeps workflow truth, lower-layer attachments, runtime activation, eval/trace evidence, migration evidence, and support truth separate while making them composable.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Local-First Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The local-first essay still provides a sharp product standard: software should work offline, collaborate across devices, and improve ownership, privacy, long-term preservation, and user control. That means the real bar is product behavior, not merely “has a CRDT somewhere.”
- Rust already has serious but heterogeneous ingredients: Automerge presents itself as a local-first sync engine, while the Automerge Repo docs explicitly say the CRDT + sync protocol still leave application plumbing around storage and networking; the storage docs are equally explicit that without a `StorageAdapter`, repo state is transient across restart.
- Yrs already separates document updates from `Awareness` session state and treats y-sync as a cross-system communication basis, while Loro explicitly frames itself as a local-first CRDT framework with undo/redo, time travel, and Rust/JS/WASM/Swift APIs; Loro 1.0 now says its data format is stable. That is exactly the pattern the archive should read as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The typed-state lane is also now real: `autosurgeon` and `lorosurgeon` both provide Rust-type ↔ CRDT-container mapping layers, which is a strong signal that product-state and migration/review concerns are escaping raw engine APIs.
- The missing contribution is not another CRDT engine, relay, or full-stack local-first framework. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **document/sync/history/storage truth, client-vs-relay boundary truth, runtime/identity activation, recovery/export/migration truth, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/local-first-productization-stack.md`](./design/local-first-productization-stack.md), [`design/local-first-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/local-first-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/replica-surface-kit.md`](./design/replica-surface-kit.md), [`design/client-app-surface-kit.md`](./design/client-app-surface-kit.md), [`design/event-surface-kit.md`](./design/event-surface-kit.md), [`design/service-surface-kit.md`](./design/service-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/identity-surface-kit.md`](./design/identity-surface-kit.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Replica Surface should keep owning collaborative document, sync, history, and storage truth. Client App plus Event/Service should keep owning client-vs-relay/runtime-role truth. Runtime Settings should keep owning activation/storage/endpoint posture. Identity should keep owning peer/principal/access posture. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning what is actually promised.
- The next credible move is now explicit: pair the ranked rollout with [`proposals/epic-local-first-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-local-first-productization-stack.md), a thin `cargo local-product` / `local-first-product-pack/v0` layer above Replica Surface + Client App Surface + Event/Service Surface + Runtime Settings + Identity Surface + Support Envelope. The rollout remains single-device durable truth first, multi-replica sync and presence truth second, relay/auth/runtime activation third, recovery/export/migration truth fourth, and support/release/atlas consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “a better CRDT”, “a universal sync backend”, or “a Rust Firebase replacement”. The bar is portable local-first-productization evidence that keeps replica, client/relay, runtime, recovery, and support truths separate while making them composable.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Interactive Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust now clearly has real interactive building blocks instead of one fragile dream: Bevy positions itself as a cross-platform engine/app framework with scenes, hot reload, and multi-platform support; `wgpu` is a serious cross-platform rendering substrate; rust-gpu already makes Rust-authored shader crates their own build lane; and Slint/egui/winit expose materially different but real UI/runtime/event-loop truths.
- Bevy’s recent releases make the productization gap sharper, not softer: web assets over `http`/`https`, optional asset caching, built-in frame-time graphs, tooling widgets, and richer rendering modes all say that content source posture, runtime evidence, and downgrade/fallback behavior are already product concerns.
- `wgpu` and Naga now make backend/shader reality impossible to hide: supported backends, adapter/environment selection, WGSL translation paths, and browser-vs-native differences all affect what an interactive Rust product can honestly claim to support.
- The missing contribution is not another engine, renderer wrapper, or asset helper. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **asset/media truth, shader/backend/device truth, window/input/frame-loop truth, runtime/perf activation, and shipped/support truth**.
