- Media Surface should keep owning content/source/runtime posture. Offload Surface should keep owning shader/backend/device/fallback truth. Client App Surface should keep owning window/input/frame/update behavior. Runtime Settings and Observability should keep owning activation/perf evidence. Distribution and Support should keep owning shipped-artifact and support truth.
- The next credible move is now explicit: pair the ranked rollout with [`proposals/epic-interactive-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-interactive-productization-stack.md), a thin `cargo interactive-product` / `interactive-product-pack/v0` layer above Media Surface + Offload Surface + Client App Surface + Runtime Settings + Observability + Distribution Contract + Support Envelope. The rollout remains asset/source truth first, shader/backend profile second, frame/input/update truth third, runtime/perf activation fourth, and support/release/customer-handoff consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “a better game engine”, “one renderer wrapper to rule them all”, or “a prettier benchmark dashboard”. The bar is portable interactive-productization evidence that keeps content, render, runtime, and support truths separate while making them composable.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Media Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s media ecosystem is now clearly heterogeneous in a way that rewards a higher-level contract: `image` is no longer just “decode/encode common formats,” but also a richer metadata/color/runtime-hook surface; Symphonia is explicit about pure-Rust demux/decode support with default-vs-feature-gated format/codec posture; `ffmpeg-next` stays valuable while also being explicit about native-version compatibility and maintenance mode; GStreamer Rust already targets applications and plugins; and `video-rs` proves there is active appetite for a more Rusty media API even on top of FFmpeg/libav.
- Runtime and evidence concerns are now part of the actual media story, not adjacent concerns. `tracing-gstreamer` explicitly bridges GStreamer logging/tracing into Rust `tracing`, while modern `image` releases keep surfacing metadata/color/profile behavior that changes what “supports this format” really means.
- `rav1e` and `mp4parse` sharpen the boundary further from opposite directions: Rust media products may be encode-heavy, inspect-only, decode-only, transcode-oriented, playback-oriented, or mixed. That is exactly the pattern the archive should read as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The missing contribution is not another codec wrapper, pipeline DSL, or FFmpeg-replacement fantasy. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **operation/format/processing truth, native/plugin/backend activation truth, service/client attachment truth, observability/fidelity evidence, and shipped/support truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/media-productization-stack.md`](./design/media-productization-stack.md), [`design/media-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/media-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/media-surface-kit.md`](./design/media-surface-kit.md), [`design/service-surface-kit.md`](./design/service-surface-kit.md), [`design/client-app-surface-kit.md`](./design/client-app-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/offload-surface-kit.md`](./design/offload-surface-kit.md), [`design/observability-kit.md`](./design/observability-kit.md), [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Media Surface should keep owning operation/format/metadata/processing truth. Service Surface should keep owning ingest/transcode/export/stream API truth. Client App Surface should keep owning playback/capture/package/device truth. Runtime Settings and Offload Surface should keep owning native/plugin/backend/device activation truth. Observability should keep owning runtime/fidelity evidence. Distribution and Support should keep owning shipped-artifact and support truth.
- The next credible move is a ranked rollout: pure-Rust image/audio truth first, FFmpeg/GStreamer runtime truth second, service attachment truth third, client playback/capture truth fourth, and observability/release/support/downstream imports fifth.
- The bar is not “a better transcoder wrapper”, “one universal media matrix”, or “a Rust replacement for every media stack”. The bar is portable media-productization evidence that keeps media semantics, runtime activation, app/API attachments, observability, and support truths separate while making them composable.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Polyglot Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The Rust project is explicitly saying mixed-language adoption is real and durable: the safety-critical writeup says teams will integrate Rust into existing C/C++ systems and carry that boundary for years, while the 2025H2 interop problem-map goal says the near-term reality is cooperation with large C++ estates rather than rewrites.
- The current host-language lanes are already serious but fragmented: PyO3 recommends `maturin` for Python packaging, but the Python lane itself now visibly splits into full-API extension, `abi3` limited-API, free-threaded, and `asyncio`-bridge sublanes; UniFFI already spans Swift/Kotlin/Python and is explicit about thread-safety and lifetime restrictions; Node-API offers a stable C ABI but documents strong caveats outside that boundary; CXX documents both Cargo-native and foreign-build-system handoff; and the Wasm Component Model now explicitly frames standardized multi-language interoperability as the point. That is exactly the pattern the archive should treat as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The missing contribution is not another lane-specific generator. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **crate-vs-foreign-package identity, generated-binding provenance, runtime/init/threading/lifetime truth, shipped/install truth, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/mixed-language-product-boundaries-crate-package-binding-runtime-and-support-truth.md`](./gaps/mixed-language-product-boundaries-crate-package-binding-runtime-and-support-truth.md), [`design/polyglot-productization-stack.md`](./design/polyglot-productization-stack.md), [`design/polyglot-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/polyglot-productization-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-polyglot-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-polyglot-productization-stack.md) as the synthesis/proposal layer for the seam.
- Treat [`design/host-package-kit.md`](./design/host-package-kit.md) as the foreign-package/runtime/install anchor, [`design/ffi-boundary-kit.md`](./design/ffi-boundary-kit.md) as the native-boundary anchor, [`design/wasm-component-kit.md`](./design/wasm-component-kit.md) as the component-boundary anchor, and Release/Support consumers as importing layers rather than silent truth owners.
- The explicit proposal-layer candidate is now [`proposals/epic-polyglot-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-polyglot-productization-stack.md): a thin `cargo poly-product` / `polyglot-product-pack/v0` layer above Host Package + FFI Boundary + Wasm Component + Release Truth + Support Envelope, with the ranked execution path in [`design/polyglot-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/polyglot-productization-pilot-program.md).
- The lower-level reusable proposal-layer candidate is now [`proposals/epic-host-package-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-host-package-kit.md): a thin `cargo hostpkg` / `hostpkg-pack/v0` layer for foreign package identity, imported boundary attachments, runtime/interpreter/ABI constraints, install receipts, and bounded support handoffs. That gives Polyglot, Extension, and Web reviews a portable package anchor without forcing stack-level adoption all at once.
- The next credible move remains a ranked pilot program: Python lane family first (full-API extension → `abi3` → free-threaded → `asyncio` bridge, per `design/python-host-lane-map.md`), Node addon lane second, UniFFI mobile-binding lane third, C/C++ handoff lane fourth, and Wasm component lane fifth.
- The bar is not another binding generator, package wrapper, or one universal IDL. The bar is portable polyglot-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, policy, and ecosystem guidance can import honestly.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Canonical Learning Stack (DocProof + Compile Guidance)
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain the preferred canonical reference, while some learning traffic appears to be shifting toward LLM tooling and agentic editors. That makes maintainer-authored learning artifacts more valuable, not less.
- Rust’s vision work is unusually explicit now: the project should expand Rust’s extensibility to cover **better diagnostics and guidance from crates** as well as deeper compilation-workflow integration. That means crate-authored compile guidance now belongs in the same strategic conversation as executable docs and teaching surfaces.
- docs.rs already exposes target and feature metadata for hosted docs, rustdoc already executes documentation examples, mdBook already tests Rust examples, and `trybuild` already captures compile-fail teaching flows. The ecosystem has enough point primitives; the missing contribution is the portable, canonical layer above them.
- This is strategically upstream-compatible: semantic-context, edit-workflow, atlas, release, and support consumers all get better when there are clearer canonical learning inputs to import.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/canonical-learning-maintainer-authored-teaching-and-derived-consumer-boundaries.md`](./gaps/canonical-learning-maintainer-authored-teaching-and-derived-consumer-boundaries.md), [`design/canonical-learning-stack.md`](./design/canonical-learning-stack.md), [`design/canonical-learning-pilot-program.md`](./design/canonical-learning-pilot-program.md), [`design/canonical-learning-consumer-pilot-program.md`](./design/canonical-learning-consumer-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-canonical-learning-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-canonical-learning-stack.md) as one execution band above [`design/docproof-kit.md`](./design/docproof-kit.md), [`design/docproof-pilot-program.md`](./design/docproof-pilot-program.md), [`design/compile-guidance-kit.md`](./design/compile-guidance-kit.md), and [`design/compile-guidance-pilot-program.md`](./design/compile-guidance-pilot-program.md).
- `cargo docproof` should own guide/example/doc-host support truth. `cargo guidance` should own crate-authored compile-time diagnostics, lint catalogs, compile-fail guidance, and hook-profile imports. `cargo learncanon` should own the stack-level learning subject, validation/evidence joins, and bounded consumer handoffs. Docs hosts, CI, editors, assistants, atlas views, and release/support consumers should import these artifacts through explicit consumer profiles rather than pretending to be the canonical source.
- The next credible move is now the ranked stack-level rollout in [`design/canonical-learning-pilot-program.md`](./design/canonical-learning-pilot-program.md): API docs + docs.rs first, guide-book lane second, compile-guidance lane third, shared consumer-import lane fourth, and atlas/support/release-review lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a docs portal,” “a nicer UI-test harness,” “AI-enhanced docs,” or “one giant learning schema.” The bar is “maintainer-authored canonical learning truth with explicit derived-consumer contracts.”


## Emerging seam just below the top band: Lending Surface Kit
**Why it now deserves promotion from “important later” to “active frontier edge”**
- Official Rust work is no longer treating lending-style APIs as a distant theoretical wish. The 2025H1 async flagship said the next generation of async libraries has been blocked on stable async traits and streams; the 2025H2 Polonius goal explicitly says future patterns such as lending iterators need the improved borrow checker; and the 2026 flagship themes still name lending iterators and reborrow progress as active work.
- The reborrow-traits goal is especially important because it says userland still cannot achieve **true reborrowing** today. That means there is room for an ecosystem contribution, but the right contribution is a companion/evidence substrate, not a pretend final language abstraction.
- RFC 2996, RTN, async closures, and pin-ergonomics work together imply that Rust is gaining more ways to *express* borrow-friendly async sequence and callback shapes, but the ecosystem still lacks a reviewable way to compare and migrate between them honestly.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/lending-surface-kit.md`](./design/lending-surface-kit.md), [`design/lending-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/lending-surface-pilot-program.md), [`gaps/lending-surfaces-borrowing-streams-and-sequence-interop.md`](./gaps/lending-surfaces-borrowing-streams-and-sequence-interop.md), and [`proposals/epic-lending-surface-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-lending-surface-kit.md) as one band.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: sync lending first → async-sequence bridge second → borrowing callback / async-closure lane third → pin/runtime-sensitive adapters fourth → domain sequence pilots fifth.
- The bar is not “finally pick the one true Stream replacement.” The bar is “reviewable borrowing-sequence semantics, adapter truth, and explicit language/runtime blockers that let the ecosystem advance without waiting for total convergence.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Package Admission Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s 2026 flagship theme **Secure your supply chain** is unusually concrete now: it explicitly names control over public API dependencies and SBOM generation as current milestones. That makes package-admission review an ecosystem-core problem instead of a niche enterprise afterthought.
- The archive now has an explicit **Publish Set Kit** beneath package admission, which means selected package/payload/check/receipt truth no longer needs to stay smeared across manifest, release, registry, and CI notes.
- Cargo 1.94 still lists public/private dependencies as unfinished and keeps the SBOM fragment work in planning, while Cargo’s current docs now expose workspace-aware publish selection, unstable machine-readable packaging lists, and explicit upload/index receipt behavior. That combination says the package-admission boundary is both active and still missing a durable orchestration layer.
- crates.io now exposes stronger package-side trust signals — Security-tab advisory views, Trusted Publishing controls, blocked risky triggers, and `pubtime` — but those are still useful imported facts rather than one reviewable publish bundle.
- The archive already had the components — Publish Set, Dependency Control, Public API, SBOM Evidence, Trust Signals, and Policy — but they were still too easy to read as parallel ideas rather than one stack for the moment a package is admitted to the registry.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/package-admission-publish-review-evidence-waivers-and-handoffs.md`](./gaps/package-admission-publish-review-evidence-waivers-and-handoffs.md), [`design/package-admission-stack.md`](./design/package-admission-stack.md), [`design/package-admission-pilot-program.md`](./design/package-admission-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-package-admission-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-package-admission-stack.md), [`design/publish-set-kit.md`](./design/publish-set-kit.md), [`design/dependency-control-stack.md`](./design/dependency-control-stack.md), [`design/public-api-kit.md`](./design/public-api-kit.md), [`design/sbom-evidence-kit.md`](./design/sbom-evidence-kit.md), [`design/trust-signals-kit.md`](./design/trust-signals-kit.md), and [`design/policy-kit.md`](./design/policy-kit.md) as one execution band.
- Publish Set should keep owning selected package/payload/check/authority/receipt truth. Dependency Control should keep owning chosen graph / activation / public-private posture. Public API should keep owning exposure drift / semver / witness / MSRV truth. SBOM Evidence should keep owning inventory capture and lossiness. Trust Signals should keep owning publisher / freshness / name-risk / audit inputs. Policy should keep owning verdicts, waivers, and `INCONCLUSIVE` posture.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo package-admission` / `package-admission-pack/v0` layer proven through a ranked package-side rollout — single-crate publish-review gate first, workspace publish-group lane second, proc-macro/build-lane scrutiny third, package-to-release handoff fourth, and thin registry/downstream consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “a secure publish wrapper”, “a registry risk score”, or “one umbrella release tool”. The bar is “portable package-admission truth that lets maintainers, CI, registries, downstream packagers, and policy tools see what package set was admitted, what public contract changed, what inventory/trust facts mattered, and what decision was actually made.”


## Emerging seam just below the top band: Inventory Evidence Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust's 2026 flagship theme **Secure your supply chain** now explicitly includes **SBOM generation**, and the goals page separately calls out **Stabilize Cargo SBOM precursor**. That means Cargo-native inventory continuity is an active upstream seam, not a sidecar.
- Cargo's unstable `sbom` support emits precursor files **next to compiled artifacts**, exposes `CARGO_SBOM_PATH`, and allows the same crate to appear multiple times when compiled differently. The tracking issue explicitly says the remaining work includes demonstrating end-to-end generation of an industry-standard SBOM from Cargo's precursor while keeping non-Rust dependencies out of scope for the precursor itself. That means the missing contribution is not “pick the winning exporter,” but the continuity layer above precursor and below downstream policy/incident consumers.
- crates.io now exposes stronger package/release-side ingredients around this seam — a Security tab, GitLab Trusted Publishing, Trusted Publishing-only mode, blocked risky triggers, and `pubtime` — while the malicious-crate policy now leans on RustSec advisories as the durable record. Those are not inventory by themselves, but they make inventory continuity and incident imports more realistic.
- `cargo package` and `cargo install` still prove that package publication and consumer installation can diverge materially through manifest normalization and lockfile behavior. `cargo-auditable` proves binary recovery is practical and now explicitly points users at Cargo's native SBOM precursor for more accurate recording.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/inventory-evidence-stack.md`](./design/inventory-evidence-stack.md), [`design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md`](./design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-inventory-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-inventory-evidence-stack.md), [`design/sbom-evidence-kit.md`](./design/sbom-evidence-kit.md), [`design/package-admission-stack.md`](./design/package-admission-stack.md), [`design/release-truth-stack.md`](./design/release-truth-stack.md), and [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md) as one execution band.
- SBOM Evidence should keep owning subject/capture/graph/artifact-link/projection/diff truth. Package Admission should keep owning package-side review and verdicts. Release Truth should keep owning built artifacts, signatures, provenance, and rebuild attachments. Distribution Contract should keep owning channel/fallback/verification/install-receipt truth.
- The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo inventory-evidence` / `inventory-evidence-pack/v0` layer proven through a ranked rollout — Cargo precursor capture first, standards projection second, binary-recovery / artifact-link cross-check third, package-to-release attachment fourth, and distribution/install receipt imports fifth.
- The bar is not “the one true SBOM exporter,” “a registry inventory page,” or “a universal supply-chain dashboard.” The bar is “portable inventory continuity that lets package review, release review, distro ingestion, install receipts, and incident/policy consumers all import the same evidence honestly.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Pointer Surface Kit
**Why it now deserves promotion from “important later” to “active frontier edge”**
- The Rust project is still explicitly investing in **Beyond the `&`**: the 2026 flagships keep field projections, reborrow traits, and in-place-initialization alignment on the active roadmap, which means custom pointer/reference ergonomics are not a side quest.
- The pin-ergonomics goal makes the near-term problem sharper: pinning is central to async and generators, but pinned borrowing syntax, pattern support, and pinned receiver ergonomics are still being worked out.
- RFC 3519 and RFC 3621 together make the missing contribution unusually clear: custom smart pointers still need explicit receiver and trait-object support, and some of the most important use cases are foreign or kernel-facing pointers that cannot honestly claim Rust-reference semantics.
- That means the right contribution is not “invent the best smart pointer” and not “wait for the language to finish”. It is a pilotable review boundary for pointer identity, aliasing, projection, receiver posture, and transitions while the language frontier is still opening.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/pointer-surface-kit.md`](./design/pointer-surface-kit.md), [`design/pointer-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/pointer-surface-pilot-program.md), [`gaps/reference-surfaces-smart-pointers-and-custom-borrow-contracts.md`](./gaps/reference-surfaces-smart-pointers-and-custom-borrow-contracts.md), and [`proposals/epic-pointer-surface-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-pointer-surface-kit.md) as one execution band.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: shared-ownership lane first → pinned-projection lane second → foreign-reference lane third → receiver/dyn lane fourth → transition/migration lane fifth. The shared-ownership lane should now read `design/pointer-shared-ownership-lane-map.md` as part of that first step so ordinary `Rc`, ordinary `Arc`, unique-to-shared setup, weakless or borrowed arc families, publication/storage wrappers, and language-watch ergonomic-ref-counting work stay distinct instead of collapsing into one fake ‘Arc ergonomics’ verdict.
- The bar is not “a universal smart-pointer trait,” “a nicer derive macro,” or “a new unsafe abstraction empire.” The bar is “reviewable pointer semantics that library authors, interop codegen, kernel-facing crates, docs/atlas authors, and future language migrations can all consume honestly.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Borrowing Frontier Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s current roadmap is no longer moving one isolated borrowing-adjacent feature at a time. The 2026 flagship themes explicitly keep **Beyond the `&`** and **Unblocking dormant traits** active, with milestones around field projections, reborrow traits, in-place initialization, next-solver stabilization, and the Sized hierarchy. That is strong evidence that pointer/reference semantics, trait evolution, lending patterns, and construction/destruction posture are now one frontier neighborhood rather than scattered curiosities.
- The 2025H2 goal slate makes the coupling even sharper: reborrow traits is about replacing userland facsimiles and removing `Pin` special-casing; pin ergonomics is about making pinned borrowing/receivers usable; in-place initialization explicitly says the ecosystem already has multiple competing crate-level approaches that the language now wants to learn from; and Polonius is motivated directly by future patterns such as lending iterators.
- RFC 3519, RFC 3621, and the async-traits / RTN direction show why a worthy external contribution here is not “invent the final abstraction in crates”. The real missing layer is a reviewable substrate that can publish **trait-family truth + pointer/reference truth + lending truth + initialization truth** while the language frontier still shifts.
- The workaround ecology is now strong enough that it should be treated as evidence, not noise: `reborrow`, `lending-iterator`, `dynosaur`, `pin-project`, `pinned-init`, `pin-init`, `moveit`, `ouroboros`, and `self_cell` each solve real slices of the frontier, but in different ways and at different semantic layers. That is exactly the pattern where the archive should prefer a portable comparison/migration boundary over one more winner-picking crate pitch.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/borrowing-frontier-stack.md`](./design/borrowing-frontier-stack.md), [`design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md`](./design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md), [`design/trait-surface-kit.md`](./design/trait-surface-kit.md), [`design/pointer-surface-kit.md`](./design/pointer-surface-kit.md), [`design/lending-surface-kit.md`](./design/lending-surface-kit.md), and [`design/initialization-surface-kit.md`](./design/initialization-surface-kit.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Trait Surface should keep owning dyn posture, return-shape guarantees, impl coverage, and split-family truth. Pointer Surface should keep owning identity / aliasing / projection / receiver truth. Lending Surface should keep owning borrowing-mode and sequence-adapter truth. Initialization Surface should keep owning placement, staged assembly, pinning, and teardown truth.
- The next credible move is now split into an explicit proposal layer plus the ranked shared pilot program: [`proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md) should own the stack-level contract, while [`design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md`](./design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md) should still run pointer/receiver lane first, lending/reborrow lane second, dyn-return plus initialization lane third, foreign/kernel lane fourth, and migration/consumer lane fifth.
- The bar is not “one universal smart-pointer trait”, “the one true Stream replacement”, “a trait-system manifesto”, or “a constructor empire”. The bar is “portable surface truth that lets Rust libraries and future language migrations stay legible while the frontier is still moving.”

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Extension Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s 2026 flagships explicitly include **Wasm Components**, and the current Rust/component-model guidance now says native `wasm32-wasip2` tooling is first-class while `cargo-component` remains useful for custom-WIT lanes. That means extension/runtime plurality is becoming more mainstream, not less.
- Serious Rust hosts now already expose materially different extension realities: Zed extensions are Git repositories with `extension.toml`, Rust→WebAssembly code, and explicit user-restrictable capabilities; Nushell plugins are separately installed executables speaking a versioned protocol that must track host updates; Tauri plugins are Cargo/NPM/mobile package families governed by permissions and capabilities with dangerous commands/scopes blocked by default until enabled; Extism treats the plug-in system itself as a host-defined interface with manifest/runtime constraints and explicit host functions.
- The missing contribution is not another plugin SDK, gallery wrapper, marketplace scraper, or Wasm-only abstraction. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **host/plugin surface truth, runtime-kind/capability truth, extension package/install/update truth, compatibility/migration truth, and shipped/support truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/extension-productization-stack.md`](./design/extension-productization-stack.md), [`design/extension-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/extension-productization-pilot-program.md), [`proposals/epic-extension-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-extension-productization-stack.md), [`design/plugin-surface-kit.md`](./design/plugin-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-capability-kit.md`](./design/runtime-capability-kit.md), [`design/wasm-component-kit.md`](./design/wasm-component-kit.md), [`design/host-package-kit.md`](./design/host-package-kit.md), [`design/distribution-contract-stack.md`](./design/distribution-contract-stack.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Plugin Surface should keep owning host extension points, lifecycle, manifests, and compatibility posture. Runtime Capability should keep owning permission/scope/authority truth. Wasm Component and Host Package should keep owning runtime/package-specific attachments. Consumer Install should keep owning install/override receipts, while Update Continuity should keep owning update/downgrade continuity. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning public promise and checked-doc truth.
- The next credible move is now split into an explicit proposal layer plus the ranked pilot: [`proposals/epic-extension-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-extension-productization-stack.md) should own the stack-level contract, while [`design/extension-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/extension-productization-pilot-program.md) should still run the single-host Wasm lane first, capability-gated lane second, versioned protocol-executable lane third, gallery/install/update lane fourth, and mixed-runtime host lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a better plugin SDK”, “one extension marketplace to rule them all”, or “just use Wasm everywhere”. The bar is portable extension-productization evidence that keeps host, capability, runtime, install, and support truths separate while making them composable.


## Emerging seam just below the top band: Event Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The archive already has a strong Event Surface Kit, but current ecosystem signals make the next synthesis obvious: projects increasingly need an **event-product boundary** above channels, envelopes, delivery profiles, replay runbooks, schema lore, runtime settings, and dashboard folklore.
- AsyncAPI 3.1.0 explicitly frames the AsyncAPI document as a **communication contract between senders and receivers**, while CloudEvents explicitly exists to give event data a common format across services, platforms, and systems. That means the standards-side lesson is already written down: event products need portable contract layers, not just one more broker client.
- The Rust-side ingredient set is now clearly heterogeneous: `async-nats` spans pub/sub plus JetStream, key-value, object-store, and service lanes while deprecating the old sync client; `rdkafka` spans async producers/consumers plus EOS via idempotent and transactional producers; `lapin` carries runtime and TLS-backend diversity; `zenoh` mixes pub/sub, query/reply, storage, and computation while explicitly saying its configuration surface is unstable; and the Rust CloudEvents SDK is real but still explicitly unstable. That is exactly the pattern the archive should read as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The missing contribution is therefore not another broker wrapper, schema registry, replay daemon, or queue abstraction. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **channel/event/delivery truth, schema/evolution truth, runtime/topology activation, replay/observability evidence, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/event-productization-stack.md`](./design/event-productization-stack.md), [`design/event-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/event-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/event-surface-kit.md`](./design/event-surface-kit.md), [`design/schema-contract-kit.md`](./design/schema-contract-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/observability-kit.md`](./design/observability-kit.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- Event Surface should keep owning channel/event/envelope/delivery/replay truth. Schema Contract should keep owning payload evolution and registry-subject truth. Runtime Settings should keep owning broker/topology/durable/credential/TLS activation. Observability should keep owning replay/DLQ/lag/correlation evidence. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning support/docs truth. Service / Protocol / Local-First / Media / Agent / Data should remain importing consumers.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: single-broker lane first, replay/DLQ/backfill lane second, schema/registry lane third, activation/observability lane fourth, and service/support/release/incident consumers fifth.
- The bar is not another broker client, schema registry, event DSL, or hosted ops platform. The bar is portable event-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, and incident consumers can import honestly.

## Important non-demotion: language-shaping kits
The following ideas remain important even when they are not the **best next external contribution target**:
- Atomic Surface Kit
- Encoding Surface Kit
- Validity Surface Kit

These still matter because they point toward ideal Rust. They simply have a different contribution posture right now: many of them depend more on language/compiler evolution than on a near-term ecosystem-side artifact push. The archive should keep refining them, but should not confuse **deep importance** with **best current leverage for an epic external build**.

## Archive policy from this frontier refresh
1. Prefer revisions that strengthen the top frontier unless a lower-ranked seam gains major new upstream traction.
2. When a proposal overlaps active project-goal work, classify it explicitly as one of:
   - upstream candidate,
   - companion kit,
   - evidence substrate,
   - watch/wait.
3. Prefer clusters over isolated gadgets: Public API, Build-State, Policy/Trust/SBOM/Lifecycle, Semantic Context/Edit Workflow, Navigation/Commons, and Maintenance Reality are stronger as bands than as disconnected commands.
4. Keep “ideal Rust” work alive, but do not let the archive drift into ranking abstractions above seams that are more actionable today.

## Emerging seam just below the top band: Web Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The 2024 State of Rust survey says WebAssembly use is mostly in the **browser** (23%) rather than other WebAssembly contexts (7%). That means browser-facing Wasm is already a mainstream Rust product lane, not a side curiosity.
- The ingredient layer is now real but heterogeneous: `wasm-bindgen` plus `web-sys` define the browser API/JS interop lane; `wasm-pack` makes npm/browser package outputs first-class; Trunk is a real HTML-entry bundler for Wasm, JS snippets, and assets; Leptos openly splits CSR-with-Trunk from SSR/hydration-with-`cargo-leptos`; Dioxus treats bundle splitting, CDN assets, SSG, and Wasm-based deployment providers as part of the app surface; and Yew’s SSR docs are explicit that browser APIs are unavailable on the server side. That is exactly the pattern the archive should treat as “needs a productization layer above the ingredients.”
- The missing contribution is not another frontend framework, another bundler, or a framework comparison chart. It is the portable contract/evidence layer for **render-mode truth, browser capability/interop truth, bundle/base-path/deploy truth, browser-vs-server split truth, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/web-productization-stack.md`](./design/web-productization-stack.md), [`design/web-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/web-productization-pilot-program.md), [`gaps/web-apps-browser-wasm-and-fullstack-productization-contracts.md`](./gaps/web-apps-browser-wasm-and-fullstack-productization-contracts.md), and [`proposals/epic-web-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-web-productization-stack.md) as one execution band.
- Client App Surface should keep owning page/route/render-mode truth. Service Surface should keep owning SSR/server-function/API attachment truth. Runtime Settings should keep owning base-path/origin/env activation truth. Host Package should keep owning JS/Wasm package identity truth. Distribution Contract and Support Envelope should keep owning shipped/deployed/support claims.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: static CSR/base-path lane first, browser capability / JS interop lane second, SSR/hydration split lane third, full-stack deploy lane fourth, and support/docs consumer lane fifth.
- The bar is not “a better Rust React”, “one bundler to rule them all”, or “another WebAssembly tutorial generator”. The bar is portable web-productization evidence that keeps render, browser runtime, deploy/package, split-boundary, and support truths separate while making them composable.


## Emerging seam just below the top band: Protocol Productization Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- Rust’s protocol landscape is already serious but heterogeneous: `tonic` is a production-oriented gRPC-over-HTTP/2 building block, `tonic-web` directly translates grpc-web without an external proxy, Quinn is a pure-Rust QUIC substrate, `h3` is generic over QUIC transports while still explicit about its experimental posture, `rustls` exposes protocol-feature and ALPN/SNI configuration surfaces, and transport-agnostic RPC crates like `tarpc` keep reminding us that one service contract can span multiple transport stories.
- Browser and modern transport bridges are now active enough that they cannot stay implicit. WebTransport crates, `tonic-h3`, and `h3-axum` all point to a world where browser/native, HTTP/2/HTTP/3, and translated/proxied lanes are part of product reality rather than optional demos.
- The missing contribution is therefore not another transport crate, proxy helper, TLS wrapper, or RPC framework. It is the productization layer for **wire truth, bridge/security truth, service/schema attachments, conformance evidence, and support/docs truth**.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/protocol-productization-stack.md`](./design/protocol-productization-stack.md), [`design/protocol-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/protocol-productization-pilot-program.md), [`design/protocol-surface-kit.md`](./design/protocol-surface-kit.md), [`design/service-surface-kit.md`](./design/service-surface-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/schema-contract-kit.md`](./design/schema-contract-kit.md), [`design/conformance-traceability-stack.md`](./design/conformance-traceability-stack.md), and [`design/support-envelope-kit.md`](./design/support-envelope-kit.md) as one coupled execution band.
- Protocol Surface should keep owning wire/transport/interaction/security/bridge truth. Service Surface should keep owning application-facing method/route plus reflection/health attachment truth. Runtime Settings should keep owning cert/root/ALPN/origin/proxy activation. Schema Contract should keep owning payload/interface evolution. Conformance Traceability should keep owning spec/vector/interop evidence. Support Envelope and DocProof should keep owning public promise and checked-doc truth.
- The next credible move is a ranked rollout: gRPC-over-HTTP/2 + grpc-web lane first, QUIC/HTTP3/WebTransport lane second, transport-agnostic RPC lane third, conformance/cross-language lane fourth, and support/docs/release consumers fifth.
- The bar is not another transport abstraction, proxy layer, or framework shootout. The bar is portable protocol-productization evidence that humans, CI, release review, support, and atlas/documentation consumers can import honestly.


## Emerging seam just below the maintenance band: Keystone Stewardship Stack
**Why this now deserves first-class treatment in the archive**
- The Rust Foundation’s 2026–2028 strategy makes **Stable Infrastructure** and **Sustainable Maintenance** core pillars, which is strong evidence that some Rust projects now need continuity and support treatment above ordinary crate metadata or repo activity.
- The Maintainers Fund is explicitly designed for **consistent, transparent, long-term support** aligned with high-impact priorities and maintainer continuity, while the Rust Innovation Lab exists because some Rust projects have matured into **critical pieces of global software infrastructure** needing neutral governance and reliable institutional backing.
- Rustls gives the archive a concrete public model: the Foundation describes the kinds of projects it wants to support as ones that provide **foundational infrastructure**, have **clear industry traction and operational need**, and are driven by **deeply committed maintainers who need space to build**. That is almost a direct specification for a keystone-stewardship review layer.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`gaps/keystone-projects-critical-infrastructure-and-institutional-stewardship-contracts.md`](./gaps/keystone-projects-critical-infrastructure-and-institutional-stewardship-contracts.md), [`design/keystone-stewardship-stack.md`](./design/keystone-stewardship-stack.md), [`design/keystone-stewardship-pilot-program.md`](./design/keystone-stewardship-pilot-program.md), and [`proposals/epic-keystone-stewardship-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-keystone-stewardship-stack.md) as one execution band.
- Maintenance Reality should keep owning declared lifecycle and observed stewardship work. Trust Decision should keep owning release-authority and advisory/provenance inputs. Library Productization / Release Truth / Support Envelope should keep owning the user-facing product and release surface. Keystone Stewardship should keep owning **criticality, institutional support, continuity risk, obligation posture, and transition reports**.
- The next credible move is a ranked pilot program: security keystone subject first, shared-building-block subject second, ecosystem-service / infra subject third, continuity-transition lane fourth, and Atlas/adoption/support-funding consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “top 100 crates”, “foundation blessed”, or “criticality by download count”. The bar is portable keystone-stewardship evidence that keeps criticality, support, continuity, obligations, and consumer conclusions separate while making them composable.

### 23) Workspace Environment Stack (Tooling Contract + Toolchain Productization + Native Dependency + Runtime Settings + Credentials)
**Why this now deserves frontier treatment**
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online documentation remains the preferred canonical reference while people increasingly learn with LLM tooling in the loop, and it separately notes that editors with agentic support are on the rise. That means environment/setup truth now needs bounded machine-readable handoff rather than only prose.
- Cargo config is hierarchical across parent directories and rustup overrides are discovered by directory walk, while Cargo 1.94 is actively discussing workspace/configuration discovery because stray parent manifests or `.cargo/config.toml` files can poison unrelated builds. That makes workspace environment identity a real Rust tooling seam instead of generic onboarding fluff.
- rust-analyzer already exposes override commands, extra env, feature/target posture, and linked-workspace behavior, which is a concrete sign that editor reality and build reality still need a shared contract.
- Dev Containers and Nix prove that reproducible realization substrates exist, but they are intentionally generic. The missing Rust contribution is the thin review layer above them.

**What the contribution should look like in practice**
- Treat [`design/workspace-environment-stack.md`](./design/workspace-environment-stack.md), [`design/tooling-contract-stack.md`](./design/tooling-contract-stack.md), [`design/toolchain-productization-stack.md`](./design/toolchain-productization-stack.md), [`design/native-dependency-kit.md`](./design/native-dependency-kit.md), [`design/runtime-settings-kit.md`](./design/runtime-settings-kit.md), [`design/credentials-kit.md`](./design/credentials-kit.md), and [`proposals/epic-workspace-environment-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-workspace-environment-stack.md) as one coupled frontier band.
- `cargo workenv` should own workspace subject/discovery truth, declared environment intent, realization reports, secret-handle posture, observed-state reports, and bounded handoff artifacts. Tooling Contract should keep owning discovery/scope/build facts. Toolchain Productization, Native Dependency, Runtime Settings, and Credentials should remain distinct imported truth families.
- The next credible move is a ranked rollout: workspace subject + intent lane first, host-shell observation lane second, Dev Container / Nix realization lanes third, editor / CI / agent handoff lanes fourth, and support / onboarding / archaeology consumers fifth.
- The bar is not “another environment manager”, “a Rust-only secret vault”, or “force every workspace onto one substrate”. The bar is portable workspace-environment evidence that contributors, maintainers, editors, CI systems, and assistants can all consume honestly.
